If the probe teaches, primes, fatigues, changes exposure, or cannot be run safely, narrow the conclusion or return an unresolved differential that states the observation limit and its effect on the conclusion.

For any new observation, record the evidence collector, holder participation, declared use, protected conditions, access and privacy boundary, and whether another owner must interpret the result. Treat an assisting System's inference as decision input. Acting on it requires separately established authority for the particular action.

#### HCD.3:4.4 - Record the Result

A qualified intervention target contains:

| Result position | Required content |
| --- | --- |
| use boundary | Human holder, later-Work demand, configuration, horizon, evidence window, receiving decision, participation basis, and governance limits. |
| observed gap | Claim-sized Work observation, intended result or critical-error boundary, and why the gap matters. |
| target | The person's difficulty with the required contribution and the smallest supported human change to target under the named conditions. A later decision selects any practice or other intervention. |
| evidence | Observations, work products, contrasts, inference rule, uncertainty, and contamination or measurement limits. |
| rival disposition | The explanations considered, any still-plausible alternatives, and their effects on the target and next observation. When none remain, keep the evidence limits and reopen condition explicit. |
| protected conditions | Method, task, tools, prompts, information, time, environment, assignment, authority, supports, and specialist routes held or bounded. |
| intended observation | The target change to observe, its conditions, and the claim that would depend on it. Explaining a result as an effect of the intervention through that target requires evidence for each link. |
| boundaries and continuation | Direct specialist or owner boundary, unsupported uses, and the condition that reopens the target. |

Observed performance may be reported with its conditions before a causal explanation is established. A better score alone establishes neither a change in the proposed target nor the intervention's effect through that target.

A non-training return contains:

| Result position | Required content |
| --- | --- |
| actual owner or missing result | The direct Method, tool, assignment, organization, authority, specialist, evidence, or other owner and the exact result or value needed. |
| blocked HCD decision | The diagnosis or later development decision that cannot be made. |
| evidence and rival disposition | What is known, what remains unresolved, and why a human target is not supported. |
| safe interim boundary | What must not be assigned, inferred, prescribed, or used meanwhile. |
| retry condition | The exact new result, protected observation, owner decision, or compatible evidence that permits reconsideration. |

#### HCD.3:4.5 - What Changes in Practice

A practitioner no longer asks, “Which training should fix this failure?” They ask, “Which live explanation would change the next Work, what observation distinguishes it safely, and what is the smallest human target the evidence actually supports?” A missing Method, prompt, authority, specialist result, or diagnostic observation is returned under its own name.

### HCD.3:5 - Archetypal Grounding

#### HCD.3:5.1 - Constructed Positive Case: ImpactReview-17

`ImpactReview-17` is a constructed pedagogical case. `ImpactReview-17-Domain-Oracle-v1` supplies engineering correctness and criticality criteria for `PumpWorks-Config-Baseline-42` and `AI-Assist-M4`. The baseline observations and neutral-probe results below are stipulated case data.

For human holder Engineer-P14, the constructed HCD.1 account demands independent review of AI-proposed change-impact claims under a forty-five-minute limit with current sources, configuration trace, auditable evidence, and bounded safety and maintenance specialist returns. The baseline stipulates correct source/configuration trace and evidence return in eight of eight cases, correct specialist routing in two of two relevant cases, and rejection of two of four plausible wrong AI claims. Both misses occur in unfamiliar variants and are labelled safety-critical only by the constructed oracle.

Several rivals would change the next Work. The applicable MethodDescription, source access, tool configuration, time, assignment, authority, and specialist route are present in the case, so those branches do not explain the two misses. An `E.23.CAE`-shaped neutral probe preserves those conditions. Engineer-P14 recognizes that independent checking is required but does not generate and test a competing configuration hypothesis on either unfamiliar miss.

HCD.3 therefore returns this **qualified intervention target**:

> Generate and test an independent configuration counter-hypothesis before accepting plausible AI output in unfamiliar variants.

The evidence is narrow: two unfamiliar misses inside eight constructed cases. A prompt-design rival remains live because the neutral prompt may have suppressed the counter-hypothesis move. The protected conditions are the same source, configuration, AI assistant, time limit, authority, and specialist routes. The intended observation is whether Engineer-P14 produces and tests a competing configuration hypothesis and rejects the planted wrong claim in each of two further unfamiliar cases: one first attempt with a neutral prompt, and one first attempt on the other case with an explicit counter-hypothesis prompt. Different cases leave case-difference uncertainty; the comparison informs the next decision without isolating a causal prompt effect. Reopen when the prompt, configuration, case family, source access, tool posture, time, observation count, or target-domain oracle changes.

The result is a bounded target for a later development decision, not an assignment to practice. Operational use needs qualified current domain criteria and observations for the actual person and Work, including evidence that distinguishes the live rivals.

#### HCD.3:5.2 - Missing Case: ReleaseWork-R9

Engineer-E27 has only a prospective HCD.1 demand hypothesis for `ReleaseWork-R9`. The actual Work occurrence, occurrence-produced result claim, occurrence-bound result record, representative observations, and compatible obtaining `SYSE.21` supplier result required by `XRI-21` are not recoverable.

HCD.3 returns a **non-training return** with `diagnostic-evidence-missing`. The blocked decision is whether any human capability, knowledge, skill, discrimination, misconception, behaviour, self-regulation, or commitment is the limiting change target. A MethodDescription, plan, target repertoire, curriculum, constructed demonstration, course completion, or platform score cannot discriminate the rival branches.

The safe interim boundary is: do not base training assignment, an inference of human deficiency, employment consequences, or a transformation claim on the unsupported HCD inference. Separate decisions require their own sufficient evidence and appropriate authority. Retry after a compatible HCD.1 demand basis and holder-specific evidence distinguish the live demand/envelope, Method, tool/support, assignment/authority, specialist, and human-target branches under the intended configuration and evidence window. Recovering a Work occurrence alone does not complete the diagnosis; the observations must be decision-discriminating.

#### HCD.3:5.3 - Unlike Case: Social-Anxiety Clinical Route

When an adult seeks treatment for social anxiety, return that question to qualified clinical care. A diagnostic label, symptoms, avoidance, or treatment request alone does not establish a target for changing a separately specified contribution in later Work. The current NICE social-anxiety guideline ([`BEH-05`](https://www.nice.org.uk/guidance/cg159/chapter/recommendations)) places recognition, assessment, treatment choice, provider competence, safety, shared decision-making, and outcome monitoring with qualified clinical care.

HCD.3 returns a **non-training clinical route** whenever diagnosis, treatment choice, exposure or CBT content, clinical safety, consent, provider competence, or clinical outcome interpretation is current. It may preserve a legitimate later-participation Work question and the distinction between observed behaviour, support, transfer, and outcome, but it does not prescribe exposure, behavioural experiments, homework, counterconditioning, symptom targets, or provider arrangements. Retry HCD only when the person and clinical owner supply a bounded non-clinical Work question whose evidence can be used safely without taking clinical authority.

### HCD.3:6 - Bias-Annotation

| Recurring bias | Likely drift | Repair |
| --- | --- | --- |
| training-default bias | A poor result becomes a course or practice assignment. | Compare decision-changing rivals and return the smallest supported target or a non-training result. |
| dispositional bias | A configuration-dependent observation becomes a stable trait, attitude, or motivation label. | Bind holder, Work, conditions, horizon, observations, and any still-plausible rivals. |
| tool-blame or human-blame bias | Responsibility is assigned to the AI tool or person before the joint arrangement is separated. | Vary protected conditions and keep human, tool, Method, prompt, authority, and specialist contributions distinct. |
| familiar-mechanism bias | “Forgot,” “misconception,” or “not motivated” substitutes for a controlled differential. | Use `E.23.CAE` where applicable and retain any still-plausible explanation that could change the next decision. |
| target-rhetoric bias | Naming autonomy, confidence, motivation, or practice is treated as evidence that it is limiting. | Require holder-specific evidence for the proposed target. A later claim that an intervention worked through that target also needs target-engagement evidence. |
| clinical appropriation bias | A disorder or treatment process is recast as development. | Return diagnosis, treatment, provider, safety, consent, and clinical outcomes to qualified clinical ownership. |
| surveillance bias | More data collection is assumed to be harmless and authorized. | Name declared use, participation, access, privacy or worker-governance conditions, and direct authority before collection. |
| intervention leakage | The target wording quietly names a programme, provider, schedule, or method. | Name the person's difficulty with the required contribution, the smallest supported human change to target, and the intended observation; select any intervention separately. |

### HCD.3:7 - Conformance Checklist

- [ ] The exact human holder and a compatible HCD.1 account or equivalent qualified later-Work input are recoverable.
- [ ] The observed gap, configuration, horizon, evidence window, receiving decision, and protected conditions are explicit.
- [ ] The rival set contains every credible branch that would change the next Work and does not expand into an unbounded cause catalogue.
- [ ] An applicable `E.23.CAE` disposition is used before capability loss or more practice is declared when its differential can change the decision.
- [ ] Method, tool, AI, cue, information, access, environment, workload, recovery, assignment, authority, organization, provider, specialist, and human-target branches keep their own kinds.
- [ ] Any qualified target names the person's difficulty with the required contribution and the smallest supported human change under the named conditions.
- [ ] Evidence, uncertainty, the disposition of considered rivals, any still-plausible alternatives, protected conditions, intended observation, specialist boundary, and reopen condition are present.
- [ ] A non-training return names the actual owner or missing result, blocked HCD decision, safe interim boundary, and retry condition.
- [ ] Evidence collection names collector, declared use, holder participation, access, privacy or worker-governance boundary, and required direct owners.
- [ ] The result authorizes no surveillance, assignment, employment action, specialist action, treatment, or development intervention.
- [ ] The result claims neither target engagement nor transformation before later evidence supports it.
- [ ] The receiver can identify the next observation, safe interim boundary, and owner of any returned question.

### HCD.3:8 - Common Anti-Patterns and How to Avoid Them

| Anti-pattern | Why it fails | Repair |
| --- | --- | --- |
| “The score is low, so train the skill” | The score does not identify the required contribution, where the person has difficulty, or the rival explanations. | Recover the later-Work account and compare decision-changing branches. |
| “They know it when prompted, so the problem is solved” | Reference recovery does not establish unaided applicability, adaptation, enactment, or transfer. | Record the qualified differential and probe the earliest unresolved position. |
| “They missed twice, so capability was lost” | Small, configuration-bound evidence cannot eliminate support, prompt, envelope, or measurement rivals. | Preserve uncertainty and any still-plausible rival; narrow or return the target. |
| “Motivation is the cause” | Motivation rhetoric is not target engagement evidence. | Operationalize the proposed target and require an observation that it changed before linking it to a result. |
| “The AI made the error” or “the human made the error” | The joint Method, prompt, source, review, and authority arrangement is hidden. | Separate contributions and use a crossed or otherwise discriminating condition where safe. |
| “A clinical technique is just another learning intervention” | HCD lacks diagnosis, treatment, provider, consent, safety, and clinical outcome authority. | Return the case to qualified clinical ownership and retain only a legitimate non-clinical Work question. |
| “The target is practice” | Practice is an activity intended to produce a change; it does not identify the person's difficulty. | Name the difficulty making the required contribution, what must change, and the observation that would show that change. |
| “Diagnosis chooses the programme” | A target is evidence for a later decision, not a programme or authorization. | Keep programme comparison (`HCD.2`) separate; stop at the target or non-training return. |

### HCD.3:9 - Consequences

A development decision can focus on one evidence-supported target instead of a generic deficit, or stop early when the real constraint belongs to another owner. Rival explanations and governance remain visible, reducing unnecessary practice, unsafe probing, and employment overreach.

The cost is disciplined observation. Some cases remain unresolved, and a narrow target may reopen when prompt, tool, task, support, evidence, or specialist information changes. Using a qualified target to design development still requires a separate intervention choice. A claim that the intervention worked through this target requires later target-engagement and result evidence.

### HCD.3:10 - Rationale

The smallest decision-changing target is more useful than a complete causal story. It is sufficient to change the next development question while avoiding claims about hidden mechanisms that the evidence cannot support. Recording the considered explanations, any still-plausible alternatives, evidence limits, and reopen conditions prevents “qualified” from becoming “certain.”

The non-training return is a positive result because it prevents misdirected Work and tells the receiving owner exactly what is needed. The specialist boundary is not a residual exception: clinical, safety, legal, target-domain, assignment, and employment questions retain their own authority even when human development is also relevant.

### HCD.3:11 - SoTA-Echoing

The working question is: **How can a practitioner identify a development-relevant human target without treating every poor result as a training need or a medical diagnosis?**

The selected current line joins three bounded contributions. First, Melanson and Fahmie’s [Functional analysis of problem behavior: a 40-year review](https://doi.org/10.1002/jaba.983) (2023; `BEH-01`) supports the warning to distinguish maintaining conditions before intervening, but its clinical populations, controlled procedure, causal functions, and specialist safety arrangements are not generalized into HCD. Second, `E.23.CAE` separates envelope, configuration, applicability, access, adaptation, enactment, and actual capability change through an observation-first differential. Third, Sheeran and colleagues’ meta-analyses [Self-determination theory interventions for health behavior change](https://doi.org/10.1037/ccp0000501) (2020; `MOT-01`) and [Does increasing autonomous motivation or perceived competence lead to health behavior change?](https://doi.org/10.1037/hea0001111) (2021; `MOT-02`) support an intervention-to-target-to-result evidence chain when explaining an intervention's result through its proposed motivational target. A theory label or assigned technique is not evidence that the target changed.

A serious alternative is the generic training-needs interview or competency-gap score. It is fast and legible, but it commonly treats the selected measure as the cause, omits support and authority rivals, and moves directly from gap to programme. HCD.3 keeps those measures as evidence cues, then mutates the workflow into a bounded rival comparison and one target-or-return result.

This line does not supply a universal causal diagnosis, clinical protocol, intervention, or transfer claim. Human mechanism and intervention claims still require a direct source matched to the population, target, comparison, measure, horizon, support, and limit. Reopen when the holder, demand, configuration, prompt, tool, support, Method, assignment, authority, specialist result, evidence window, observation, declared use, or governance condition changes.

### HCD.3:12 - Relations

- **`HCD.1`:** supplies a same-holder demand account and evidence needs. HCD.3 may instead use an equivalent qualified later-Work input. Qualified inputs permit direct entry without a prescribed pattern sequence.
- **`E.23.CAE`:** supplies differential dispositions when apparent loss, access, expression, adaptation, or enactment would change the next Work. Its result is evidence, not an intervention choice.
- **`E.23.CDI`:** governs development-intervention and transfer evidence only after development is separately selected; HCD.3 does not perform that selection.
- **[Melanson and Fahmie’s review (2023)](https://doi.org/10.1002/jaba.983) (`BEH-01`):** supplies only the bounded “differentiate before intervening” warning; its clinical procedure and population remain outside HCD.
- **Meta-analyses by Sheeran and colleagues ([2020](https://doi.org/10.1037/ccp0000501), [2021](https://doi.org/10.1037/hea0001111); `MOT-01`, `MOT-02`):** supply target-engagement and intervention-to-target-to-result evidence boundaries; they do not make motivation the default cause.
- **The [NICE social-anxiety guideline](https://www.nice.org.uk/guidance/cg159/chapter/recommendations) (`BEH-05`) and direct clinical sources:** provide criteria for diagnosis, treatment, provider competence, safety, consent, and outcome interpretation. Clinical specialists exercise professional judgement; the person and other authorized actors make the decisions that belong to them. HCD.3 supplies the stop-and-route boundary.
- **Target-domain Methods and specialist results:** provide criteria and evidence for correctness, quality, criticality, hazard, legality, and other domain questions in the diagnostic comparison. Qualified specialists make the professional judgements, and authorized actors decide the corresponding actions.
- **`HCD.4`:** may receive a qualified target and any still-plausible rival as claim-level evidence for profile comparison; it does not inherit an intervention or mastery claim.
- **Programme comparison (`HCD.2`):** may use a qualified target under its own Method and decision boundary. HCD.3 selects no programme and requires no programme-comparison result.
- **Employment, assignment, safety, legal, provider, organization, platform, and tool owners:** retain their direct decisions and authority.

### HCD.3:End

<a id="hcd-4"></a>
## HCD.4 — Architect a Balanced Human Capability Profile Across Simultaneous Work

> **Type:** Human Capability Development practitioner profile-and-choice pattern


### HCD.4:0 - Use This When

Use this pattern when one named person's later Work requires several contributions at once and a manager, practitioner, or the person needs to know what to preserve, investigate, defer, or make a development priority. Begin with a compatible HCD.1 demand account or equivalent qualified scope and compare the claims one by one; do not begin with a course sequence, mastery ladder, role level, platform score, or one total.

With compatible scope and claim-level evidence, the first useful result is a **condition-qualified human capability profile**: priority characteristics, explicit trade-offs, limiting gaps, and a recommendation or unresolved question for one named decision owner. If a bounded choice is also requested, apply the `C.11` requirements in §4.4. Missing choice inputs block that choice; keep any comparison and recommendation that the scope and evidence support. If required scope or profile evidence is missing, return `profile-decision-blocked` for the affected conclusion with the missing value and retry condition.

The practical gain is a profile that preserves strong contributions while exposing one consequential gap and its uncertainty. Use `HCD.1` when the demand frame is missing. Use `HCD.3` when the limiting cause or target is unresolved. A separate programme comparison (`HCD.2`) may use the profile; HCD.4 neither requires nor invents a programme.

Do not use this pattern to claim current mastery from an aggregate, prescribe curriculum or provider, assert a learning trajectory or transformation, perform ongoing-Work steering, decide employment, safety, legal, clinical, or release matters, or treat an assisting System as the chooser by label alone.

### HCD.4:0.1 - Working Terms

| Name in this pattern | What it denotes |
| --- | --- |
| capability claim | One condition-qualified claim about a human contribution or interface needed in representative later Work. It is not a course outcome, trait label, or scalar coordinate by itself. |
| current profile | The set of supported current claim dispositions for one human holder, configuration, conditions, evidence window, and date. |
| target profile | The set of intended claim dispositions justified by representative later Work. It is not proof that the holder obtained them. |
| intermediate state | An optional description of a contribution and result boundary between the current and target profiles, under named conditions. Distinguish an intended intermediate target from an observed state. |
| profile difference | The claim-by-claim current-versus-target comparison, including preserved strengths, gaps, uncertainty, support dependence, critical errors, and trade-offs. |
| bounded profile decision | A lawful `C.11` choice over an already-available option set, supported by the profile comparison and kept separate from authorization, commitment, and later Work. |
| profile-decision-blocked | A substantive return naming the exact absent scope, evidence, chooser, option, comparison, probe, participation, or authority value and its retry condition. |
| simultaneous Work | Representative Work whose contributions or interfaces coexist, overlap, recur, or constrain one another; printed order does not make them a sequence or hierarchy. |

Current and target profiles describe supported and intended contributions. A profile difference compares those descriptions, and an optional aggregate summarizes selected claims. A curriculum plans instruction. Keep these descriptions, comparisons, and plans separate from the person's realized learning trajectory, the Work performed in learning, and any human transformation. Claims about what happened or changed need evidence of those occurrences.

### HCD.4:1 - Problem Frame

A person can be strong in three contributions and weak in a fourth. One contribution may be safe only with a specialist return; another may be independent of a tool; a third may fail only in unfamiliar variants. A single level or average can hide the critical asymmetry that should drive the next decision.

Visible source structures are seductive. A course order can look like a capability hierarchy, a Method decomposition can look like a development sequence, an organization chart can look like a profile, and a platform prediction can look like mastery. In simultaneous Work these structures need not line up one-for-one.

A profile must therefore preserve claim identity, conditions, evidence, support dependence, uncertainty, critical-error boundaries, and trade-offs. It may inform a choice, but choice starts only after the chooser, options, comparison basis, and probe value are real.

### HCD.4:2 - Problem

The common shortcut is to compress unlike claims into one score and then optimize the score. The resulting decision can trade away an already-reliable safety boundary, conceal unsupported transfer, or treat assisted performance as independent human capability.

The opposite shortcut records many dimensions but never closes a practical question. The practitioner needs a readable comparison that says what to preserve, what gap matters, what uncertainty is decision-changing, and whether to choose, probe, reroute, or stop—without fabricating a programme or authority.

### HCD.4:3 - Forces

- A scalar is easy to compare, while claim identity, asymmetry, critical-error status, support dependence, and uncertainty may be decision-bearing.
- A staged course or Method hierarchy is easy to draw, while the way human contributions constrain one another in later Work need not follow that order or hierarchy.
- Target states guide development, while current evidence and actual transformation must not be inferred from the target.
- Assisted scores can predict performance, while platform or AI output is model- and condition-dependent evidence rather than mastery.
- Self-monitoring can improve, while self-report and confidence do not establish later-Work capability.
- A recommendation helps an owner act, while the profile does not create authority, options, budget, or commitment.
- Another probe can reduce uncertainty, while probing consumes time and may itself change the holder or Work.
- Preserving every strength is attractive, while real trade-offs may move time, workload, autonomy, support, or risk.
- Human participation matters, while manager, safety, release, clinical, and employment owners retain different decisions.

### HCD.4:4 - Solution

Build the profile as a set of evidence-bound claim rows across simultaneous representative Work. Compare current and target dispositions without imposing a source sequence, expose trade-offs, and close only the decision that the current evidence and authority support.

#### HCD.4:4.1 - Bind the Profile Use

Name:

1. the exact human holder and holder participation basis;
2. the HCD.1 demand account or equivalent qualified scope, including later-Work family, configuration, conditions, effective horizon, evidence window, and receiving decision;
3. the named profile decision owner and every direct owner whose authority or result remains external;
4. the representative cases and simultaneous contribution interfaces that can change the comparison; and
5. the protected consequences, critical errors, privacy or worker-governance conditions, and stop boundaries.

If the same-holder scope cannot be recovered, return `profile-decision-blocked` for the missing demand frame. Do not substitute a role profile, population average, course map, or imagined future person.

#### HCD.4:4.2 - Build Claim Rows

For each selected capability claim, record:

| Claim field | Required content |
| --- | --- |
| contribution and interface | What the human contribution supports in representative later Work and which simultaneous contributions, tools, or specialist returns it interfaces with. |
| current disposition | Current evidence, conditions, support dependence, date, uncertainty, and any critical-error observation; `unsupported` is preferable to an invented level. |
| target disposition | The contribution and result boundary demanded by representative later Work, with its source and horizon. |
| intermediate disposition | The intermediate contribution, result boundary, and conditions, only when they help this decision. Keep a proposed next action separate from the state description. A threshold may define an intended result boundary, but a claim that it was reached needs observations. |
| discriminating evidence | Representative task, observation, work product, inference rule, and protected consequence that distinguish the claim. |
| trade-off and conflict | What time, workload, autonomy, support, evidence burden, or other claim could be harmed by prioritizing this one. |
| next change point | The nearest observation, source return, owner decision, or condition that can change the row. |

Use an aggregate only when the receiving decision does not need any claim identity, asymmetry, critical-error status, support dependence, or uncertainty that the aggregate removes. Keep the underlying rows available whenever an aggregate is used.

#### HCD.4:4.3 - Compare Without Inventing Order

Apply `C.32.MWA`'s guard against layout substitution. A course sequence, Method hierarchy, Work order, organization chart, or platform dashboard may be a source structure; none is the profile architecture merely because its rows line up with capability names.

Ask which contributions are simultaneous, which interfaces constrain each other, which supported claims must be preserved, which gaps are limiting, and where a local gain moves burden or risk. State the current profile, target profile, difference, priority characteristics, explicit trade-offs, and the recommendation or unresolved question before making a choice.

#### HCD.4:4.4 - Make a Bounded Choice Under C.11

When a bounded choice is requested, obtain the inputs and conditions required by `C.11`:

| `C.11` input or condition | HCD.4 use |
| --- | --- |
| `DecisionSubject` and `DecisionSubjectGranularity` | The actual chooser and chooser-bearing level. Do not default to generic `Agent`; route unresolved agency to `A.13`. |
| `OptionSet` | Concrete options already on the table. HCD.4 does not generate a missing programme alternative. |
| `PreferenceOrder` or `EvaluativeMeasure` | The explicit comparison basis, including protected claims and unacceptable critical errors. |
| `BeliefState` | Current uncertainty, evidence limits, and live rivals shared across the options. |
| `OutcomeModel` | How the options and uncertainty map to the consequences that matter for this choice. |
| dependence layer | Evidential by default; causal, counterfactual, subjunctive, or other heavier dependence requires its own current support. |
| `ChoiceRule` | The condition under which `choose now`, `reject current set`, `probe again`, or `reroute` is lawful. |
| probe-worthiness | `ProbeActionSet`, `ProbeBudget`, `CostToProbe`, and value of information or computation for a live probe. If no feasible further probe is worth its cost, state why. |
| governance | Holder participation and the chooser's applicable authority; authorization, commitment, and later execution remain separate. |

If a required input, condition, or necessary current/target evidence is absent, keep the profile comparison and recommendation if they are supported, then return `profile-decision-blocked` with the missing value, blocked choice, safe boundary, and retry condition.

With those inputs and conditions established, the chooser applies the explicit `ChoiceRule` to the current comparison basis, dependence layer, and probe-worthiness values. Emit a `ChoiceResult` with the lawful disposition, its reason, and the next question specified by `C.11`. The decision record documents this application, the inputs used, and the emitted result.

#### HCD.4:4.5 - Recognition and Assurance

**Recognition.** Use HCD.4 when a conversation contains “well-rounded,” “level up,” “mastery score,” “skills matrix,” “promotion profile,” or a course pathway, while the actual decision depends on several different contributions to later Work.

**Assurance.** Stronger reliance requires holder- and condition-compatible task evidence for each decision-bearing claim; explicit current, target, and uncertainty positions; preservation of critical-error and support-dependence boundaries; an inspectable trade-off account; and, for a choice, all required current `C.11` inputs and conditions, application of the rule, and an inspectable result. High-stakes employment, clinical, safety, legal, or release uses need their direct owner and assurance in addition to this profile.

#### HCD.4:4.6 - What Changes in Practice

A practitioner stops asking, “What level is this person?” and asks, “Which contributions are supported under which conditions, which must be preserved, which gap matters across simultaneous Work, and what uncertainty could still reverse the decision?” The profile returns a useful comparison even when no choice is yet lawful.

### HCD.4:5 - Archetypal Grounding

#### HCD.4:5.1 - Constructed Positive Case: ImpactReview-17

`ImpactReview-17` is a constructed pedagogical case. `ImpactReview-17-Domain-Oracle-v1` supplies engineering correctness and criticality criteria for `PumpWorks-Config-Baseline-42` and `AI-Assist-M4`. The observations and decision conditions below are stipulated case data.

The human holder is Engineer-P14. The later-Work demand is independent review of AI-proposed change-impact claims under a forty-five-minute case limit, current source/configuration access, auditable evidence, and bounded safety and maintenance specialist returns. HCD.4 keeps four claim rows:

| Claim in the constructed profile | Current evidence | Target and trade-off disposition |
| --- | --- | --- |
| source/configuration trace | 8/8 under the stipulated configuration | Preserve; do not trade traceability for speed. |
| independent AI-output challenge | 2/4 planted-error cases; both misses are unfamiliar variants | Priority gap; target 4/4 and zero accepted seeded safety-critical omissions, while the forty-five-minute limit remains a visible cost. |
| bounded specialist return | 2/2 relevant cases | Preserve the authority boundary; do not internalize specialist judgement. |
| auditable evidence return | 8/8 | Preserve; no aggregate may hide a missing critical-error record. |

The resulting comparison says: preserve the three supported claims, investigate independent AI-output challenge as the limiting gap, keep the prompt-design rival live, and do not select a programme. The current profile is not one score; the target is not proof of mastery or transformation.

Engineer-P14 faces one person-level self-development-focus choice within a separately pre-authorized twelve-hour allocation. The constructed decision record captures the chooser's inputs and application of `C.11`:

| Decision value | Constructed case value |
| --- | --- |
| `DecisionSubject` | Engineer-P14. |
| `DecisionSubjectGranularity` | Person-level; Engineer-P14 participates directly in the comparison. |
| `OptionSet` | `O1` preserve the profile without selecting a change; `O2` make independent AI-output challenge the priority for later programme comparison while preserving the three supported claims; `O3` defer a human profile-change priority and return the live prompt-design question to the Method/tool owner. |
| `EvaluativeMeasure` | Prefer fewer accepted seeded safety-critical omissions while preserving source/configuration trace, specialist authority, auditable evidence, the forty-five-minute case limit, and reversibility. |
| `BeliefState` | Two unfamiliar misses, a small sample, and a live prompt-design rival remain. |
| `OutcomeModel` | A two-case prompt comparison can still change which of `O2` or `O3` survives, while differences between the cases remain unresolved. This supplies evidential input rather than an isolated causal prompt effect. `O1` leaves the observed critical-error gap unchanged. |
| dependence layer | Evidential baseline only; no causal intervention or capability-change claim is used. |
| `ChoiceRule` | Choose a profile-change priority now only if it survives the live nonhuman rivals and no feasible decision-changing probe is worth its cost; otherwise emit `probe again` for the smallest feasible probe that can reverse `O2` versus `O3` within the allocation and protected conditions. |
| `ProbeActionSet` | Two further unfamiliar seeded-error cases, with one first attempt per case: one uses a neutral prompt, the other an explicit counter-hypothesis prompt. Observe whether Engineer-P14 generates and tests a counter-hypothesis, and whether they reject the wrong AI claim. Keep the same sources, tools, specialist routes, and forty-five-minute limit per case. |
| `ProbeBudget` | Two hours remaining for this pre-choice probe inside the twelve-hour allocation. |
| `CostToProbe` | Up to ninety minutes for the two attempts, plus thirty minutes reserved for preparation and comparison: two hours in total. |
| value of information | Decision-changing: a tested counter-hypothesis and rejection of the wrong claim only under the explicit prompt would strengthen `O3`; misses under both prompts would strengthen `O2`. Interpret these outcomes with the small sample and unresolved case differences. The seeded critical-error boundary makes this possible change in choice worth the bounded cost. |
| probe-worthiness | Feasible and worth its cost. |
| `ChoiceResult` | **`probe again`** with this two-case comparison, one attempt per prompt condition. No profile option is selected yet. |
| next question and authority | Return the new observations to HCD.3 and the updated comparison to HCD.4. The accountable manager owns time allocation, Work assignment, and employment consequences; safety and release owners retain their authority. |

Applying the stated `ChoiceRule` returns `probe again`: the probe fits the two-hour budget, preserves the protected conditions, and can still change which of `O2` or `O3` survives. Engineer-P14 participates in the comparison. The pre-authorized time supplies resources for the bounded probe; safety, release, and employment decisions remain with their authorized owners.

#### HCD.4:5.2 - Missing Case: ReleaseWork-R9

For Engineer-E27, HCD.1 can return a prospective `ReleaseWork-R9` demand hypothesis, but no compatible current profile comparison is supportable. The actual Work occurrence, occurrence-produced result claim, occurrence-bound result record, representative observations, and obtaining `SYSE.21` supplier result required by `XRI-21` remain distinct missing values.

HCD.4 may retain prospective target claims for AI-output challenge, configuration-impact reasoning, evidence return, specialist coordination, critical-error detection, and unfamiliar-variant adaptation. It cannot populate current claim rows, claim a current-versus-target gap, or select a profile priority.

Return `profile-decision-blocked`. The blocked choice is any preserve, investigate, defer, narrow, specialist-evidence, or profile-change decision that depends on current holder evidence. The incomplete HCD profile does not justify claims of mastery, a current-versus-target gap, or transformation, or decisions about a programme, allocation, or employment. This boundary applies to uses of that unsupported profile; separate decisions need their own sufficient evidence and appropriate authority. Retry with a compatible same-holder demand frame, claim-by-claim current and target evidence under the intended configuration and evidence window, direct target-domain and specialist boundaries, holder participation, and—if a choice is requested—the current `DecisionSubject`, pre-existing `OptionSet`, comparison basis, probe-worthiness values, authority, and `ChoiceRule`. Do not fabricate HCD.2 alternatives to complete the record.

#### HCD.4:5.3 - Unlike Case: Clinical and Participation Profiles

For an adult seeking treatment for social anxiety, use qualified clinical care for diagnosis, treatment targets, provider competence, safety, and outcome interpretation. Symptom scales, exposure ladders, treatment plans, and provider pathways inform that clinical work; they do not by themselves establish a profile of the person's contributions in later Work.

HCD.4 may compare condition-qualified contributions for a separately legitimate non-clinical participation or later-Work question only when the person and clinical owner supply a safe boundary and compatible evidence. A symptom change alone does not demonstrate the later-Work contribution. HCD.4 does not select treatment, and clinical safety must not be traded against productivity. Managerial authority over allocation confers neither clinical competence nor clinical decision authority and does not replace the person's consent. If the required boundary or evidence is missing, return `profile-decision-blocked`; route a clinical question to its clinical owner.

### HCD.4:6 - Bias-Annotation

| Recurring bias | Likely drift | Repair |
| --- | --- | --- |
| scalar bias | One score hides claim identity, critical errors, support dependence, and uncertainty. | Keep claim rows; aggregate only when the receiving decision loses nothing material. |
| ladder bias | Current, intermediate, and target states become a universal maturity path. | Use intermediate descriptions only when decision-useful and condition-qualified; distinguish intended targets from observed states. |
| curriculum bias | Course order becomes capability architecture. | Recover simultaneous later-Work contributions and keep curriculum future and separate. |
| source-layout bias | A Method hierarchy, Work order, organization chart, or dashboard becomes the profile. | Recover contributions and their mutual constraints from simultaneous Work. Use a corresponding source structure only when that correspondence is justified for the profile question. |
| tool-score bias | A model prediction or assisted result becomes human mastery. | Bind the prediction to its task, data, support posture, uncertainty, and direct evidence. |
| deficit-only bias | Strong claims disappear while one gap is amplified. | State what must be preserved and the trade-off created by prioritizing the gap. |
| chooser bias | A manager, platform, or assisting System is treated as the chooser without a current choice relation. | Name `DecisionSubject` and granularity; route unresolved agency to `A.13`. |
| probe-for-free bias | More information is always requested. | Name budget, cost, decision-changing value, and the condition for stopping. |
| authority collapse | A profile recommendation becomes authorization, employment action, safety choice, or commitment. | Keep comparison, `ChoiceResult`, authorization, commitment, and later Work separate. |

### HCD.4:7 - Conformance Checklist

- [ ] One exact human holder and a compatible HCD.1 demand account or equivalent qualified scope are recoverable.
- [ ] Later-Work family, configuration, conditions, horizon, evidence window, receiving decision, and representative simultaneous interfaces are explicit.
- [ ] Each claim row states contribution, current evidence, target, optional intermediate only when useful, conditions, support posture, observation date, uncertainty, discriminating task/evidence, critical-error boundary, trade-off, and next change point.
- [ ] Profile descriptions and their comparison or aggregate are distinguished from instructional plans, realized learning trajectory, performed learning Work, and human transformation; occurrence and change claims have corresponding evidence.
- [ ] Any source structure used for the profile is justified by the contributions and mutual constraints in representative Work.
- [ ] Supported claims to preserve are visible alongside gaps and trade-offs.
- [ ] An aggregate is used only when it removes no decision-bearing identity, asymmetry, critical-error, support, or uncertainty value.
- [ ] The profile first returns priority characteristics, trade-offs, limiting gaps, and a recommendation or unresolved question to one named owner.
- [ ] For any bounded choice, the chooser has applied the `ChoiceRule` to the current `C.11` inputs; the record includes chooser granularity, pre-existing options, comparison basis, belief, outcome model, rule, probe-worthiness, emitted result, participation, and authority.
- [ ] A missing decision or evidence value produces `profile-decision-blocked` with blocked choice, safe boundary, and exact retry condition.
- [ ] The result fabricates no HCD.2 programme and authorizes no curriculum, provider, employment, safety, legal, clinical, or release action.
- [ ] The receiver can identify claims to preserve, the consequential gap, what could reverse the recommendation, and who decides next.

### HCD.4:8 - Common Anti-Patterns and How to Avoid Them

| Anti-pattern | Why it fails | Repair |
| --- | --- | --- |
| “Overall mastery: 72%” | The aggregate can hide a critical error and unsupported claim. | Restore claim rows and make the receiving decision justify any later aggregate. |
| “Complete level 2 before level 3” | A convenient ladder becomes an unsupported Work or learning sequence. | Show simultaneous contributions. Use a local intermediate target only when the current decision needs it, and judge its achievement from observations rather than course position. |
| “The platform predicts mastery” | A model output depends on its data, item representation, task, and support assumptions. | Keep it as one evidence source and require direct claim-task-observation-inference trace. |
| “The target profile is the current profile” | Intention is mistaken for current evidence or transformation. | Record current, target, difference, and evidence dates separately. |
| “Improve the weak dimension” | The decision can damage a strong claim, authority boundary, or protected consequence. | State preserved claims and moved trade-offs before recommending priority. |
| “The profile chose the programme” | A comparison neither generates alternatives nor supplies an HCD.2 Method. | Stop at recommendation, blocker, or complete `C.11` choice over already-available non-programme options. |
| “More data” | The next observation has no decision-changing purpose or bounded cost. | Name the exact probe, budget, cost, value, and reversal condition—or close now. |
| “The manager is the subject” | Manager authority over allocation does not erase the human holder's participation or transfer clinical, safety, or release authority. | Keep `DecisionSubject`, participation, and direct-owner authorities explicit. |

### HCD.4:9 - Consequences

The result makes a profile usable without flattening it. Practitioners can preserve reliable contributions, expose one limiting gap, show the cost of prioritization, and distinguish a recommendation from a lawful choice. Missing evidence or choice values become exact blockers instead of silently completed forms.

The cost is a larger evidence surface than one score. Claim rows can disagree, uncertainty can remain, and the lawful result may be `probe again` or reroute rather than a selected priority. A later programme, intervention, or employment decision still needs its own Method and authority.

### HCD.4:10 - Rationale

Capability profiles are architectures of simultaneous, condition-qualified claims, not inventories arranged by visual order. Claim identity is needed because contributions can have different supports, critical-error limits, uncertainty, and trade-offs. The profile's practical value lies in the difference it preserves, not in a total it can display.

Keeping comparison before choice prevents the profile from inventing a chooser, options, or authority. Under `C.11`, applying the `ChoiceRule` can return `probe again` when a feasible observation is worth its cost and can still change which option survives. The record makes the used inputs, applied rule, and result inspectable; `profile-decision-blocked` identifies absent prerequisites.

### HCD.4:11 - SoTA-Echoing

The working question is: **How should a practitioner compare several human capability claims for simultaneous later Work without turning a score, source layout, or target into mastery or a programme?**

The selected current line combines claim-level assessment traceability and bounded mastery evidence. Winget and Persky’s [A practical review of mastery learning](https://doi.org/10.5688/ajpe8906) (2022; `COG-06`) describes arrangements with local thresholds, formative evidence, correction, and retest; it establishes neither one universal level nor later professional performance. Gutierrez de Blume’s [Calibrating calibration](https://doi.org/10.1037/edu0000674) (2022; `COG-07`) informs the use of self-monitoring accuracy as one evidence source, while confidence is not a demonstration of capability. Arieli-Attali and colleagues’ [Expanded evidence-centered design](https://doi.org/10.3389/fpsyg.2019.00853) (2019; `COG-08`) supports making the claim, task, observable evidence, inference, and proposed transition recoverable; this design framework supplies no causal treatment comparison, and an evidence model does not make its claim true. Abdelrahman and colleagues’ [Knowledge tracing: a survey](https://doi.org/10.1145/3569576) (2023; `COG-09`) describes latent-state and next-response predictions under model and data assumptions; such predictions do not demonstrate transfer, retention, capability, or causal learning. `C.32.MWA` adds the architecture guard: relationships among simultaneous contributions need not match course order, Method partition, Work structure, or organization structure. Recover those relationships from the Work; a matching layout alone does not justify the correspondence.

A serious alternative is the single competency score, role-level matrix, or mastery ladder. It is compact and can support coarse reporting, but it commonly loses asymmetric claims, critical errors, support dependence, uncertainty, simultaneous interfaces, and moved burden. HCD.4 retains a scalar only for a receiving decision that demonstrably needs none of those values; the authoritative comparison remains claim by claim.

The selected line does not establish a universal capability state space, threshold, curriculum, development sequence, transfer result, or transformation. Choice requirements come from `C.11`; a decision record documents their application to the case. Direct target-domain sources supply correctness and criticality criteria. Reopen when the holder, later Work, configuration, Method or source edition, support posture, claim set, evidence date, critical-error predicate, trade-off, profile owner, options, chooser, authority, or decision rule changes.

### HCD.4:12 - Relations

- **`HCD.1`:** supplies the same-holder scope and result frame. An equivalent qualified demand account may substitute; an incompatible population profile may not.
- **`HCD.3`:** may supply a qualified target and any still-plausible rival as evidence. HCD.4 does not inherit an intervention, programme, or transformation claim.
- **`C.32.MWA`:** prevents course sequence, Method hierarchy, Work order, organization chart, model, or platform layout from becoming the profile architecture by alignment alone.
- **[Winget and Persky’s review (2022)](https://doi.org/10.5688/ajpe8906) (`COG-06`):** supplies the local-threshold, correction, and retest contribution, not a universal mastery ladder.
- **[Gutierrez de Blume’s meta-analysis (2022)](https://doi.org/10.1037/edu0000674) (`COG-07`):** supplies self-monitoring accuracy as one bounded evidence source.
- **[Arieli-Attali and colleagues’ design framework (2019)](https://doi.org/10.3389/fpsyg.2019.00853) (`COG-08`):** supplies claim/task/evidence/inference traceability, not a causal intervention result.
- **[Abdelrahman and colleagues’ survey (2023)](https://doi.org/10.1145/3569576) (`COG-09`):** supplies model-prediction limits; a predicted state does not establish this person's mastery.
- **`C.11`:** governs one local choice over a current `OptionSet` and emits `choose now`, `reject current set`, `probe again`, or `reroute` under an explicit `ChoiceRule`.
- **`A.13`:** receives an unresolved agency claim when an assisting System, coupled arrangement, team, or organization is proposed as chooser.
- **`E.23.CAE` and `E.23.CDI`:** may supply differential or later development/transfer evidence under their own entry conditions; neither is implied by a profile.
- **Target-domain and specialist sources:** provide criteria and evidence for correctness, quality, criticality, safety, legal, clinical, and specialist-return questions in the claim rows. Qualified specialists supply professional judgements, and authorized actors make the corresponding decisions.
- **Programme comparison (`HCD.2`):** may receive priority characteristics and trade-offs as fit criteria for the same person, later Work, and decision. HCD.4 requires no programme-comparison result and does not fabricate programme alternatives.
- **Managers and direct decision owners:** retain allocation, assignment, employment, safety, legal, clinical, and release authority; the human holder's participation remains explicit.

### HCD.4:End

<a id="hcd-2"></a>
## HCD.2 - Compose and Compare Capability-Development Programmes for Later Work

> **Type:** Human Capability Development practitioner programme-composition pattern
> **Status:** Candidate

### HCD.2:0 - Use This When

Use this pattern when one named person has a recoverable later-Work demand and a practitioner, manager, or that person needs several feasible ways to develop the required contributions. Begin with the person's current mastered and supported configuration, the complete later-Work tasks, and the conditions of participation; do not begin with a topic list or a preferred course.

The first useful result is a **programme-comparison account**: comparable descriptions of the actual programme candidates, their intended task closure, contribution and access allocations, burdens, uncertainties, evidence needs, applicability conditions, and the smallest change that would reopen the comparison. If only one candidate remains feasible, describe that candidate and the missing comparison rather than inventing a rival or calling it dominant. A separate authorized choice consumes the comparison.

The practical gain is a programme that can be inspected as a finite change from what the person can already do with available support. The practitioner can see whether a proposed component completes a usable contribution, preserves a prerequisite, adds a future option, or merely displaces practice that another required result still needs.

Do not use HCD.2 to design a universal course for an unknown audience, diagnose a person, choose on behalf of the decision owner, promise learning, or establish provider competence. When the current recipient is a learning-product author who does not yet know the learners, return the outputs, audience assumptions, existing alternatives, resource boundary, and individualization questions needed for the smallest comparable learning-product design result. A named person's compatible Work, entry performance, support, and participation conditions are the retry inputs for personal comparison.

### HCD.2:0.1 - Working Terms

| Name in this pattern | What it denotes |
| --- | --- |
| human holder | The one identified person whose development is being considered. A course audience, job family, cohort, or average participant is not that person. |
| current mastered and supported configuration | The contributions the person can currently make under stated conditions, together with references, AI, tools, specialists, and other supports that are actually available. Unsupported assumptions about current capability or access do not belong in the baseline. |
| capability-development programme | A bounded arrangement of preparation, representative attempts, feedback, correction, retries, access, and later checks intended to help the person make specified contributions in later Work. It is not the learning product that may carry parts of the arrangement and does not establish that development occurred. |
| complete work task | A representative later-Work task whose required result, critical errors, human contributions, supporting contributions, and stopping conditions are sufficiently present to guide programme composition. |
| contribution allocation | A statement of what the person must recognize or perform, what may be retrieved from a reference, what controlled AI help may supply, and what a specialist or other provider must contribute. Several allocations may coexist in one task. |
| access allocation | The tools, sources, people, environments, permissions, timing, and support conditions that a programme assumes or must obtain. Intended help, permitted help, actual availability, and help received in an attempt remain different facts. |
| comparable programme description | One candidate described against the same holder, later-Work outputs, protected errors, horizon, evidence posture, and resource coordinates as the other candidates. |
| learning-product design return | A positive outside-owner return for an author who has audience hypotheses but no personal holder evidence. It preserves the design question and states which learner evidence is needed before personal comparison. |

The programme, its description, a later decision to adopt it, the Work that enacts it, and observed human development are different objects. HCD.2 composes and compares descriptions; it does not make the later events true.

### HCD.2:1 - Problem Frame

A person may need to recognize when a professional Method applies, retrieve a precise rule, perform a calculation, challenge an AI proposal, and obtain a specialist result in one piece of later Work. A syllabus can mention every topic and still fail to make that combination attainable. Conversely, requiring the person to memorize every detail can waste scarce time and remove useful support already present in the Work.

Programme candidates start from different baselines. One person may already perform the routine operation but miss a hazardous mismatch. Another may need a prerequisite before a whole task is useful. A third may be capable with a reference and specialist return but not under unsupported conditions. Time, participation, project access, teacher capacity, and the cost of later checking can change which programme is feasible.

The practitioner therefore needs to compose finite alternatives around complete work tasks, make their human and external contributions visible, and compare them without treating detail, duration, novelty, or a technique label as evidence of superiority.

### HCD.2:2 - Problem

The common shortcut is to translate a demand into topics, allocate hours, and call the result a programme. This hides whether the pieces combine in representative Work, whether practice and correction fit inside the budget, and whether the assumed teacher, data, AI, specialist, or project access exists.

A second shortcut starts with one polished programme and compares every rival with its documentation rather than with the same required result. The detailed candidate then appears to dominate because its assumptions and costs are visible while the others remain sketches.

A third shortcut assigns an audience profile to every future learner. It converts a course-design hypothesis into a personal diagnosis and can recommend development without same-holder evidence. The practitioner needs a useful return for both situations: a personal comparison when compatible inputs exist, and a precise learning-product design question when they do not.

### HCD.2:3 - Forces

- Complete tasks expose coordination and critical errors, while some prerequisites need preparation before meaningful whole-task practice.
- Internalized performance can reduce access delay, while reliable references, tools, AI, and specialists can be legitimate parts of later Work.
- A detailed programme is easier to inspect, while unequal detail is not evidence that it contributes more.
- Personalization can remove redundant work, while role labels and audience averages do not establish one person's starting capability.
- A finite time budget forces displacement, while hidden reading, preparation, provider Work, and retries are easy to leave outside the count.
- Early supported performance can enable practice, while it does not establish later independent, changed-condition, or delayed performance.
- One candidate may be the only feasible option now, while feasibility alone does not establish dominance, effectiveness, or a final choice.
- Programme design can reveal missing access or provider capability, while the description does not make that support available.
- Future options can be valuable, while speculative option value must not replace the required near-term work result.

### HCD.2:4 - Solution

Compose each candidate as a finite change from the person's current mastered and supported configuration. Keep complete later-Work tasks, human and external contributions, practice and correction, resources, evidence limits, and future options visible on one comparison basis. Return the comparison without making the final choice.

#### HCD.2:4.1 - Bind the Personal Comparison

Name:

1. the exact human holder and the basis for their participation;
2. the receiving decision, decision owner, later-Work family, configuration, horizon, and protected result or critical-error conditions;
3. a compatible HCD.1 demand account or equivalent qualified later-Work input;
4. the current performance evidence, supported contributions, unresolved gaps, and any HCD.3 target or HCD.4 profile that the comparison actually uses;
5. the professional Methods, target-domain criteria, source access, tools, AI posture, specialists, provider contributions, environment, authority, and time that are available or still assumed; and
6. the development stake that the candidates must address without silently changing it.

HCD.1, HCD.3, and HCD.4 can supply these inputs, but their PatternIDs are not ritual prerequisites. Compatible direct evidence is enough for the positions the current comparison consumes. Use diagnosis only when rival causes would change the programme.

If personal evidence, participation, later Work, or support conditions are missing, stop the personal comparison at the affected claim. For an unknown audience, issue a learning-product design return. For a missing target-domain Method or correctness criterion, return the exact qualified outside-owner question. If no candidate can make the supplied development stake feasible, return that constraint and the surviving alternatives to the separate navigation or target decision rather than weakening the stake inside HCD.2.

#### HCD.2:4.2 - Compose Task-Closing Candidates

1. **Select representative complete tasks.** State the required result, important variants, critical errors, Work products, conditions, and stopping rules. A topic or Method name is only a discovery cue.
2. **Decompose only far enough to allocate contributions.** For every material contribution, state what the person must recognize and perform, what may be retrieved from a current reference, what controlled AI help may supply, and what requires a specialist or other provider. Keep the human check required for assisted output explicit.
3. **Separate prerequisites from simultaneous complements.** A prerequisite must be available before the relevant practice can proceed. A reference, tool, or specialist contribution may instead remain part of the same later Work. Do not teach every complement as if it had to become unaided personal performance.
4. **Construct finite changes from the baseline.** Add, substitute, remove, or retain programme elements relative to the person's current configuration. Explain which task combination each change is intended to close, which future option it opens, and which existing contribution it might displace or weaken.
5. **Include the complete development loop.** Account for preparation, demonstrations or explanations where useful, representative attempts, feedback, correction, retries, changed-condition or later checks, and the access and provider contributions that make those activities possible.
6. **Keep downstream elaboration explicit.** HCD.5 can select the development Methods, HCD.6 can design the practice tasks, HCD.7 can establish available support, HCD.8 can build a missing arrangement, and HCD.9–HCD.10 can specify practice and progression. HCD.2 needs enough of those contributions to make a candidate comparable; it does not pretend that an unperformed elaboration already exists.

#### HCD.2:4.3 - Make the Descriptions Comparable

Describe every candidate on the same decision-bearing positions:

| Comparison position | Required content |
| --- | --- |
| holder and use | Human holder, decision owner, participation basis, later-Work family, configuration, horizon, and receiving decision. |
| current configuration | Supported current contributions, protected strengths, unavailable values, uncertainty, and evidence window. |
| intended task closure | Complete tasks and outputs the programme is intended to make attainable, including protected errors and variants. Keep intended attainability separate from observed learning. |
| contribution allocation | Human recognition and performance, reference use, controlled AI help and human checking, specialist returns, and other provider contributions. |
| preparation and sequence | True prerequisites, simultaneous complements, dependency reasons, and any replaceable or optional ordering. Printed topic order is not a capability hierarchy. |
| practice and evidence plan | Representative attempts, permitted help, feedback, correction, retries, changed-condition checks, delayed checks, and the observation each supports. |
| access and provider basis | Actual and assumed sources, tools, AI, people, environments, permissions, capacity, peak load, and missing enablement. |
| resources and displacement | Learner time, provider time, preparation, assessment, transition, tool or material burden, and the useful activity displaced by the change. |
| uncertainty and source limits | Evidence for each expected contribution, unsupported effect claims, transfer and retention limits, and the observation that could change the estimate. |
| continuation | Future options opened, preserved or closed; applicability conditions; exact constraint or evidence gap; and smallest change that reopens the comparison. |

Unequal detail is itself a comparison gap. Develop only the smallest missing fragment needed to compare the same task, output, conditions, support, and resource coordinates. Do not require complete competing courses when one closed task family can discriminate the alternatives.

#### HCD.2:4.4 - Compare the Finite Programme Changes

Use `C.11.CRC` to compare each realizable candidate configuration with the person's current configuration. Name the baseline, finite addition or substitution, candidate configuration, horizon, scenarios, result and resource coordinates, interactions, uncertainty, transition, reversibility, and option effects.

For HCD.2, the decision-bearing coordinates normally include:

- closure of the required later-Work outputs and protected critical-error conditions;
- learner, teacher, assessor, specialist, tool, and transition burden;
- dependence on references, AI, cues, project access, authority, and provider availability;
- preservation of current strengths and practice needed by other contributions;
- the qualified expectation and evidence plan for supported, independent, changed-condition, transfer, and delayed performance; and
- future development options and the cost of reopening a closed route.

Use `dominated`, `non-dominated`, `beneficial`, `harmful`, or `indeterminate` only for the candidates, coordinates, horizon, conditions, and evidence actually compared. A programme is not non-dominated merely because it was generated, and one feasible candidate is not dominant merely because no rival is ready. HCD.2 supplies the comparison claim; `C.11` supplies any later bounded choice from the complete choice inputs.

#### HCD.2:4.5 - Return the Programme-Comparison Account

Return one account containing:

| Result position | Required content |
| --- | --- |
| comparison boundary | Holder, later Work, supplied development stake, decision owner, candidates, horizon, conditions, participation, evidence window, and direct outside owners. |
| candidate descriptions | The comparable positions in §4.3 for every actual candidate, including any one candidate that remains feasible. |
| comparison claims | Supported relative contribution, burden, dependence, uncertainty, displacement, and option claims; any dominance relation is explicitly bounded. |
| access allocations | Intended human, reference, AI, specialist, provider, environment, permission, and timing contributions, with actual availability distinguished from assumption. |
| evidence and gaps | Sources and observations supporting each claim, missing values, unsupported stronger claims, and the conclusion each gap blocks. |
| disposition | `comparable candidates`, `one feasible candidate with comparison gap`, `current candidate set infeasible`, or `personal comparison blocked with learning-product design return`. These are readable HCD.2 dispositions, not a final programme choice. |
| continuation | The smallest comparable fragment, provider result, personal observation, budget change, target decision, or other condition that permits the next comparison or choice. |

When a final choice is requested, pass this account to the authorized decision owner and the applicable decision Method. Preserve the option set, preferences, belief state, outcome model, protected conditions, probe worth, and authority required there. Do not turn the HCD.2 comparison into authorization, commitment, launch, or proof of effect.

#### HCD.2:4.6 - Recognition and Assurance

**Recognition.** Use HCD.2 when the live question sounds like “Which way could this person become able to do the whole job?” and the alternatives differ in preparation, practice, support, time, or future options. The smallest useful result may be one comparable task fragment rather than a complete curriculum.

**Assurance.** Stronger reliance needs same-holder and condition-compatible inputs, qualified target-domain tasks and error criteria, credible provider and access claims, complete resource coordinates, and explicit evidence limits. A programme can be compared before it is chosen or run. Effectiveness, transfer, retention, provider operation, and personal development require their own observations.

#### HCD.2:4.7 - What Changes in Practice

A practitioner stops asking, “Which course covers all the topics?” and asks, “Which finite programme change closes this person's complete later-Work tasks, with what human and external contributions, at what burden, and what evidence is still missing?” A detailed course becomes one candidate; an unknown audience receives a design question rather than an invented personal profile.

### HCD.2:5 - Archetypal Grounding

#### HCD.2:5.1 - Constructed Personal Case: Learner-FP6

`Learner-FP6` is a constructed pedagogical holder. The stipulated entry attempt shows that this person can build a device model but confuses a flow unit, the recipient of the result, and the organizational permission needed for the proposed action. The intended later-Work outputs, mandatory tasks, 340 learner-hour limit, available teacher and worked examples, and protected critical errors are held fixed for this comparison. No observation of actual learning is claimed.

Two six-hour candidates replace harder optional cases rather than add time:

| Candidate | Finite change and intended contribution | Burden, dependence, and evidence gap |
| --- | --- | --- |
| focused preparation | Allocate two hours each to selecting the result recipient, tracing consequences for other systems, and checking flow units and constraints before the next whole task. The mandatory tasks, later variation, and final result remain unchanged. | Uses an explicit prerequisite route and keeps teacher feedback inside the existing arrangement. It may isolate the three weaknesses clearly, but the comparison still needs a changed-condition whole-task attempt to show whether the contributions combine. |
| supported whole-task preparation | Use the same six learner hours for a complete decision task in which the person must calculate with the flow unit, address the right recipient, and obtain or respect the required permission. A worked example and bounded teacher questions support the first attempt; a changed case then removes answer-revealing help. | Tests integration earlier but may conceal which prerequisite failed. Selecting, demonstrating, and reviewing the extra case consumes teacher preparation and feedback Work that must fit an established capacity or be priced separately. |

HCD.2 returns two comparable descriptions, the common output and evidence plan, and the unresolved provider-load and changed-condition evidence. It does not choose between them. An independent attempt showing three separable errors would strengthen focused preparation; success on the components but failure to combine them would strengthen the whole-task candidate. Either observation reopens only the affected comparison claims.

#### HCD.2:5.2 - Unknown Audience: FoundationsProgramme A, B, and C

A learning-product author has two intended outputs, an audience hypothesis, three 340-hour compositions, and no named learners. A allocates 202/96/42 hours to general/domain/joint application, B allocates 88/204/48, and C allocates 136/136/68. C is described in more detail; that detail does not establish dominance.

The HCD.2 boundary returns a learning-product design question: preserve the two outputs, audience assumptions, three alternatives, learner and teacher resource coordinates, support assumptions, and individualization questions. Use C's existing material and develop only the smallest missing A/B fragment for one closed task family with the same output, entry assumptions, representative attempt, feedback, retry, access, and resource account. Do not fabricate one future learner or require three complete courses before comparison becomes useful.

If the learner budget falls from 340 to 204 hours while both outputs remain fixed, none of the three original candidates is launchable as described. Multiplying every activity by 0.6 or hiding reading and practice outside the budget does not close the task. The return asks for a new candidate that identifies the removed or replaced 136 hours and preserves the mechanisms needed for the outputs. The separate 240-hour teacher calculation does not replace learner time or establish a competent available service. A 170-hour ThinkingFoundations-only offer answers a narrower output question and therefore is not an improved version of the same candidate set.

#### HCD.2:5.3 - One Feasible Candidate Is Not a Winner

A named person has a qualified near-term engineering task and strong entry evidence. Only one candidate currently fits the deadline and available project access; a second candidate depends on an unavailable specialist. HCD.2 may return the feasible candidate, its finite comparison with the current configuration, and the exact specialist result that blocks the other candidate. It does not invent a weak rival, call the feasible programme universally best, or infer that the programme will work. A later provider result, deadline change, or changed task reopens the bounded comparison.

### HCD.2:6 - Bias-Annotation

Lenses: **Gov**, **Arch**, **Onto/Epist**, **Prag**, **Did**. Scope: personal human-capability programme composition and comparison under qualified later-Work, participation, support, and evidence conditions.

| Recurring bias | Likely drift | Repair |
| --- | --- | --- |
| course-solution bias | The requested course or familiar programme is treated as the answer before later Work and baseline are recovered. | Begin from the holder, complete work tasks, current configuration, and supplied development stake. |
| detailed-candidate bias | The most documented candidate appears superior to sketches. | Compare one minimal closed-task fragment on the same positions before expanding the candidates. |
| topic-count bias | Coverage or PatternID count substitutes for contribution to later Work. | Trace every included element to task closure, a prerequisite, a support contribution, an evidence need, or a future option. |
| role-average bias | Audience or role assumptions become one person's current profile. | Require compatible same-holder evidence or issue the learning-product design return. |
| support invisibility | Teacher, assessor, specialist, AI, access, and preparation are assumed free and available. | Record access allocations, actual availability, peak load, and missing enablement separately. |
| assistance attribution | Helped output is treated as independent human capability or learning. | Preserve permitted, available, received, independent, changed-condition, and delayed evidence states. |
| sunk-curriculum bias | Existing content is retained because it was expensive to create. | Compare the finite contribution and displacement of retaining it under the current task and budget. |
| technique-brand bias | A pedagogy label is assumed to work across holders and tasks. | State its proposed mechanism, source limit, required observation, and serious alternative. |

### HCD.2:7 - Conformance Checklist

- [ ] One exact human holder is named for a personal comparison; an unknown audience receives a learning-product design return rather than an invented profile.
- [ ] The later-Work family, supplied development stake, protected outputs and errors, configuration, horizon, participation, evidence window, decision owner, and outside owners are explicit.
- [ ] The baseline contains only supported current human and external contributions under stated conditions.
- [ ] Every programme candidate closes or tests a recognizable complete-task combination rather than merely covering topics.
- [ ] Human recognition and performance, reference retrieval, controlled AI help and checking, specialist returns, and other provider contributions are allocated explicitly.
- [ ] True prerequisites are distinguished from supports and complements used during the same later Work.
- [ ] Preparation, representative attempts, feedback, correction, retries, changed-condition or delayed checks, and provider/access work are inside the candidate account.
- [ ] Candidates use the same decision-bearing output, error, burden, support, horizon, uncertainty, and future-option positions; unequal detail is reported as a gap.
- [ ] Learner, teacher, assessor, specialist, transition, tool, material, and displaced-work burdens are visible where they change the comparison.
- [ ] Supported, independent, changed-condition, transfer, and retention claims remain distinct, and unobserved gains remain hypotheses.
