- [ ] Any dominance wording is bounded to the candidates, coordinates, horizon, conditions, and evidence actually compared.
- [ ] The result contains a usable disposition and smallest reopen condition but no final choice, authorization, launch, provider-operation, or effectiveness claim.

### HCD.2:8 - Common Anti-Patterns and How to Avoid Them

| Anti-pattern | Why it fails | Repair |
| --- | --- | --- |
| “Give everyone programme C” | Detail and a broad audience description do not establish personal fit. | Keep C as one design candidate; obtain same-holder inputs before personal comparison. |
| “This programme covers more topics” | Topic count says nothing about task closure, displacement, support, or evidence. | Compare contributions to complete later-Work results and protected errors. |
| “Scale 340 hours to 204” | Uniform scaling can remove preparation, attempts, feedback, or correction that makes the output plausible. | Name every removed or replaced hour and show how the required mechanisms and outputs remain available. |
| “Teacher hours make it feasible” | A time calculation does not establish competence, access, scheduling, or operation at peak load. | Obtain the HCD.7 support result or return the exact missing contribution; use HCD.8 only when it must be built. |
| “AI support means the person knows it” | Assisted performance can differ from later unassisted performance. | Allocate the human checking contribution and preserve actual help in every observation. |
| “Every component is a prerequisite” | A printed sequence turns legitimate same-task references and specialists into unnecessary memorization or gates. | Separate before-practice prerequisites from simultaneous complements and supports. |
| “One feasible candidate dominates” | Missing rivals or evidence do not create a supported dominance relation. | Return the feasible description and the exact comparison gap. |
| “HCD.2 chooses and launches” | Comparison is collapsed with preference, authority, commitment, and execution. | Pass the comparison to the applicable decision Method and authorized owner. |
| “The course author is the learner” | Recipient and holder are silently merged. | Return a class-level design question now and reopen personal comparison when a named learner supplies compatible evidence. |

### HCD.2:9 - Consequences

The practitioner gains a comparison that connects development activities to complete later Work, exposes external support before commitment, and preserves uncertainty without becoming silent. A small comparable fragment can be enough to improve a decision, so the method does not require building several complete courses first.

The cost is explicit composition work. Human and provider time, access, transitions, retries, displaced practice, and later evidence can make an attractive programme infeasible. Some requests return to learning-product design, provider enablement, target-domain owners, or a separate target decision rather than yielding a personal recommendation.

### HCD.2:10 - Rationale

Development is configuration-relative. What one programme adds depends on what this person can already contribute, what support is actually available, which complete tasks matter, and which protected errors cannot be traded away. Starting from an empty curriculum exaggerates every addition; starting from a role average fabricates the holder.

Whole tasks reveal coordination and applicability, while claim-sized contribution allocation prevents the whole-task label from hiding references, AI, specialists, or prerequisites. Comparing finite changes keeps resources and displaced work visible. Separating comparison from choice protects preference, authority, commitment, launch, and evidence of effect as their own questions.

### HCD.2:11 - SoTA-Echoing

The working question is: **How should a practitioner compose and compare a development programme for one person's consequential later Work without reducing it to topics or promising an unobserved effect?**

The selected best-known line combines sufficiently whole complex-task practice with expertise-sensitive support, explicit correction and retesting where those mechanisms fit, and configuration-relative finite comparison. The maintained [4C/ID model](https://www.4cid.org/about/) and its [fourth-edition presentation](https://www.4cid.org/readings/books/) supply the serious complex-task design candidate: whole learning tasks, support for non-routine reasoning, timely procedural information, and additional practice for recurrent parts. Paas and van Merriënboer's 2020 review ([`COG-05`](https://doi.org/10.1177/0963721420922183)) supports changing guidance with prior knowledge and task complexity. Winget and Persky's 2022 review ([`COG-06`](https://doi.org/10.5688/ajpe8906)) supplies the bounded formative-assessment, corrective-activity, retest, and threshold arrangement. HCD.2 adapts these contributions into §§4.2–4.3; none establishes later professional performance or the effect of a local programme.

The serious ordinary alternative is a syllabus-first or branded-programme comparison: list content and hours, match them to a role profile, then prefer the most complete or familiar package. It is cheaper to describe, but it hides complete-task closure, same-holder starting evidence, contribution allocations, provider dependence, displaced work, and unequal detail. HCD.2 rejects topic count and programme labels as the comparison basis, while retaining a syllabus as one carrier of a candidate once its contribution is made explicit.

`C.11.CRC` supplies the internal finite-comparison grammar used in §4.4. The HCD evidence chain keeps intended instruction, supported attempts, independent performance, transfer, and retention in their direct pattern owners; HCD.2 carries only the expectations and evidence needs used by the comparison. Bastani and colleagues' 2025 school-mathematics trial ([`AI-04`](https://doi.org/10.1073/pnas.2422633122)) is counterexample evidence that improved AI-assisted practice can fail to improve later unassisted performance; its population and task do not supply an adult workplace effect size or a universal AI prohibition. These limits produce the separate evidence states in §§4.3–4.6 rather than a promised causal programme effect.

This comparison adds design and evidence work, but it is non-dominated for the stated question because it preserves complete-task usefulness, personal fit, support dependence, finite burden, and uncertainty that the cheaper syllabus-first alternative loses. Reopen the selected line if a stronger source changes the complex-task, support, correction, AI-dependence, or transfer boundary, or if representative use shows that the same decision values can be preserved with materially less effort.

### HCD.2:12 - Relations

- **`HCD.1`:** can supply the compatible human capability-demand account and later-Work boundary; programme composition does not alter that demand.
- **`HCD.3`:** can supply a qualified human target or non-training return when rival causes change the programme; diagnosis is not mandatory otherwise.
- **`HCD.4`:** can supply the current/target claim comparison, protected strengths, priorities, and trade-offs; a profile does not generate programme alternatives.
- **`HCD.5`:** selects human-development Methods for the target contribution and evidence limits exposed by a candidate.
- **`HCD.6`:** designs representative practice tasks, permitted help, variations, and result criteria needed by the selected Method.
- **`HCD.7`:** establishes which provider, source, tool, AI, access, and human-check contributions are actually available.
- **`HCD.8`:** builds and tests a missing development arrangement only when an unavailable contribution must be created.
- **`HCD.9` and `HCD.10`:** carry out focused practice and change its variation, spacing, interleaving, challenge, or help while preserving attempt history.
- **`HCD.11`–`HCD.13`:** provide compatible observed performance, transfer, retention, and support-dependence evidence; the programme description does not manufacture those observations.
- **`HCD.14`:** revises only the programme assumptions and parts affected by compatible evidence.
- **`C.11.CRC`:** supplies the finite configuration-relative comparison claim. **`C.11`** separately supplies a bounded choice when its preference, belief, outcome, probe, and authority inputs exist.
- **PSD and other advice Methods:** may recommend a programme to a named recipient from qualified inputs; advice does not compose the missing programme or make the choice for its owner.
- **Learning-product and target-domain practices:** own a class-level product, its concrete sequence and materials, and professional task correctness. HCD.2 returns their exact question when personal or domain inputs are absent.
- **Provider, clinical, safety, legal, employment, specialist, organization, and release owners:** retain their direct competence, participation, authority, and consequence decisions.

### HCD.2:End

<a id="hcd-5"></a>
## HCD.5 - Select Capability-Development Methods

> **Type:** Human Capability Development practitioner method-selection pattern
> **Status:** Candidate

### HCD.5:0 - Use This When

Use this pattern when a named person's development target is sufficiently qualified, an initial or recent attempt is available, and different ways of developing the contribution could lead to different practice or support. Begin with the exact action that must change and the conditions of the observed attempt; do not begin with a favourite pedagogy, a deficit label, or the instruction to practise more.

The first useful result is a **Method-selection account**: a justified set of capability-development Methods, the target contribution and proposed mechanism for each, their task and learner fit, required support, evidence limits, burden and risk, surviving alternatives, and the observation that would change the selection. The result can contain one Method or a small coordinated set. It does not establish target engagement, learning, transfer, or effectiveness.

The practical gain is discrimination. A person who calculates correctly but accepts an unsupported cause receives a different development proposal from someone who cannot perform the calculation, cannot recognize when the Method applies, or lacks the data and permission to act. An external constraint is returned to its owner instead of being disguised as a need for more training.

Do not use HCD.5 to diagnose a disorder or human limitation, design the full practice task, assign a provider, launch an intervention, or infer development from participation. Use HCD.3 when rival causes still change the human target. Use HCD.6 to turn a selected Method into representative tasks, HCD.7–HCD.8 for support and provider arrangements, and HCD.9–HCD.14 for enactment and evidence.

### HCD.5:0.1 - Working Terms

| Name in this pattern | What it denotes |
| --- | --- |
| development target | The smallest supported change in the person's contribution that the proposed development should address under the named conditions. A score, trait label, activity, or programme topic is not the target by itself. |
| starting attempt | A condition-qualified observation of the person's relevant action before the selected development. Record the task, help received, work product, result, and important errors rather than only a score. |
| capability-development Method | A repeatable way of arranging human action, task, information, feedback, support, and correction to pursue a named development target. Its description guides action; it does not establish that the Method was enacted or effective. |
| proposed mechanism | The reason the Method is expected to change the target, stated as a testable local claim rather than a technique label. Causal effect remains unestablished until compatible evidence supports it. |
| target engagement | An observed change in the selected target under the stated conditions. It is not the same as attendance, satisfaction, assisted task success, or a theory label. |
| Method set | One Method or a small combination whose members make distinct necessary contributions to the same bounded target or task. A catalogue of plausible techniques is not a selected set. |
| surviving alternative | A serious alternative that remains compatible with the current evidence and would change the development action or evidence plan. |
| non-training return | A return naming the external constraint, owner, blocked HCD decision, safe interim boundary, and retry condition when a human-development Method is not yet the supported next move. |

The target, selected Method, Method description, provider, enacted development Work, observed target engagement, and later capability claim remain different objects. HCD.5 selects the Method and records the description and conditions needed by the next design use; later evidence determines what was enacted and what changed.

### HCD.5:1 - Problem Frame

The same poor result can arise from different action failures. A person may lack a usable explanation, make a recurrent calculation error, fail to distinguish situations that call for different Methods, or combine individually correct steps badly in a whole task. The same visible failure may instead come from missing data, inaccessible sources, absent permission, an unsuitable tool, an unavailable specialist, or an unsafe workload.

Development Methods act differently. A worked example can expose reasoning that unsupported problem solving hides, but unchanged guidance can obstruct an experienced learner. Part practice can stabilize a recurrent operation, but it cannot teach when to use it. Whole tasks can expose coordination, but they can overload a novice when a true prerequisite is missing. Feedback can change the next attempt only when its information identifies an actionable difference.

Method selection therefore has to connect one qualified target and starting attempt to a plausible change mechanism, while preserving the task, learner, support, evidence, burden, and risk conditions under which that mechanism might help.

### HCD.5:2 - Problem

The common shortcut is treatment by label: “deliberate practice,” “coaching,” “mastery,” “retrieval,” “interleaving,” “motivation,” or “AI tutor” is prescribed because the term sounds relevant. The label hides what the person must do differently and which observation would show that the proposed target changed.

Another shortcut chooses the activity that is easiest to provide. A lecture substitutes for missing project access, repeated calculation substitutes for causal discrimination, or a score substitutes for feedback. The intervention can be delivered successfully while the limiting contribution remains untouched.

The opposite failure is an exhaustive menu with no selection. The practitioner needs a small justified Method set, one serious alternative, and a precise return when the next step is not human development.

### HCD.5:3 - Forces

- A familiar technique is easy to commission, while its mechanism may not match the observed action or later-Work target.
- Whole-task practice preserves coordination, while a true prerequisite or recurrent suboperation may need focused preparation.
- Guidance can help a novice begin, while the same guidance can become redundant or answer-bearing as knowledge grows.
- Repetition supplies more attempts, while repeated practice history alone does not establish causality or a universal hours threshold.
- Feedback is widely available, while its information content, timing, receiver, and opportunity for correction determine what it can change.
- A self-regulation plan can support an already endorsed goal, while goal enactment is not the same as capability or transfer.
- Supported performance can make practice possible, while it can hide the human contribution the target requires.
- A small source effect can justify a candidate mechanism, while population, task, measure, horizon, and provider differences limit transfer to this person.
- Human development can be useful, while access, authority, environment, provider, safety, clinical, or target-domain problems may own the next action.

### HCD.5:4 - Solution

Recover the target action and its starting conditions, compare a small set of Methods that propose materially different change mechanisms, and select only the Method or combination whose task fit, learner fit, support, burden, risk, and evidence plan remain credible. Return external constraints before prescribing development.

#### HCD.5:4.1 - Bind the Selection Use

Name:

1. the exact human holder, participation basis, receiving decision, and decision owner;
2. the later-Work contribution, configuration, horizon, protected result and critical-error conditions;
3. the HCD.3 target or another qualified basis that states the action to change and any still-plausible rival;
4. the starting attempt, including the task, permitted and received help, work product, result, error pattern, uncertainty, and evidence window;
5. current strengths, prior relevant practice, available time, access, tools, AI posture, provider contribution, environment, privacy, safety, and other protected conditions; and
6. the observation that the receiving decision would use after the Method is applied.

If the human target is not supported and rival causes would change the next action, return to HCD.3. If the target-domain Method, correctness criterion, access, permission, tool, specialist result, or safe participation condition is missing, issue a non-training return to its direct owner. Do not make the missing value into a motivation, practice, or knowledge deficit.

#### HCD.5:4.2 - Generate a Small Candidate Set by Mechanism

Use the observed action difference to generate serious candidates. The following cues are questions, not an exhaustive intervention classification:

| Observed difference | Candidate Method question | Boundary to preserve |
| --- | --- | --- |
| the person lacks a usable representation or cannot follow expert reasoning | Would an explanation, demonstration, integrated worked example, or prompted self-explanation expose the decision-bearing relation? | Guidance should fit prior knowledge and later be reduced or changed; understanding the example is not independent performance. |
| the person performs an operation when named but does not recognize when it applies | Would discrimination practice across contrasting situations improve recognition and Method selection? | Repeating execution with the Method name supplied cannot test this target. |
| a recurrent component is slow or error-prone and constrains the whole task | Would focused part practice with immediate correction stabilize that component? | The component must contribute to the later task; part success does not establish whole-task coordination. |
| separate contributions are adequate but their coordination fails | Would sufficiently whole-task practice with bounded support expose the interfaces and stopping conditions? | A true prerequisite may still need preparation; task realism does not excuse unsafe or uninterpretable complexity. |
| the next attempt repeats a known, actionable difference | What information-specific feedback, corrective activity, and retest would let the person change that action? | Praise, a score, or another attempt is not enough when it does not identify the difference to change. |
| the person has an endorsed goal and feasible action but misses a stable cue | Would a bounded if–then plan or environmental cue support enactment? | Goal attainment is not capability, and a plan cannot repair an unavailable or unsafe action. |
| competent participation in complex Work is needed | Would demonstration, coaching, guided participation, or supervised workplace practice provide the missing contribution? | The provider, task, access, supervision, and consequence boundary must be real; the label “apprenticeship” establishes none of them. |
| the observed limit disappears when data, permission, workload, tool, or support is restored | Is the supported next move an access or arrangement change rather than a development Method? | Return the condition to HCD.7, HCD.8, or its direct owner and preserve any remaining human target separately. |

Spacing, retrieval, interleaving, cue focus, support fading, and other practice features may refine a candidate when their mechanism matches the target. Their names do not select the Method. HCD.10 chooses the condition-sensitive practice change after the governing Method and observations are present.

#### HCD.5:4.3 - Compare Method Fit and Evidence

For each serious candidate, record:

| Selection position | Required content |
| --- | --- |
| target contribution | The exact human action or judgement intended to change and its role in later Work. |
| proposed mechanism | How the Method is expected to change that target, what source or prior observation supports the expectation, and which rival explanation remains live. |
| task and learner fit | Starting performance, prior knowledge, task complexity, similarity, support dependence, and the reason this Method fits better than the serious alternative. |
| enacted form | The minimally sufficient explanation, demonstration, practice, feedback, correction, plan, coaching, or support change; identify a qualified professional description when local design would be unsafe or under-specified. |
| provider and access need | Competence, time, data, tools, AI, environment, supervision, permission, privacy, and peak-load conditions required for the Method. |
| burden and risk | Learner and provider effort, displaced Work, error exposure, overload, autonomy, fatigue, clinical or safety risk, and the cost of another attempt or probe. |
| evidence plan | Starting observation, target-engagement observation, independent or changed-condition attempt where relevant, later-Work evidence needed for the stronger claim, and contamination or measurement limits. |
| alternatives and stop | Surviving alternative, rejected candidate with reason, condition for stopping or returning, and smallest change that reopens selection. |

Compare candidates at comparable enactment effort. A richly specified Method does not defeat an unnamed alternative; make only the smallest alternative description needed to expose a decision-bearing difference. Preserve source limits instead of transporting one average effect, hours threshold, cue, sequence, or professional arrangement to a different task and population.

#### HCD.5:4.4 - Select a Coherent Method Set

Select the smallest set whose members make distinct contributions to the target. For a combination, state:

1. which target or task difference each member addresses;
2. whether it prepares a prerequisite, enables the first attempt, supplies feedback, supports correction, or tests changed performance;
3. which order is required by dependence and which order is merely convenient;
4. which help is allowed during each observation and what the person must do without an answer-bearing cue; and
5. when the set stops, changes, or returns because target engagement is absent, another limitation appears, or a provider condition fails.

Do not accumulate techniques as insurance. If two candidates propose the same contribution at higher burden, retain the lower-burden adequate candidate unless a material evidence, safety, participation, or future-use value justifies the difference. If methods address different supported targets, keep those targets and evidence plans distinct even when one programme coordinates their enactment.

#### HCD.5:4.5 - Record the Method-Selection Account

| Result position | Required content |
| --- | --- |
| selection boundary | Human holder, later-Work contribution, configuration, horizon, participation, receiving decision, decision owner, evidence window, and direct outside owners. |
| target and start | Qualified development target, starting attempt, important conditions, current strengths, error pattern, help received, uncertainty, and surviving rivals. |
| selected Method set | Each Method's action, proposed mechanism, intended target contribution, enacted form, required support, and stop condition. |
| comparison | Serious alternatives, comparable effort, task and learner fit, evidence, burden, risk, and reason for selection or rejection. |
| evidence plan | Target-engagement observation, permitted help, independent or changed-condition observation where material, stronger later-Work evidence, and inference limits. |
| disposition | `selected Method set`, `conditional design hypothesis for stated entry evidence`, `non-training return`, or `Method description or provider result blocked`. |
| continuation | The practice-task design, provider/access result, first enacted attempt, missing professional description, outside-owner question, or smallest condition that reopens selection. |

For a future audience, return only a conditional design hypothesis tied to the stated entry evidence and planned diagnostic branches. Do not turn an audience description into an individual prescription. When a competent professional Method or provider result is required and unavailable, name that result rather than writing a generic local substitute.

#### HCD.5:4.6 - Recognition and Assurance

**Recognition.** Use HCD.5 when the question is “What kind of development activity could change this person's specific action?” and a worked example, whole task, focused component practice, feedback loop, coaching arrangement, self-regulation aid, or support change would lead to different next Work.

**Assurance.** Stronger reliance needs a qualified target, condition-compatible starting evidence, a task- and learner-matched mechanism claim, source limits, credible provider and access conditions, material burden and risk, and an observation that can detect target engagement without conflating helped performance with learning. Clinical, safety, employment, legal, provider-competence, and target-domain correctness decisions require their direct owners.

#### HCD.5:4.7 - What Changes in Practice

A practitioner stops asking, “Which learning technique should we use?” and asks, “Which exact action must change, what Method could change it under these conditions, what serious alternative remains, and what observation would show that the target—not merely the activity or score—changed?” When the answer is access, permission, data, workload, or specialist input, the work leaves Method selection with a named return.

### HCD.5:5 - Archetypal Grounding

#### HCD.5:5.1 - Correct Calculation, Unsupported Cause

In a constructed pump case, flow remains 100 m³/h while measured power rises from 20 to 22.4 kW, so the calculated energy per unit volume rises by 12%. The person computes the ratio correctly but concludes that the controller change caused the increase. Current facts leave at least resistance, regulation efficiency, and measurement error as live explanations.

The target is not unit conversion or arithmetic. It is proposing competing explanations, stating a discriminating observation, and keeping the conclusion conditional. A selected Method set uses one worked explanation that makes the rival predictions visible, a coached discrimination attempt, and a later changed case in which the person must choose the next observation without the Method or answer supplied. Focused unit practice remains a surviving candidate only if a separate starting observation shows a unit error. Generic repetition of the original calculation is rejected because it does not address the target.

The selection account records the proposed reasoning mechanism, teacher feedback needed, safe stipulated data, changed-condition observation, and the limit that this exercise does not establish engineering capability or the causal effect of the Method.

#### HCD.5:5.2 - Execution with a Cue, Failure to Select

A person can apply a finite comparison correctly whenever `C.11.CRC` is named, yet chooses a local score when a new situation requires comparing complete configurations. More worked examples with the PatternID printed beside the task would rehearse execution while preserving the selection failure.

HCD.5 selects discrimination practice across cases in which a finite comparison, a sensitivity result, or a source check is appropriate. Early feedback names the decision-bearing cue; later tasks remove the answer-bearing Method label and change the configuration or horizon. The intended target-engagement observation is correct recognition and selection before execution. HCD.6 supplies the actual representative task set; HCD.9 records what happened in each attempt.

#### HCD.5:5.3 - Missing Project Access Is Not a Practice Dose

A participant can explain the target Method on a supplied case but cannot apply it in their work because the project data and permission for a small safe trial are absent. The missing access changes the next action before any learning-technique comparison. HCD.5 returns the exact data, permission, owner, blocked practice decision, and retry condition to HCD.7 or the direct project owner. It preserves any supported human target but does not prescribe a longer course or call non-participation low motivation.

#### HCD.5:5.4 - Future Audience Design Hypothesis

A learning-product author expects entrants to differ in unit work, causal discrimination, and Method recognition. HCD.5 may propose conditional branches: focused component preparation for an observed unit error, worked explanation and coached discrimination for an unsupported cause, and Method-selection practice for cue dependence. The author still needs entry tasks that distinguish those conditions. The branch is not a frequency claim about engineers or a prescription for any person not yet observed.

### HCD.5:6 - Bias-Annotation

Lenses: **Gov**, **Arch**, **Onto/Epist**, **Prag**, **Did**. Scope: selecting capability-development Methods for one qualified human target under stated task, participation, support, and evidence conditions.

| Recurring bias | Likely drift | Repair |
| --- | --- | --- |
| technique allegiance | One school or branded intervention becomes the default. | Compare proposed mechanisms and a serious alternative against the exact target and starting attempt. |
| activity bias | Attendance, repetition, coaching time, or content exposure substitutes for change in the target. | Name the target-engagement observation and stronger evidence boundary. |
| deficit bias | A tool, access, authority, workload, provider, or specialist failure becomes a human limitation. | Issue a non-training return while preserving any separately supported target. |
| score bias | A scalar selects an intervention without the underlying action or error. | Recover the task, work product, help, error pattern, and inference limit. |
| novice-default bias | Guidance is kept constant despite changing prior knowledge. | State the learner-fit condition and the rule for reducing, changing, or removing support. |
| dose bias | More hours or repetitions are presumed causal. | Specify the Method contribution and observation; keep practice history as evidence rather than mechanism proof. |
| feedback optimism | Any response from a teacher or tool is called useful feedback. | State the information that identifies the action to change and the opportunity for correction. |
| motivation rhetoric | A theory label explains non-enactment without target or context evidence. | Compare cue, feasibility, support, environment, and the observed target before selecting a self-regulation Method. |

### HCD.5:7 - Conformance Checklist

- [ ] One human holder, later-Work contribution, qualified target, starting attempt, participation basis, horizon, receiving decision, and decision owner are explicit.
- [ ] The starting attempt records task conditions, permitted and received help, work product, result, error pattern, current strengths, and uncertainty.
- [ ] External access, Method, tool, authority, provider, specialist, clinical, safety, or environment constraints are returned to their direct owners rather than renamed as training needs.
- [ ] Every serious candidate states a proposed mechanism, target contribution, task and learner fit, minimally sufficient enacted form, required support, burden, risk, and evidence plan.
- [ ] The comparison includes one serious alternative at comparable enactment effort and preserves source population, task, measure, horizon, support, and causal limits.
- [ ] Whole-task, part-practice, explanation, feedback, coaching, planning, and support candidates are selected by their contribution, not by label.
- [ ] A selected combination assigns one distinct necessary contribution to each member and states required versus convenient order.
- [ ] Intended help, permitted help, actual availability, received help, and independent action remain distinct.
- [ ] Target engagement is distinguished from attendance, satisfaction, assisted success, later capability, transfer, and retention.
- [ ] The result records surviving alternatives, rejected candidates with reasons, stop conditions, and the smallest change that reopens selection.
- [ ] A future-audience result is conditional on stated entry evidence and does not prescribe for an unobserved person.
- [ ] HCD.5 selects no provider, full task set, programme choice, intervention launch, clinical action, employment action, or effectiveness conclusion.

### HCD.5:8 - Common Anti-Patterns and How to Avoid Them

| Anti-pattern | Why it fails | Repair |
| --- | --- | --- |
| “Use deliberate practice” | The label omits the target, task, feedback, refinement, provider, and evidence limits; accumulated practice is not a universal cause. | Specify the proposed mechanism and enacted form for this target, or choose another Method. |
| “They need more examples” | Examples may improve representation or preserve cue dependence; the action difference is unknown. | Compare worked explanation, discrimination practice, and independent selection against the observed error. |
| “The score is low, so reteach” | A score can mix arithmetic, recognition, support, access, and measurement error. | Recover the claim-sized starting attempt and live rivals first. |
| “Whole tasks are always best” | Missing prerequisites, overload, or safety conditions can make a whole task unusable. | Prepare the true prerequisite and retain sufficiently whole practice for the contributions that must combine. |
| “Remove all help” | Abrupt withdrawal can test the wrong thing or make practice unsafe. | Select help and its change by target, current knowledge, task, and intended observation. |
| “Feedback works” | A score, praise, or generic comment may not identify the next action. | Specify information content, receiver, correction opportunity, and retest. |
| “Write an if–then plan for any gap” | A plan cannot create capability, access, authority, or an unendorsed goal. | Use planning only for a supported enactment target with detectable cue and feasible response. |
| “The missing project proves low motivation” | No human inference follows from unavailable data or permission. | Return the access condition and retry predicate to its owner. |
| “Select every plausible technique” | The list hides contribution, sequence, burden, and stop conditions. | Keep the smallest coherent Method set and one serious surviving alternative. |

### HCD.5:9 - Consequences

The practitioner gains a reasoned bridge from a qualified target to a practice design and evidence plan. Different error patterns receive different candidate mechanisms, and a provider or access failure can stop unhelpful development before time is committed.

The cost is more precise starting evidence and explicit comparison. A selected Method may require a competent provider, safe task, additional observation, or professional description that is not yet available. Some familiar interventions will remain only conditional hypotheses, and absence of target engagement can force reselection.

### HCD.5:10 - Rationale

A development activity is useful only through the contribution it changes for the receiving later-Work use. Technique names and average effects compress away the holder, task, starting action, support, and evidence boundary that determine local fit.

Mechanism comparison keeps the proposal generative without claiming causality in advance. The target-engagement observation makes failure informative: it can challenge the selected Method before a poor later result is blamed on the person. A small Method set preserves complementarity while preventing a catalogue from masquerading as a choice.

### HCD.5:11 - SoTA-Echoing

The working question is: **How should a practitioner select a human-development Method that fits one qualified target and starting attempt rather than merely choosing a familiar intervention label?**

The selected best-known line is target-, task-, learner-, and evidence-sensitive Method selection. Paas and van Merriënboer's 2020 review ([`COG-05`](https://doi.org/10.1177/0963721420922183)) supports integrated information and worked examples for avoidable novice load while requiring guidance to change with prior knowledge. Wisniewski, Zierer, and Hattie's 2020 meta-analysis ([`MOT-05`](https://doi.org/10.3389/fpsyg.2019.03087)) shows why feedback must be selected by information content, task, receiver, and opportunity to use it rather than by the claim that “feedback works.” Sheeran and colleagues' 2020 meta-analysis ([`MOT-01`](https://doi.org/10.1037/ccp0000501)) contributes the need to observe engagement of the proposed target; its health-behaviour population and small average effect do not establish professional capability transfer.

The serious ordinary alternative is intervention by family label: prescribe deliberate practice, mastery, coaching, motivation work, retrieval, interleaving, or AI tutoring from a broad diagnosis or a low score. It is cheaper to commission, but it cannot explain why the selected activity should change this action rather than a rival limitation. HCD.5 adapts the source line into the bound selection positions in §§4.1–4.5 and rejects a universal technique or dose.

Macnamara, Hambrick, and Oswald's 2014 meta-analysis ([`BEH-02`](https://doi.org/10.1177/0956797614535810)) is limit evidence: observed deliberate-practice histories account for different and incomplete shares of performance variance and do not establish a universal hours threshold or causal intervention. Sheeran and colleagues' 2024 planning synthesis ([`MOT-03`](https://doi.org/10.1080/10463283.2024.2334563)) supplies a bounded if–then planning candidate only when a goal is endorsed, a cue is detectable, and an action is feasible. Carpenter, Pan, and Butler's 2022 review ([`COG-01`](https://doi.org/10.1038/s44159-022-00089-1)) supports spacing and retrieval as candidates for durable access under source-specific conditions, not as a complete curriculum or default Method for every target.

The selected line costs more than matching labels to a catalogue, but it preserves target fit, external returns, provider needs, risks, and an observation capable of changing the selection. Reopen it when stronger source evidence changes a relied-on mechanism or limit, when target engagement contradicts the expected action, when the task or learner conditions change, or when a lower-effort selection practice preserves the same decision-bearing values.

### HCD.5:12 - Relations

- **`HCD.1`:** can supply the later-Work demand and protected contribution boundary.
- **`HCD.2`:** can expose programme components that need Method selection; HCD.5 does not choose the programme.
- **`HCD.3`:** supplies a qualified human target or non-training return when rival causes matter.
- **`HCD.4`:** can supply current strengths, priority characteristics, trade-offs, and condition-qualified profile evidence.
- **`HCD.6`:** turns the selected Method into representative tasks, permitted help, variations, and result criteria.
- **`HCD.7`:** establishes whether the required people, sources, tools, AI, access, environment, and checking contributions are available.
- **`HCD.8`:** builds a missing development arrangement only after the required provider or environment result is explicit.
- **`HCD.9` and `HCD.10`:** enact the selected focused practice and adjust its variation, spacing, interleaving, progression, or help from observed attempts.
- **`HCD.11`–`HCD.13`:** supply stronger same-holder performance, transfer, retention, and support-dependence evidence; HCD.5's mechanism claim does not substitute for those results.
- **`HCD.14`:** may revise the selected Method when compatible evidence changes the target, mechanism, fit, support, or result expectation.
- **Target-domain, clinical, safety, legal, employment, privacy, organization, provider, and specialist practices:** retain correctness, risk, participation, authority, and competence decisions.
- **Learning-product practices:** may enact a selected explanation, task, feedback interaction, plan, or schedule for a declared audience; the product does not make the Method effective for a person by publication.

### HCD.5:End

<a id="hcd-6"></a>
## HCD.6 - Design Representative Practice Tasks

> **Type:** Human Capability Development practitioner practice-task design pattern
> **Status:** Candidate

### HCD.6:0 - Use This When

Use this pattern when a named human contribution and a capability-development Method have been selected, but the person still needs a task in which to recognize the situation, act, obtain an inspectable result, receive feedback, and try again under a meaningful change. Begin with the target action and target-domain truth; do not begin by changing names, adding difficulty, or copying a production task without a learning purpose.

The first useful result is a **representative practice set**: a sufficiently whole task family with a worked demonstration or explanation where useful, a first attempt, a materially varied attempt, target-domain success and critical-error criteria, permitted help, feedback and correction opportunities, and progression or return conditions. The set states what each task is intended to expose. It does not establish that the tasks were performed or that capability changed.

The practical gain is an interpretable attempt. A practitioner can tell whether the person failed to calculate, to recognize the applicable Method, to challenge an unsupported source, to coordinate a specialist contribution, or to act when the supplied evidence is sufficient. A warranted refusal and a supported continuation can both be correct in different cases.

Do not use HCD.6 to invent target-domain correctness, turn a worked answer into learner evidence, schedule all repetitions, provide unavailable teachers or tools, or assess later capability. HCD.5 supplies the selected development Method; HCD.7–HCD.8 supply needed support; HCD.9–HCD.10 enact and vary practice; HCD.11–HCD.13 interpret performance, transfer, and retention.

### HCD.6:0.1 - Working Terms

| Name in this pattern | What it denotes |
| --- | --- |
| target human action | The recognition, judgement, operation, coordination, or result-producing contribution the person is intended to practise under stated conditions. |
| representative practice task | A bounded task that preserves the later-Work relations needed to elicit the target action and interpret its result. Representation is relative to the contribution and claim; visual realism or a workplace label is not enough. |
| sufficiently whole task | A task containing the purpose, result recipient, decision-bearing conditions, required contributions, interfaces, and stopping or critical-error boundaries needed for the target action. It may omit detail that does not change that action. |
| decision-bearing condition | A fact whose value can change which Method applies, which action is warranted, or which result can be supported. |
| worked explanation | A demonstration that exposes the intermediate observations, comparisons, reasons, checks, and uncertainty needed for the target action rather than showing only the final answer. |
| answer-bearing cue | Information that supplies the Method choice, decisive relation, calculation, or conclusion the current attempt is meant to elicit from the person. A legitimate reference or tool is not answer-bearing merely because it helps. |
| permitted help | The references, tools, AI actions, prompts, peers, specialists, teacher moves, and other support allowed for one stated practice purpose. It remains distinct from help actually available and help actually received. |
| representative practice set | The task descriptions, conditions, materials, permitted help, criteria, variations, feedback opportunities, progression or return rules, and evidence-purpose limits needed for a coherent practice use. |

A task description, learning product, performed attempt, work product, feedback event, and capability claim remain different objects. HCD.6 designs the task set; later Methods establish what was provided, performed, observed, and inferred.

### HCD.6:1 - Problem Frame

A practice task can look realistic while testing the wrong contribution. A pump story can reduce to arithmetic when the target is causal discrimination. A ticket-queue story can repeat the same calculation even though the real difficulty is choosing the unit of account. An engineering release case can reward generic caution when a complete compatible report actually supports a bounded action.

Support changes the task. A worked example may help a novice see the reasoning, but leaving the same intermediate prompts in the next attempt can turn recognition into copying. Removing every source or tool can make the task unlike later Work and test memory that was never required. AI assistance can generate a plausible answer while hiding whether the person noticed a configuration mismatch.

The practitioner therefore has to preserve the relations that make the target action necessary, choose help for the current practice purpose, and vary a condition that changes the decision rather than the scenery.

### HCD.6:2 - Problem

The common shortcut is surface variation: change the industry, numbers, names, or diagram while leaving the same Method and answer obvious. Repetition may improve familiarity without showing whether the person can recognize applicability or adapt the Method.

The opposite shortcut maximizes authenticity. A full production case includes so many uncontrolled dependencies, missing inputs, risks, and specialist decisions that neither learner nor practitioner can tell which contribution changed. Complexity becomes noise rather than representation.

A third shortcut treats refusal as the safest universal answer. It can hide a real capability gap when sufficient evidence is present and action is required. The task set must include both missing-evidence and sufficient-evidence cases when the target contribution depends on that distinction.

### HCD.6:3 - Forces

- Whole tasks expose coordination and applicability, while irrelevant complexity can make the target action uninterpretable.
- Worked explanations can reveal reasoning, while answer-bearing cues can conceal recognition and selection.
- Legitimate references, AI, tools, and specialists belong to later Work, while their contributions must not be attributed to the person.
- Familiar cases lower entry burden, while meaningful transfer requires changes that affect the decision rather than only the story.
- Stable conditions help isolate one action, while varied conditions reveal whether the Method is selected and adapted.
- A correct refusal protects against unsupported action, while habitual caution is wrong when the supplied evidence supports continuation.
- Immediate correction supports another attempt, while feedback can teach, prime, fatigue, or otherwise contaminate later evidence.
- Target-domain realism improves relevance, while professional correctness and hazards must come from qualified domain sources or explicitly stipulated teaching data.
- A fixed challenge level is easy to schedule, while prior knowledge, task complexity, and support determine usable difficulty.

### HCD.6:4 - Solution

Design backward from the target human action and the later-Work result it supports. Build one sufficiently whole task, expose the relevant reasoning in a demonstration when useful, create an independent attempt with only permitted help, and vary decision-bearing conditions so that different warranted actions become observable.

#### HCD.6:4.1 - Bind the Practice Use and Domain Truth

Name:

1. the exact human holder or declared future-audience branch, participation basis, and practice recipient;
2. the later-Work family, target human action, selected HCD.5 Method, and intended practice result;
3. the target-domain source or stipulated teaching oracle that supplies correct facts, calculations, quality criteria, critical errors, hazards, and specialist boundaries;
4. current performance and prior knowledge relevant to task complexity and support;
5. permitted references, tools, AI, prompts, peers, teachers, specialists, time, environment, data, access, privacy, authority, and safety conditions; and
6. whether the current task is for explanation, first practice, correction, changed-condition practice, or a later observation.

If domain correctness, a necessary Method description, safe conditions, or a required provider contribution is absent, return the exact missing result. Do not repair the gap by simplifying the answer key, accepting generic caution, or treating fluent output as correct. For a future audience, keep task branches conditional on the entry evidence they are meant to distinguish.

#### HCD.6:4.2 - Build a Sufficiently Whole Task

1. **Give the task a real purpose.** State the situation, result recipient, decision or operation, intended work product, and what happens next when the result is acceptable.
2. **Include the relations that elicit the target action.** Supply the facts, comparisons, interfaces, alternative explanations, configuration identifiers, constraints, or specialist-return conditions that make the selected Method relevant.
3. **Make the human contribution visible.** State what the person must notice, choose, calculate, explain, adapt, coordinate, check, or return. Preserve what references, AI, tools, peers, and specialists may contribute.
4. **Declare success and critical error before the attempt.** Use target-domain criteria. Include warranted uncertainty and stopping conditions; a polished answer cannot compensate for a critical mismatch.
5. **Remove irrelevant burden.** Omit production detail that changes neither the target action nor its interpretation. When realism introduces another independently limiting contribution, prepare it, support it, or split the practice purpose.
6. **Keep the result feasible.** Fit the case, feedback, correction, and retry inside the declared learner and provider resources. A case too large for the available observation window returns for redesign rather than silently extending the programme.

#### HCD.6:4.3 - Separate Demonstration, First Attempt, and Help

A demonstration or worked explanation should expose the intermediate action the learner will later need: the cue noticed, Method selected, competing alternatives, calculation or transformation, check, remaining uncertainty, and next move. It should not rely on a final answer whose reasoning remains hidden.

For the first attempt, change the surface particulars and at least one condition needed to elicit the target action. Remove the Method name, recommended option, decisive calculation, or next control move when that value is the target. Retain references, calculators, AI, specialist returns, or other supports that later Work legitimately allows, while stating the human check that remains required.

Record permitted help before the attempt. Intended help in a programme, available help in the environment, and help actually received are separate. If a teacher or AI supplies an answer-bearing cue, keep the attempt as supported practice and do not reuse it as evidence of independent recognition.

#### HCD.6:4.4 - Design Decision-Bearing Variations

Choose variations from the relation the person must discriminate:

- change a measurement or its uncertainty when competing explanations predict different observations;
- change the unit of account when duplicate events, batching, or aggregation alters the workload calculation;
- change a configuration identifier when evidence is valid for one configuration but not another;
- supply a complete compatible report after an earlier missing-evidence case so the learner must act rather than repeat a refusal;
- change available time, support, task similarity, or authority when those values change the warranted continuation; and
- change the cue that signals which Method applies when recognition and selection are the target.

Do not require every type of variation. Select the smallest change that can distinguish the target action from repetition, answer recall, or generic caution. HCD.10 later chooses how variation, spacing, interleaving, progression, or support change should be distributed across practice episodes.

#### HCD.6:4.5 - Provide Feedback and Correction Opportunities

Link feedback to the exact criterion or action difference. State what remains correct, what must change, why it matters to the result, and what the learner should inspect or try next. A score or praise can summarize but cannot replace actionable information.

The correction task should preserve the affected requirement while changing enough of the case to prevent answer replay. Recheck only the affected claims unless the changed response gives reason to reopen others. If feedback revealed a different limiting target, return to HCD.5 or HCD.3 rather than adding repetitions under the old explanation.

Practice feedback and later assessment serve different uses. HCD.9 records the feedback and next attempt as practice history. HCD.11 decides what a condition-qualified performance attempt can support, including the effect of help and contamination.

#### HCD.6:4.6 - Record the Representative Practice Set

| Result position | Required content |
| --- | --- |
| practice boundary | Human holder or future-audience branch, later-Work family, target action, selected Method, practice purpose, participation, conditions, horizon, and receiving use. |
| domain basis | Qualified source or stipulated oracle, required result, correct facts, quality criteria, critical errors, hazards, and outside-owner boundaries. |
| task family | Demonstration where used, first attempt, decision-bearing variations, correction task, and the relation each task is meant to expose. |
| human and support allocation | Human contribution, permitted references, tools, AI, prompts, peers, teachers, specialists, authority, and the required human check. |
| materials and work products | Data, descriptions, models, reports, calculations, decisions, explanations, or other artifacts needed and returned by each task. |
| criteria and feedback opportunity | Success, warranted uncertainty, critical error, stop conditions, feedback source and content, and retry condition. |
| progression or return | What observation permits less help, greater complexity, another variation, or later assessment; what returns to Method selection, diagnosis, support, domain truth, or task redesign. |
| evidence-purpose limit | Which attempt is demonstration, supported practice, independent practice, correction, or later observation, and which stronger learning, transfer, retention, or effectiveness claim remains unestablished. |

The result is complete when another competent practitioner can present the intended task and help, identify the target action in the work product, apply the domain criteria, and choose the stated feedback, correction, progression, or return without reconstructing the design from private notes.

#### HCD.6:4.7 - Recognition and Assurance

**Recognition.** Use HCD.6 when practice feels busy or realistic but the practitioner cannot say which human action the task elicits, which condition changes the correct move, or what the learner must do without an answer-bearing cue.

**Assurance.** Stronger reliance needs a qualified target-domain oracle, safe and accessible conditions, explicit support allocation, criteria that discriminate warranted action from critical error, and a task relation suitable for the intended evidence claim. Domain experts retain correctness and hazard decisions; performed-attempt and capability claims require their own evidence.

#### HCD.6:4.8 - What Changes in Practice

A practitioner stops asking, “Can we make another realistic exercise?” and asks, “Which relation must this person notice and act on, what help is legitimate, which condition would change the warranted answer, and what result will let us give focused feedback?” The next case can require a justified refusal, a supported continuation, or a different Method rather than the same answer in new scenery.

### HCD.6:5 - Archetypal Grounding

#### HCD.6:5.1 - Pump Explanation and a Pressure Change

The stipulated demonstration begins with a pump delivering 100 m³/h at 400 kPa and 20 kW. After a controller change, measured flow remains 100 m³/h and power becomes 22.4 kW, while comparable pressure data are absent. The worked explanation calculates the 12% rise in energy per volume, keeps resistance, regulation efficiency, and measurement error as rivals, and selects observations that would distinguish them.

In the first attempt, independent measurements give 22.3 kW and 99.5 m³/h with stated uncertainty. The person must compare like quantities, revise the measurement-error explanation, and choose a feasible pressure observation with the remaining specialist time. A later condition near 448 kPa supports increased resistance under the stipulated model; a value near 400 kPa directs attention toward regulation efficiency. Neither observation establishes a unique cause by itself.

The target action is conditional explanation and choice of a discriminating observation, not arithmetic alone. A renamed machine with the same numbers would add no useful variation. A generic “collect more data” answer also fails because the case supplies a resource, rival predictions, and a decision recipient.

#### HCD.6:5.2 - Ticket Load after Duplicate Removal

Four people each have six active-processing hours per day. Incoming records rise from 50 to 80 and each distinct request takes 18 minutes. The initial worked case shows that 50 and 80 distinct requests require 15 and 24 person-hours; the observed queue alone does not establish lower individual productivity.

In a stipulated variation, the 80 records include 20 duplicate messages attached to existing requests, leaving 60 distinct requests. The target action is to recognize that the unit of account changed before calculating 18 person-hours of active processing. A second variation with an unknown duplicate count supports a request for that count rather than a fabricated workload. Merely replacing the support team with a warehouse would not test this discrimination.

#### HCD.6:5.3 - FW7 Refusal and FW8 Continuation

The first engineering case supplies a vendor report for FW7 while the intended release configuration requires HW3/FW8/SCHED2. The correct action is to identify the mismatch and withhold that use; a correct financial calculation cannot repair incompatible evidence.

The varied case supplies a report for HW3/FW8/SCHED2, confirms mandatory checks, and leaves 24 test hours. Ten changes in three homogeneous batches require 23 hours. The person should propose the bounded ten-change use and preserve the continuing queue when demand is twelve. Repeating the earlier refusal is now wrong because compatible evidence is present. When available time falls to 22 hours, promising ten changes becomes unsupported again.

This task family makes evidence applicability and the resource calculation jointly visible. It tests a warranted refusal, a supported continuation, and a changed constraint rather than rewarding universal caution.

#### HCD.6:5.4 - AI-Supported Practice without Attribution Drift

AI may propose sources, calculations, alternatives, or draft wording in a practice task. The task description states which actions the person still performs: checking decisive references, configuration, assumptions and calculations; selecting the Method; explaining the result; and returning unsupported claims. A later attempt may remove AI for a narrow independent contribution while retaining supplied tables and a calculator. The supported and changed-support attempts remain separate in the practice history; neither is silently upgraded to transfer or retention evidence.

### HCD.6:6 - Bias-Annotation

Lenses: **Gov**, **Arch**, **Onto/Epist**, **Prag**, **Did**. Scope: representative practice-task design for a named human contribution or an explicitly conditional future-audience branch.

| Recurring bias | Likely drift | Repair |
| --- | --- | --- |
| surface-realism bias | A workplace story or visual detail is treated as representation. | Preserve the relations and conditions that elicit and discriminate the target action. |
| difficulty bias | More complexity is assumed to create more learning. | Remove irrelevant burden and vary only what changes the Method, action, or interpretation. |
| cue blindness | Method names, intermediate prompts, or AI output supply the answer being tested. | Declare permitted help and remove answer-bearing cues for the relevant attempt. |
| tool-removal bias | All references and tools are removed in the name of independence. | Retain legitimate later-Work supports and isolate only the human contribution the current task targets. |
| caution bias | Refusal is accepted in every uncertain case. | Include sufficient-evidence cases that require bounded action as well as genuine missing-evidence stops. |
| answer-key bias | The final answer is checked while the reasoning, source applicability, or critical error remains hidden. | Require the decision-bearing intermediate action and use domain criteria. |
| production-copy bias | A real case is copied without controlling risk, access, or confounding contributions. | Build a sufficiently whole bounded task and return professional facts to qualified owners. |
| assessment leakage | A supported or corrected practice attempt is reported as independent capability. | State each task's evidence purpose and preserve help and contamination history. |

### HCD.6:7 - Conformance Checklist

- [ ] The human holder or future-audience branch, later-Work family, target action, selected Method, practice purpose, and receiving use are explicit.
- [ ] A qualified target-domain source or stipulated teaching oracle supplies facts, calculations, result criteria, critical errors, hazards, and specialist boundaries.
- [ ] The task is sufficiently whole for the target action but omits complexity that changes neither action nor interpretation.
- [ ] The result recipient, decision or operation, required work product, and next move are visible.
- [ ] Human, reference, tool, AI, peer, teacher, specialist, environment, permission, and authority contributions remain distinct.
- [ ] Permitted help is stated before each attempt, and answer-bearing cues are removed only where the target requires it.
- [ ] A demonstration exposes intermediate reasoning rather than only the final answer.
- [ ] The first attempt and later variation differ on a decision-bearing condition, not merely on names or scenery.
- [ ] The set includes warranted refusal and supported continuation when that distinction belongs to the target.
- [ ] Feedback names the affected criterion and next action; correction changes enough of the case to prevent answer replay.
- [ ] Learner and provider resources include task, feedback, correction, and retry; an oversized case returns for redesign.
- [ ] The result states progression or return conditions and distinguishes demonstration, supported practice, independent practice, correction, transfer, retention, and effectiveness uses.

### HCD.6:8 - Common Anti-Patterns and How to Avoid Them

| Anti-pattern | Why it fails | Repair |
| --- | --- | --- |
| “Change the pump to a ticket queue” | New nouns can preserve the same cue and answer. | Change a relation or condition that alters the warranted Method or result. |
| “Use a real project” | Production realism can add uncontrolled risk, access limits, and other human or specialist contributions. | Bound the task around the target action and obtain qualified domain and safety inputs. |
| “Show the perfect answer, then ask for it again” | The second task may test recall of wording rather than recognition and adaptation. | Expose reasoning in the demonstration, then remove answer-bearing cues and change a decisive condition. |
| “No help means independent” | Removing legitimate references or tools can test a different contribution from later Work. | Specify the exact human contribution and retain compatible support conditions. |
| “Always refuse if evidence is uncertain” | Generic caution fails when sufficient compatible evidence supports bounded action. | Pair missing-evidence and sufficient-evidence variants with explicit criteria. |
| “The learner got it after feedback” | Corrected practice is contaminated by the feedback event. | Record help and test the affected action on a changed task before stronger inference. |
| “Harder is more representative” | Difficulty without target relevance raises burden and obscures the result. | Scale complexity to current knowledge and the action being elicited. |
| “The answer key defines professional truth” | An invented key can encode unsafe or wrong domain guidance. | Obtain a qualified domain source or label stipulated teaching facts and their limits. |

### HCD.6:9 - Consequences

The practitioner gains tasks that make a selected human action observable and correctable without pretending to be full production Work. Meaningful variations expose applicability, source compatibility, resource limits, and the difference between warranted refusal and supported continuation.

The cost is domain and provider work. Someone must qualify the facts and hazards, prepare cases, expose intermediate reasoning, review attempts, and maintain enough variation to prevent answer replay. A task may return unfinished when correctness, access, safe conditions, or feedback capacity is absent.

### HCD.6:10 - Rationale

A practice task is representative relative to the target contribution and intended evidence claim. It preserves the relations needed for that contribution and use, not every detail of the workplace. This permits bounded safe practice while protecting domain truth.

Separating demonstration, first attempt, correction, and changed-condition practice keeps help and contamination visible. Designing both refusal and continuation cases prevents a safety-oriented boundary from becoming inert caution. The resulting work products give HCD.9 a focused practice object and HCD.11 an interpretable attempt without making their later conclusions in advance.

### HCD.6:11 - SoTA-Echoing

The working question is: **How should a practitioner design practice tasks that elicit a target human contribution and support meaningful correction and variation without mistaking surface realism for representation?**

The selected best-known line combines sufficiently whole complex tasks, expertise-sensitive worked support, and decision-bearing variation. The maintained [4C/ID model](https://www.4cid.org/about/) and its [fourth-edition presentation](https://www.4cid.org/readings/books/) supply the serious whole-task design candidate: learning tasks, support for non-routine reasoning, timely procedural information, and additional practice for recurrent parts. Paas and van Merriënboer's 2020 review ([`COG-05`](https://doi.org/10.1177/0963721420922183)) supports worked examples and integrated information for novices while warning that unchanged guidance can become redundant as task knowledge grows. HCD.6 adapts these contributions into §§4.2–4.3 and the separation of whole-task and focused-part practice.

The serious ordinary alternative is a topic exercise or surface-isomorphic scenario: present the rule, vary the nouns and numbers, and score the final answer. It is cheaper to produce, but it can preserve answer-bearing cues, omit the later-Work purpose and interfaces, and reward either calculation without judgement or generic caution. HCD.6 rejects surface change as representation and adds the decision-bearing variation, human/support allocation, domain criteria, and correction task in §§4.1–4.6.

Brunmair and Richter's 2019 meta-analysis ([`COG-03`](https://doi.org/10.1037/bul0000209)) supplies limit evidence that interleaving varies by material and similarity; it does not prescribe one mixed task set. Czyż, Wójcik, and Solarská's 2024 motor-learning review ([`COG-04`](https://doi.org/10.3389/fpsyg.2024.1377122)) supports testing delayed transfer while its applied estimate and cross-domain reach remain weak. Bastani and colleagues' 2025 school-mathematics trial ([`AI-04`](https://doi.org/10.1073/pnas.2422633122)) supports separating assisted practice from a later unassisted attempt; it does not supply an adult engineering effect or a universal AI ban. These sources change the task contrasts and evidence-purpose boundary rather than selecting one universal schedule.

The selected line costs more domain and case-design effort than renaming exercises, but it preserves action interpretability, legitimate support, critical errors, and the opportunity to learn from a changed result. Reopen it when stronger sources alter the whole-task, worked-support, variation, AI-help, or transfer boundary, or when representative use shows a lower-effort task design that preserves the same decision-bearing relations and evidence value.

### HCD.6:12 - Relations

- **`HCD.1`, `HCD.3`, and `HCD.4`:** can supply the later-Work demand, qualified target, current evidence, protected strengths, and priority conditions.
- **`HCD.2`:** can expose a programme component and task closure needing elaboration; HCD.6 does not select the programme.
- **`HCD.5`:** supplies the selected development Method, proposed mechanism, learner/task fit, support need, and evidence plan.
- **`HCD.7`:** establishes whether the required provider, source, tool, AI, access, environment, and human-check contributions are available.
- **`HCD.8`:** builds a missing task-production, provider, assessment, or practice-environment capability only when the required result is explicit.
- **`HCD.9`:** enacts the tasks, feedback, correction, and retries while recording the practice history.
- **`HCD.10`:** selects variation, spacing, interleaving, progression, or support change across practice episodes; HCD.6 supplies the meaningful task conditions.
- **`HCD.11`–`HCD.13`:** interpret condition-qualified performance, transfer, retention, and tool dependence; a task design or answer key is not those observations.
- **`HCD.14`:** can revise the affected task, criterion, help, or progression condition from compatible evidence.
- **LG.05 and other learning-product practices:** can explain and package a selected task; pedagogical presentation does not select the target relation or establish professional correctness.
- **Target-domain, safety, clinical, legal, employment, provider, privacy, and specialist practices:** retain correctness, hazard, participation, authority, and consequence decisions.

### HCD.6:End

<a id="hcd-7"></a>
## HCD.7 - Arrange Providers, Access, Tools, and AI Support

> **Type:** Human Capability Development practitioner support-arrangement pattern
> **Status:** Candidate

### HCD.7:0 - Use This When

Use this pattern when a capability-development programme, Method, or practice set needs contributions from people, sources, tools, AI, an environment, or an organization, and the practitioner must establish what will actually be available. Begin with the concrete functions and their timing: explanation, task preparation, safe access, feedback, checking, independent assessment, specialist input, and fallback. Do not begin with a provider label or a total-hours budget.

The first useful result is an **enabled development arrangement**: each required contribution has an identified supplier, competent and authorized scope, access conditions, capacity at the required time, data and privacy boundary, human checks, fallback, and evidence of availability. If one of these values is missing, return the supported design and the exact unavailable contribution, timing, access condition, or provider result. A plausible design is not an enabled arrangement.

The practical gain is feasibility before participation. The practitioner can see whether feedback arrives before the next attempt, whether peak assessment demand fits the provider window, whether a peer contribution prepares rather than replaces competent assessment, and whether AI support leaves enough human time to check consequential output.

Do not use HCD.7 to create a missing provider capability, infer that a provider is competent from a job title, prove learning from access, or decide that a person is suitable for a class-level arrangement. Use HCD.8 when an unavailable contribution must be built and tested. Personal fit remains with the applicable demand, target, profile, programme, and participation decisions.

### HCD.7:0.1 - Working Terms

| Name in this pattern | What it denotes |
| --- | --- |
| required development contribution | One result or action that the selected programme, Method, or practice needs from a learner, teacher, assessor, specialist, peer, source, tool, AI system, environment, or organization. |
| provider function | The bounded contribution to be supplied, such as explaining, preparing a case, giving actionable feedback, checking a critical error, assessing independently, maintaining access, or supplying a specialist result. A role title does not define the function. |
| provider | The identified person, group, organization, service, tool, source, or other System relied upon for a stated contribution under stated conditions. Different contributions may have different providers. |
| access condition | A permission, credential, data boundary, physical or digital route, timing, environment, interface, or participation condition needed to obtain a contribution. |
| capacity window | The amount and timing of provider attention or system availability that can serve the required work, including concurrency and peak demand. A total can fit while a critical window fails. |
| human-check contribution | The human judgement, verification, explanation, authorization, or accountability that remains necessary when AI or another tool assists. It has its own competence and workload. |
| fallback | A separately feasible way to preserve the required result or return safely when the preferred provider, tool, data, or timing becomes unavailable. |
| enabled development arrangement | The contribution allocation plus established providers, access, capacity, constraints, checks, fallback, and evidence sufficient to begin the bounded development use. It does not establish that the use occurred or succeeded. |

Intended help in a programme, help permitted by a practice task, help actually available, and help received in an attempt are different facts. HCD.7 establishes actual availability; HCD.9 and HCD.11 preserve what was received and what the resulting attempt can support.

### HCD.7:1 - Problem Frame

A programme can describe excellent practice and still be impossible to run. The teacher may have enough annual hours but not enough time during final assessments. The relevant project data may be inaccessible. A specialist may be available only after the decision deadline. An AI tool may be permitted for public material but prohibited for the learner's project. A peer group may generate useful questions while lacking the competence or independence required for assessment.

Support is not one undifferentiated resource. Explanation, coaching, task preparation, feedback, technical access, domain checking, independent assessment, and specialist judgement have different competence, timing, privacy, and authority conditions. Replacing one with another can change both the learning activity and the evidence it yields.

The practitioner therefore has to allocate exact contributions, test actual availability and peak capacity, and preserve human checking and fallback rather than treating provider names, aggregate hours, or tool access as proof of an operating arrangement.

### HCD.7:2 - Problem

The common shortcut is provider-by-label: a teacher, mentor, community, apprenticeship, platform, or AI tutor is assumed to supply every support function. The label hides task competence, assessment independence, access, supervision, workload, and the conditions under which the contribution is legitimate.

A second shortcut divides a total by the number of learners. The resulting “hours per learner” can be useful for costing but says nothing about individual contact, concurrency, feedback latency, or a final-week peak. Shared meetings also do not supply additional individual checking merely because more people attend.

A third shortcut treats automated assistance as free capacity. Searching, drafting, or calculating can reduce one burden and create another: prompt preparation, source checking, error challenge, data protection, and fallback. The arrangement needs a contribution account that remains true under actual use.

### HCD.7:3 - Forces

- Aggregate capacity is easy to budget, while timing, concurrency, feedback deadlines, and peak load determine actual service.
- A provider title is easy to name, while competence and authority are contribution-specific.
- Peer participation can broaden feedback and reflection, while independent or high-consequence judgement may require another qualified assessor.
- AI can accelerate search, generation, calculation, or critique, while human checking and data governance consume real capacity.
- Supported performance can improve the immediate result, while it does not establish unaided capability or retained performance after support changes.
- One platform or community can host many functions, while access, facilitation, leadership, task expertise, and coordination remain distinct contributions.
- A standard arrangement supports class planning, while personal needs and participation conditions become known only for an identified learner.
- Redundancy improves resilience, while a nominal fallback that lacks competence, access, or time is not feasible.
- HCD can expose the required support, while employment, privacy, safety, legal, clinical, target-domain, and organizational owners retain their decisions.

### HCD.7:4 - Solution

Translate the selected programme, Method, and practice tasks into required contributions; assign each contribution to an identified provider or resource; and test competence, access, timing, capacity, constraints, human checking, and fallback before calling the arrangement enabled.

#### HCD.7:4.1 - Bind the Development Use

Name:

1. the human holder or defined learner class, participation basis, programme or practice recipient, and receiving decision;
2. the later-Work target, selected HCD.5 Method, HCD.6 practice set, required work products, protected errors, and intended evidence uses;
