3. the calendar, attempt and feedback timing, learner workload, provider windows, peak periods, and decision deadlines;
4. project, source, data, environment, tool, AI, privacy, confidentiality, accessibility, permission, safety, and authority conditions; and
5. every direct owner whose competence, result, or authorization the arrangement consumes.

For a class-level design, state the audience assumptions and capacity range. Do not infer that a particular person fits the arrangement. When that person becomes known, recheck their task, access, participation, support, and accommodation conditions rather than inheriting the class average.

#### HCD.7:4.2 - Allocate Contributions, Not Labels

Construct one contribution allocation:

| Allocation field | Required content |
| --- | --- |
| contribution and recipient | The explanation, demonstration, case, access, feedback, check, assessment, specialist result, coordination, or fallback needed; who or what uses it; and which task or result it enables. |
| provider and scope | Identified supplier, contribution-specific competence, authorization, independence where needed, and explicit non-responsibilities. |
| timing and service condition | Required date or interval, concurrency, response or feedback deadline, duration, handoff, and dependency on other contributions. |
| access and constraints | Data, source, project, environment, interface, accessibility, privacy, confidentiality, safety, legal, employment, or organizational conditions. |
| capacity | Preparation, delivery, checking, correction, coordination, fallback, and peak-load effort; competing uses and unavailable periods. |
| evidence and fallback | Evidence that the provider and access are available for this use; failure signal; feasible fallback or exact missing result and retry condition. |

One person or tool may fill several rows, but each row keeps its own competence, workload, and constraints. Several providers may jointly supply one result, but their interfaces and the person responsible for the final judgement remain explicit. A catalogue, contract, platform account, or calendar slot is evidence input; none establishes every row by itself.

#### HCD.7:4.3 - Test Competence, Access, Timing, and Capacity

For each contribution:

1. **Check competence and scope.** Obtain evidence suitable for the task and consequence. Familiarity with content, teaching ability, domain judgement, facilitation, and independent assessment are not interchangeable.
2. **Check authority and independence.** State who may provide feedback, access confidential data, change a project, judge a protected criterion, authorize a retry, or make the later decision. A helpful participant is not automatically the authorized assessor.
3. **Check actual access.** Verify source edition or data, credentials, project permission, environment, tool or model availability, interface, and accessibility during the required window.
4. **Check total and peak capacity.** Include preparation, learner contact, review, correction, coordination, disputes, fallback, and competing work. Test the busiest window and the promised feedback interval, not only the total.
5. **Check handoffs and fallback.** Make the next provider, due time, result form, and failure signal visible. A fallback is feasible only when its competence, access, capacity, and authority also fit.
6. **Return exact gaps.** If a contribution is unavailable, state the blocked practice or evidence use, current supported design, safe interim boundary, and retry condition. Use HCD.8 only when creating that capability is the selected response.

An enabled arrangement can be small: one learner, one task, a source, a teacher feedback window, an assessor, and a fallback. It need not establish a complete institutional service when the bounded use needs less.

#### HCD.7:4.4 - Arrange AI and Tool Support without Attribution Drift

For each AI or tool use, state:

- the exact permitted contribution, such as search, example generation, calculation, drafting, critique, translation, or accessibility support;
- the data and confidentiality boundary, model or tool condition when it changes risk, and prohibited uses;
- the human action that remains, including selection, source verification, assumption and calculation checks, error challenge, explanation, judgement, or authorization;
- time for prompting, checking, correction, escalation, and recording support actually received;
- a fallback for outage, unsafe output, incompatible data, or unacceptable uncertainty; and
- which practice or evidence uses require a changed-support or unassisted attempt.

AI access can improve equipped performance without establishing human learning. A no-AI observation answers only the bounded contribution and conditions it was designed to expose. HCD.10 selects a justified support change; HCD.11–HCD.13 interpret the resulting evidence.

#### HCD.7:4.5 - Keep Peer, Teacher, Assessor, and Specialist Work Distinct

Peers can expose alternative reasoning, rehearse feedback, notice a question, and help a learner prepare a revision. Those contributions can reduce avoidable teacher burden. They do not replace a teacher or assessor contribution whose result needs task competence, calibration, independence, authority, or a protected-error judgement.

A teacher may explain and coach while another assessor judges the independent result. A specialist may supply a bounded domain fact without teaching the whole task. An organization may enable project access without becoming the capability holder. State each contribution and handoff rather than turning all participants into one generic support role.

#### HCD.7:4.6 - Return the Arrangement or Exact Missing Contribution

An HCD.7 result contains:

| Result position | Required content |
| --- | --- |
| arrangement boundary | Human holder or learner class, programme or practice, later-Work target, participation, calendar, evidence uses, protected conditions, and receiving decision. |
| contribution allocation | Every required learner, reference, tool, AI, peer, teacher, assessor, specialist, environment, organization, and fallback contribution. |
| provider basis | Supplier, competence, scope, authority, independence, access, data boundary, timing, capacity, peak load, and evidence of availability for each contribution. |
| human checking and attribution | Consequential AI or tool outputs, required human checks, checking capacity, and the distinction among joint, supported, and independent performance. |
| coordination and fallback | Handoffs, deadlines, failure signals, dispute path, and feasible fallback conditions. |
| disposition | `enabled development arrangement`, `supported design with exact missing contribution`, or `class arrangement awaiting personal fit`. |
| continuation | Practice start condition, personal-fit input, HCD.8 build question, provider return, schedule or capacity change, or smallest condition that reopens the arrangement. |

Call the arrangement enabled only when every contribution needed for the bounded start and evidence use has a supported provider, access route, capacity window, and fallback or safe stop. A missing later optional contribution need not block an earlier independent result; state its reach rather than declaring the whole design impossible.

#### HCD.7:4.7 - Recognition and Assurance

**Recognition.** Use HCD.7 when a plan says “a teacher will support,” “peers will review,” “the platform provides feedback,” or “AI will help,” but the concrete contribution, recipient, timing, capacity, human check, or fallback is still unclear.

**Assurance.** Stronger reliance needs contribution-specific competence and authority, actual access during the required window, total and peak capacity, privacy and safety conditions, feasible handoffs and fallback, and evidence appropriate to the protected result. Service operation, provider competence, learning, transfer, and retention remain separate conclusions.

#### HCD.7:4.8 - What Changes in Practice

A practitioner stops asking, “Do we have a teacher and an AI tool?” and asks, “Which exact contribution must reach which learner or task, who can supply it under these data and authority conditions, does capacity fit the critical window, what human checking remains, and what happens when it fails?” Missing support becomes a named result, not a surprise during practice.

### HCD.7:5 - Archetypal Grounding

#### HCD.7:5.1 - Twelve Participants and the 240-Hour Teacher Account

The FoundationsProgramme class design has twelve participants, thirty-two ordinary weeks, and two final weeks. An ordinary week allocates 1.5 teacher hours to group review, 3 hours to checking twelve short results, 1 hour to case and source preparation, and 1 hour to individual difficulties and assessment calibration: 6.5 hours. Each final week requires 16 hours, including individual reading, conversations, subgroup review, calibration, repeat checks, and a small delay or disagreement reserve.

The total is `32 × 6.5 + 2 × 16 = 240` teacher hours. Dividing by twelve gives a costing allocation of 20 hours per participant; it does not promise twenty individual-contact hours. The arrangement is enabled only if the two sixteen-hour peaks are reserved and ordinary feedback can arrive before the next two-week block.

With twenty-four participants, the original checking and conversation rows no longer fit. A single larger group meeting supplies no additional individual review. The supported return is to double the relevant checking capacity, divide the group with competent providers, reduce or redesign the promised contribution, or delay launch. HCD.7 does not call the larger class enabled merely because 240 total hours still appear in a budget.

#### HCD.7:5.2 - Peer Review Prepares; Competent Assessment Decides

Participants work in triads. A peer checks a small result, identifies a disputed assumption, and prepares a focused question. The teacher then applies the target-domain criteria, resolves a protected-error question, and gives feedback that the learner can use before the next block. The peer contribution reduces search and coordination burden but does not become the teacher's competence or authority.

For a final attempt, the arrangement separately allocates reading, an individual conversation, possible correction, and a calibrated repeat check. If the named teacher is unavailable, a prepared colleague with compatible competence and access can be the fallback; otherwise the final week moves. A calendar placeholder or unprepared substitute is not a fallback.

#### HCD.7:5.3 - AI Search and Critique with Human Checking

AI may search public sources, propose examples, calculate, draft, or critique within the permitted data boundary. The learner first states the question and acceptable-result condition, then checks decisive sources, assumptions, configuration, and calculations and explains why the selected Method applies. A human assessor retains the final protected-error judgement.

The capacity account includes preparation of permitted prompts, checking consequential output, correcting failures, and a path for closed project data that cannot be sent to the tool. A narrow changed-support task can make one human contribution visible, but ordinary AI-supported work remains legitimate when the later-Work configuration includes it. Neither access nor fluent output establishes learning.

#### HCD.7:5.4 - Project Access Missing before Practice

A learner and teacher are available, but the planned workplace task requires project data and permission for a small safe trial that the organization has not granted. The arrangement returns the exact data, authorization, owner, affected task, and retry condition. A stipulated teaching case can preserve an earlier practice use, but it cannot be reported as the workplace result. HCD.8 is opened only if building an alternative environment or provider capability is the selected response.

### HCD.7:6 - Bias-Annotation

Lenses: **Gov**, **Arch**, **Onto/Epist**, **Prag**, **Did**. Scope: arranging actually available provider, access, tool, AI, environment, and human-check contributions for bounded capability-development use.

| Recurring bias | Likely drift | Repair |
| --- | --- | --- |
| provider-label bias | “Teacher,” “mentor,” “community,” or “platform” is assumed to supply all functions. | Allocate contribution-specific competence, authority, timing, capacity, and constraints. |
| aggregate-hours bias | A total or per-person quotient is treated as service availability. | Test feedback deadlines, concurrency, individual work, and peak periods. |
| automation-free-capacity bias | AI assistance is counted without prompting, checking, correction, escalation, or fallback. | Put the human-check contribution and its workload in the allocation. |
| peer-substitution bias | Peer participation replaces independent or competent assessment. | State the peer's preparatory contribution and retain the required assessor result. |
| access-by-account bias | A login, licence, or repository membership is treated as usable access. | Verify data, permissions, interface, timing, accessibility, and task compatibility. |
| class-average bias | A class arrangement is assumed suitable for every participant. | Recheck personal task, participation, access, support, and accommodation conditions. |
| nominal-fallback bias | An unprepared substitute or delayed source is called resilient capacity. | Test the fallback against the same competence, access, timing, and authority positions. |
| supported-capability bias | Equipped output or supervised participation becomes a human capability claim. | Preserve support conditions and route stronger evidence to HCD.11–HCD.13. |

### HCD.7:7 - Conformance Checklist

- [ ] The human holder or learner class, participation basis, programme or practice, later-Work target, calendar, evidence uses, and receiving decision are explicit.
- [ ] Every required explanation, preparation, access, feedback, checking, assessment, specialist, coordination, environment, and fallback contribution has its own row.
- [ ] Provider competence, scope, authority, and independence are supported for the exact contribution rather than inferred from a role label.
- [ ] Data, source, project, tool, AI, interface, privacy, confidentiality, accessibility, safety, legal, and organizational conditions are explicit where material.
- [ ] Total capacity includes preparation, delivery, review, correction, coordination, disagreement, fallback, and competing work.
- [ ] Peak periods, concurrency, feedback deadlines, and handoff times fit the actual provider windows.
- [ ] Peer, teacher, assessor, specialist, organization, source, tool, and AI contributions remain distinct.
- [ ] AI and tool rows state permitted work, prohibited data or use, human checks, checking capacity, attribution, and fallback.
- [ ] Intended help, permitted help, actual availability, received help, joint performance, and independent performance are not conflated.
- [ ] A fallback satisfies the same relevant competence, access, capacity, timing, and authority conditions or is reported as unavailable.
- [ ] A class-level arrangement retains personal-fit and participation questions for the named learner.
- [ ] The disposition says which bounded use can start and returns each exact missing contribution without claiming provider creation, service operation, learning, transfer, or retention.

### HCD.7:8 - Common Anti-Patterns and How to Avoid Them

| Anti-pattern | Why it fails | Repair |
| --- | --- | --- |
| “We have 20 teacher hours per learner” | A costing allocation is mistaken for individual contact and peak capacity. | Reconstruct the actual shared, individual, preparation, checking, and final-week rows. |
| “One meeting supports twice the class” | Individual checking and conversations grow even when group time does not. | Add competent capacity, divide the group, redesign the contribution, or delay. |
| “Peers can grade it” | Peer review may lack domain competence, calibration, independence, or authority. | Use peers to prepare feedback and preserve the qualified assessor contribution. |
| “The platform gives feedback” | The provider, information content, latency, and correction opportunity are hidden. | Name the exact feedback contribution and its evidence, timing, and recipient. |
| “AI saves the teacher's time” | Prompting, checking, correction, data limits, and escalations are omitted. | Compare the complete human-plus-tool workload and preserve consequential judgement. |
| “A licence means access” | Credentials can coexist with missing data, permission, interface, or usable timing. | Test access for the actual task and window. |
| “Supervised participation proves capability” | Participation, entrusted access, and supported output answer different questions. | Record the support configuration and obtain the applicable performance evidence. |
| “We should train every provider” | Existing qualified contributions are ignored and HCD.8 becomes ritual. | Use the available provider; build only the exact missing capability. |

### HCD.7:9 - Consequences

The practitioner gains a launch boundary grounded in real contributions rather than names or totals. Capacity bottlenecks, data restrictions, assessment independence, AI checking, and fallback become visible early enough to redesign the arrangement or return a precise missing result.

The cost is service-level detail. Provider competence, access, schedules, peak loads, handoffs, and privacy conditions must be checked, and some attractive programme designs will remain disabled. A class design may still need personal adaptation, and an enabled arrangement still supplies no evidence that learning occurred.

### HCD.7:10 - Rationale

Development support is a configuration of contributions. Its feasibility depends on the relation among task, provider, recipient, timing, access, capacity, and consequence. Provider names and aggregate resources erase those relations and make substitution look easier than it is.

Separating intended, permitted, available, and received help protects both design and evidence. Explicit human checking prevents automation from hiding labour or reallocating consequential judgement without notice. A precise missing-contribution return gives HCD.8 or another owner a real build question instead of a generic demand for more support.

### HCD.7:11 - SoTA-Echoing

The working question is: **How should a practitioner establish that the people, access, tools, AI, and other support needed by a development use are actually available at the required competence, time, capacity, and consequence boundary?**

The selected best-known line treats support as a configuration of distinct provider, access, coordination, and checking contributions rather than as a provider label. Brooks and colleagues' 2024 qualitative study of learning-health-system communities ([`SOC-05`](https://doi.org/10.1002/lrh2.10412)) makes people, platform, facilitation, leadership, and resource contributions separately visible; it supplies arrangement questions, not a causal capability effect. Matsuo and Tsukube's 2020 cognitive-apprenticeship review ([`SOC-02`](https://doi.org/10.1016/j.ijme.2020.100417)) supports distinguishing modelling, coaching, scaffolding, articulation, reflection, and exploration while leaving their sequence and workplace transfer conditional. HCD.7 adapts those contributions into the allocation and service tests in §§4.2–4.5.

The serious ordinary alternative is label-and-total planning: name a teacher, mentor, community, apprenticeship, or platform, total the available hours, and assume the functions will be supplied. It is cheaper to plan, but it hides competence, individual work, peak load, access, privacy, assessment independence, and fallback. HCD.7 rejects the provider label and aggregate total as sufficiency evidence while retaining them as inputs to a complete contribution account.

Tullis, Goldstone, and Hanson's 2015 experiment ([`COG-11`](https://doi.org/10.1080/00222895.2015.1008686)) is direct limit evidence that assistance improving practice can impair later unassisted performance and that support scheduling matters in its laboratory motor task; it does not prescribe a universal fading schedule or occupational arrangement. Wiese, Kilty, and Bennett's 2018 clinical realist synthesis ([`SOC-03`](https://doi.org/10.1111/medu.13655)) shows that supervised participation and graduated access depend on task risk, trust, learner position, and service pressure; its clinical governance does not transfer wholesale. Dell’Acqua and colleagues' bounded field experiment ([`AI-03`](https://doi.org/10.1287/orsc.2025.21838)) supports independent error challenge under specific task and model conditions, not a stable universal AI frontier. These limits produce the support-state, human-check, and changed-support boundaries in §§4.3–4.7.

The selected line costs more coordination than naming providers and adding hours, but it preserves the exact result, peak feasibility, authority, privacy, human judgement, and fallback that the cheaper plan loses. Reopen it when a provider, task, capacity window, access rule, AI condition, evidence use, or serious source changes, or when a lower-effort arrangement test preserves the same decision-bearing values.

### HCD.7:12 - Relations

- **`HCD.2`:** supplies intended contribution and access allocations for programme candidates; HCD.7 establishes what is actually available.
- **`HCD.5`:** supplies Method-specific provider, support, task, risk, and evidence needs.
- **`HCD.6`:** supplies task materials, permitted help, criteria, feedback opportunities, and domain-provider requirements.
- **`HCD.8`:** receives an exact unavailable provider, environment, assessment, or support contribution when creating it is selected; existing qualified contributions do not require rebuilding.
- **`HCD.9`:** uses the enabled arrangement and records help actually received, feedback delivered, corrections, and provider failures during practice.
- **`HCD.10`:** may change support across practice episodes for a stated development or evidence purpose; the revised arrangement must remain feasible.
- **`HCD.11`–`HCD.13`:** interpret supported, independent, transferred, delayed, and changed-support performance; availability alone establishes none of them.
- **`HCD.14`:** may revise only the affected support assumption or programme component from compatible evidence.
- **Learning-product practices:** can specify concrete materials, interactions, and schedules; publication or access does not establish provider competence or service operation.
- **Organization, operations, platform, privacy, safety, legal, clinical, employment, accessibility, provider, and target-domain practices:** retain their own capacity, authority, competence, data, risk, and correctness decisions.

### HCD.7:End

<a id="hcd-8"></a>
## HCD.8 - Build the Recursive Capability-Development Arrangement

> **Type:** Human Capability Development practitioner capability-building pattern
> **Status:** Candidate

### HCD.8:0 - Use This When

Use this pattern when HCD.7 or an equivalent support check has identified one exact missing contribution that prevents a capability-development use: no available teacher can distinguish the protected errors, assessors cannot yet apply the criteria consistently, a safe practice environment does not exist, or another provider or enabling System cannot supply its required result.

The first useful result is a **bounded operating development arrangement**. The missing contribution has either been obtained from an already qualified provider or created through a provider or environment build, and its operation has been observed on a representative use under the required access, timing, capacity, authority, and consequence conditions. If this cannot yet be established, return the useful preparation already completed and the exact unsupported dependency. A design, set of materials, reserved budget, or completed provider course is not an operating arrangement.

The practical gain is selective recursion. The practitioner can compare obtaining a contribution with building it, prepare the actual provider task, test service operation, and follow a builder dependency only as far as the first result that is genuinely missing. Existing qualified providers and environments are used rather than rebuilt.

Do not use HCD.8 merely because a programme has providers, because a practitioner wants every teacher to complete the same training, or because HCD.7 has not yet checked availability. Use HCD.7 to arrange existing contributions. Use the direct organization, platform, safety, privacy, legal, technical, domain, or operations practice when that owner must create or authorize the missing non-human result; HCD.8 consumes the qualified return without claiming it as human learning.

### HCD.8:0.1 - Working Terms

| Name in this pattern | What it denotes |
| --- | --- |
| missing development contribution | The exact provider, assessment, environment, access, coordination, or support result that an HCD.7 arrangement needs but cannot currently obtain under its required conditions. |
| provider target task | The concrete work a provider must perform to supply that contribution, including recipient, input, output, criteria, protected errors, support, timing, and authority. It is different from the learner's later-Work target. |
| obtain route | Use of an already capable and available provider or environment after its fit and operation for this contribution have been checked. |
| build route | Preparation or change intended to make a person, group, organization, tool, or environment able to supply the missing contribution. Each direct owner retains its own correctness and authorization decisions. |
| operating test | A representative enactment in which the intended provider or environment supplies the exact contribution under material service conditions, with observable criteria, timing, handoffs, and failure signals. |
| blocking dependency | A missing provider or enabling result without which the current build cannot produce or operate the required contribution. |
| recursive build question | The same bounded question applied to a blocking dependency: what exact result is missing, should it be obtained or built, and what operating evidence would close it? |
| bounded operating development arrangement | The tested provider or environment contribution, its dependencies, access, capacity, authority, evidence, fallback, limits, and continuation into HCD.7. It establishes neither learner participation nor learning effect. |

Recursion here is a finite search for the first unsupported provider or enabling result. It is not a generic claim that one capability creates another, a fixed hierarchy of teachers, or a reason to model every participant and tool.

### HCD.8:1 - Problem Frame

A development design can fail after its activities are selected because the arrangement itself cannot do the work. The available teacher may explain the topic but be unable to judge the target-domain error. Two assessors may reach different decisions from the same answer. A simulated environment may exist but lack safe reset or the required configuration. A platform may contain the materials but not deliver the feedback interval promised by the programme.

The visible response is often to create more training, documentation, or technology. That can move effort without closing the missing contribution. A provider may finish preparation and still fail the actual target task; a technically functional environment may remain unavailable at peak time; a new assessor guide may leave judgement disagreement unchanged.

The practitioner needs to bind the build to one missing result, compare obtaining it with creating it, expose only dependencies that block that result, and test actual operation before returning the contribution to the programme.

### HCD.8:2 - Problem

The common failure is build-by-label: “train the trainers,” “create a lab,” “add mentors,” or “install a platform.” The phrase names an intervention or asset but not the provider task, service condition, or result that was absent.

A second failure is unbounded recursion. Every provider is treated as needing another provider, every tool as needing a support service, and every service as needing another capability programme. The chain grows because available qualified contributions are not accepted as stopping points and missing relations are replaced by a generic “enables” arrow.

A third failure is readiness by preparation. Materials, credentials, rehearsals, allocated hours, or configured software are counted as service operation. They may be necessary inputs, but none shows that the required contribution can reach its recipient in the intended conditions.

### HCD.8:3 - Forces

- Obtaining an existing qualified provider can be fast, while fit, availability, independence, access, and continuity still need evidence.
- Developing a provider can preserve local knowledge and future capacity, while it consumes time and may miss the present programme window.
- Building an environment can make representative practice possible, while safety, data, configuration, reset, maintenance, and authorization remain direct-owner results.
- Standard preparation makes provider work more comparable, while a script can conceal whether the provider can handle a changed case or dispute.
- A recursive dependency view can expose the real bottleneck, while following every imaginable dependency creates delay and false structure.
- One observed operating test gives stronger evidence than a design, while its claim remains bounded to the tested task, providers, conditions, load, and horizon.
- Supported provider performance may be legitimate in the intended service, while it does not establish unsupported or retained provider performance.
- HCD can coordinate a missing development contribution, while domain, employment, safety, legal, privacy, platform, organization, and operations owners retain their own decisions.

### HCD.8:4 - Solution

Start from one exact HCD.7 gap. Compare an obtain route with the smallest build route that can close it. Specify the provider's own target task, prepare only what that task needs, follow only blocking dependencies, and observe the contribution operating under representative service conditions before returning it as available.

#### HCD.8:4.1 - Bind One Missing Contribution

Record:

1. the learner, class, programme, practice, or evidence use that is blocked;
2. the exact missing contribution, recipient, required time, output form, criteria, protected errors, and consequence boundary;
3. what HCD.7 has already established about available providers, access, capacity, constraints, and fallback;
4. the direct owner of each correctness, competence, permission, safety, privacy, technical, or authority result; and
5. the smallest operating observation that would let HCD.7 reconsider availability.

Do not replace this input with “we need better instructors” or “the lab is not ready.” Name what the instructor must do or what the environment must supply, for whom, when, and under which conditions.

#### HCD.8:4.2 - Compare Obtaining with Building

Construct at least one feasible route when one exists:

| Comparison position | Obtain an existing contribution | Build the missing contribution |
| --- | --- | --- |
| result fit | Evidence that the provider or environment can supply the exact result | Target task and proposed change capable of producing that result |
| time and window | Onboarding, contracting, access, calibration, and actual availability | Preparation, practice, correction, retest, installation, validation, and ramp-up |
| burden and risk | Search, handoff, confidentiality, continuity, dependency, and switching burden | Development effort, supervision, failure exposure, maintenance, and displaced work |
| authority and independence | Permission, accountability, assessment independence, and domain legitimacy | Who can authorize, judge, and operate the created contribution |
| operating evidence | Representative sample or observed service under the intended conditions | Representative operating test after preparation or change |
| continuation | Renewal, backup, transfer, or replacement condition | Maintenance, refresh, supervision, fallback, and smallest reopen condition |

Prefer neither route by default. An existing provider is not sufficient because of reputation or title, and a local build is not superior because it creates internal capacity. Return the non-dominated route for the current contribution, window, burden, risk, continuity, and evidence use; the authorized programme decision remains separate.

#### HCD.8:4.3 - Specify the Provider or Environment Target Task

For a human provider, state the person's own target work:

- representative inputs and cases;
- required explanation, feedback, assessment, preparation, coordination, or specialist output;
- criteria and protected errors supplied by the target domain;
- permitted references, tools, AI, colleague or supervisor help;
- required response time, volume, handoff, independence, and authority; and
- current evidence, uncertainty, preparation, first attempt, correction, and operating test.

Use the applicable HCD patterns only for the values actually missing in that provider's human capability. For an organization, platform, tool, or environment, request the direct owner's corresponding service, technical, safety, privacy, access, or operating result. Do not redescribe a non-human system as a learner merely to keep the work inside HCD.

#### HCD.8:4.4 - Expose Only Blocking Dependencies

Read backward from the missing contribution to the first unsupported dependency. A teacher may need a calibrated answer key from a domain specialist; the specialist may need an approved test result; the practice environment may need an isolated data set and a reset function. Ask for the direct result that closes that dependency.

Use A.1.STM's plain long-mantra map when the builder chain is not yet established: keep the final contribution visible, name supported answers, and stop at the first missing one. Use E.18.NET only when the current question truly needs independently identified transformation-flow structures and exact cross-boundary relation occurrences. A diagram, shared resource, calendar sequence, or word such as “creates” or “enables” does not establish that network.

Stop recursion when:

- an already qualified and available contribution satisfies the dependency;
- a direct owner returns the required result under compatible conditions;
- the current build can proceed without the optional dependency; or
- an exact unsupported dependency, owner, consequence, and retry condition have been returned.

Do not build a provider because that provider appears in the dependency map. Build only a missing result whose absence blocks the selected operating contribution.

#### HCD.8:4.5 - Prepare and Enact the Smallest Build

Select preparation that addresses the observed provider-task gap: worked examples, modelling, guided practice, criterion comparison, supervised access, a safe sandbox, a calibrated key, tool configuration, dispute rehearsal, or another qualified Method. Preserve the starting attempt and the support received so that preparation is not mistaken for prior competence.

For a human provider, connect feedback to a specific action and obtain another attempt. For an environment or service, test the changed function, access, load, failure signal, and recovery through the direct owner. Materials, schedules, purchased tools, completed instruction, and reserved hours are preparation results; retain them without calling the contribution operational.

#### HCD.8:4.6 - Run an Operating Test

Observe the intended provider or environment deliver the contribution on a representative but safely bounded case. Preserve:

1. the provider, recipient, task, configuration, support, and direct owners;
2. the input, output, criteria, protected errors, timing, volume, handoffs, and access conditions;
3. the first operating result, any disagreement or failure, feedback, correction, and repeated result;
4. actual load, latency, escalation, fallback, and consequences; and
5. the exact claim supported, its limits, and the smallest change that reopens it.

A rehearsal against the same memorized example may show procedural compliance but not handling of the required variation. Use a new representative case when the provider must discriminate, adapt, or resolve a dispute. Test the intended support configuration; require unsupported performance only when the provider's actual task needs it.

An operating test supports a bounded provider or environment contribution. It does not establish that learners received it, learned from it, transferred it to later Work, or retained capability. It also does not make the whole organization, platform, or programme operational when only one contribution was tested.

#### HCD.8:4.7 - Return the Built Result or Exact Stop

An HCD.8 result contains:

| Result position | Required content |
| --- | --- |
| build boundary | Missing contribution, blocked HCD use, recipient, provider task, conditions, consequence, and direct owners |
| route comparison | Obtain and build candidates, finite burden, time, risk, authority, continuity, and operating-evidence differences |
| dependency boundary | Supported inputs, first blocking dependency, direct result used, and where recursion stopped |
| preparation | Provider or environment change, support, materials, access, practice, feedback, correction, and unresolved limits |
| operating evidence | Representative case, conditions, observed output, criteria, timing, load, disagreement, correction, fallback, and qualification |
| disposition | `qualified existing contribution obtained`, `bounded operating development arrangement`, `preparation only`, or `exact unsupported dependency` |
| continuation | HCD.7 recheck, another operating case, direct-owner return, changed schedule, maintenance, fallback, or smallest reopen condition |

Return to HCD.7 only the contribution and conditions its arrangement needs. Keep provider-development evidence, service-operation evidence, learner attempts, and learner-effect evidence separate.

#### HCD.8:4.8 - Recognition and Assurance

**Recognition.** Use HCD.8 when a named contribution is unavailable and the proposed answer is to recruit, prepare, train, configure, build, calibrate, or supervise someone or something. Ask first whether an already qualified contribution can be obtained and what exact operating result is missing.

**Assurance.** Stronger reliance needs an exact provider target task, qualified domain criteria, truthful starting evidence, a bounded obtain/build comparison, supported blocking dependencies, an operating test under material conditions, direct-owner authorization, and a return limited to the observed contribution. A course completion, asset existence, design review, or one rehearsed answer cannot substitute for operation.

#### HCD.8:4.9 - What Changes in Practice

A practitioner stops asking, “How do we train all providers?” and asks, “Which missing contribution blocks this development use, can we obtain it, what must the provider or environment actually do, which dependency is first unsupported, and what representative operating result would let HCD.7 enable the arrangement?” Build work becomes finite, testable, and stoppable.

### HCD.8:5 - Archetypal Grounding

#### HCD.8:5.1 - Calibrate Assessors on Three Contrasting Answers

HCD.7 has exposed a missing contribution: assessors cannot yet apply the engineering-case criteria consistently. Their provider target task is to judge a short answer, identify the exact requirement and evidence, preserve supported parts, distinguish a correct bounded use from a resource error or justified request, and resolve disagreement within the final-week assessment window.

Preparation uses three contrasting answers:

| Case answer | Required assessor judgement |
| --- | --- |
| The report matches HW3/FW8/SCHED2, mandatory checks are confirmed, and 24 test hours are available. The learner proposes ten changes in three homogeneous batches, requiring 23 hours, and states that demand of twelve leaves a growing queue. | Supported bounded proposal; evidence applicability, resource limit, and queue consequence are preserved. |
| The same data are supplied, but the learner promises twelve changes because batching is faster. | Unsupported: twelve changes require 27 hours, exceeding the 24-hour limit. |
| The changed report does not identify the tested firmware, and the learner asks which version was tested before relying on it for FW8. | Justified missing-information return. The same refusal would be incomplete when the first row's full compatible report is present. |

Assessors judge independently, then locate each disagreement in the disputed requirement, answer fragment, and supplied evidence. A calibrated specialist result or answer key resolves the domain question; smooth writing or a general impression of the learner does not. The assessors then receive another representative case, such as the same ten-change proposal with only 22 hours. They must reject the now-unsupported promise, preserve unaffected criteria, and invoke the disagreement route within the promised time.

The three examples, key, and reserved calibration hours are preparation. Consistent and timely judgement on the changed case supports the bounded assessor contribution. It does not prove general assessor capability, operation at the whole final-week load, or any learner effect; those stronger uses need their own operating evidence.

#### HCD.8:5.2 - Obtain a Qualified Provider instead of Missing the Window

A programme needs a specialist to check control-logic errors before practice feedback is released. No local teacher currently has evidence for that contribution. Developing one would take six weeks, while the next practice block begins in ten days. An external specialist is available, but title and contract alone are insufficient.

The obtain route checks a representative answer, the protected-error criteria, access to permitted data, a forty-eight-hour feedback window, the handoff to the teacher, independence, confidentiality, peak volume, and a backup. If the sample operation fits, HCD.8 returns the qualified contribution to HCD.7. Local provider development can remain a later candidate, but the immediate arrangement does not wait merely to prefer an internal build.

#### HCD.8:5.3 - Build a Safe Practice Environment through Direct Owners

A learner needs to practise a configuration change, but no environment currently permits a safe trial. The build route asks platform and safety owners for an isolated data set, the intended configuration, reset, logging, access control, and a bounded failure test. HCD.8 does not assert that these technical and safety properties exist; it consumes their qualified results.

The operating test has an authorized participant enter the sandbox, perform and reverse the change, trigger the failure signal, restore the baseline, and produce the required record within the practice window. If reset fails or production data remain exposed, the result is `exact unsupported dependency`, not an almost-ready lab. A stipulated paper case may preserve another learning use, but it is not the missing environment contribution.

#### HCD.8:5.4 - Stop the Builder Chain at an Available Contribution

Assessor preparation needs a correct answer key. A domain engineer already has compatible evidence, authority, and time to supply and defend the key. That contribution closes the dependency; HCD.8 does not create a programme to teach the engineer how to train assessors. If no qualified domain result is available, the return names that exact specialist dependency and its effect on assessor calibration. The map stops there until the direct owner supplies or authorizes another route.

### HCD.8:6 - Bias-Annotation

Lenses: **Gov**, **Arch**, **Onto/Epist**, **Prag**, **Did**. Scope: obtaining or building one missing provider or enabling contribution for a bounded capability-development use.

| Recurring bias | Likely drift | Repair |
| --- | --- | --- |
| train-everyone bias | Every provider is sent through a common programme whether a contribution is missing or not. | Start from one HCD.7 gap and accept qualified existing contributions as stops. |
| asset-readiness bias | Materials, credentials, allocated hours, or installed software are called operational. | Observe the contribution on a representative case under service conditions. |
| internal-build bias | Local capacity is preferred without comparing delay, evidence, risk, and continuity. | Compare obtain and build routes on the current contribution and window. |
| title-competence bias | Reputation, occupation, or contract is taken as contribution-specific capability. | Test fit and operation for the exact provider task. |
| generic-enablement bias | A diagram arrow hides the relation or direct result needed from another owner. | Name the exact blocking dependency and use its subject practice. |
| infinite-recursion bias | Every support dependency triggers another full build. | Stop at available qualified contributions, optional branches, or a truthful unsupported result. |
| same-example bias | Repeating the training example is treated as operating discrimination. | Test a new representative case when adaptation or judgement is required. |
| service-effect bias | Successful provider operation is reported as learner capability or programme effectiveness. | Keep provider operation, learner receipt, learner attempt, transfer, and retention separate. |

### HCD.8:7 - Conformance Checklist

- [ ] The exact HCD.7 gap or equivalent missing contribution, blocked use, recipient, conditions, and consequence are explicit.
- [ ] An existing qualified provider or environment is considered before a build is selected.
- [ ] Obtain and build routes are compared on result fit, time, burden, risk, authority, continuity, and operating evidence.
- [ ] A human provider's own target task is distinct from the learner's later-Work target.
- [ ] Non-human environment, platform, tool, safety, privacy, legal, and operating claims come from their direct owners.
- [ ] Recursion follows only an exact blocking dependency and stops at the first available qualified result or truthful unsupported return.
- [ ] Preparation, design, materials, credentials, hours, and installed assets remain preliminary until the contribution operates.
- [ ] The operating test uses representative inputs, criteria, protected errors, timing, load, handoffs, support, access, and failure signals.
- [ ] A changed or unfamiliar case is used when discrimination or adaptation is part of the provider task.
- [ ] The result states the exact contribution, qualification, limits, fallback, continuation, and smallest reopen condition.
- [ ] HCD.7 receives only the availability result it needs; provider-development, service-operation, and learner-effect evidence are not conflated.
- [ ] No provider, environment, programme, learner, transfer, or retention conclusion exceeds the observed conditions.

### HCD.8:8 - Common Anti-Patterns and How to Avoid Them

| Anti-pattern | Why it fails | Repair |
| --- | --- | --- |
| “Train the trainers” | The missing provider task and current competent providers disappear behind a generic intervention. | Name one missing contribution and compare obtain with build. |
| “The assessor passed the course” | Completion does not show consistent judgement on the target cases. | Preserve attempts, resolve criterion-level disagreement, and test another case. |
| “The lab has been designed” | A description does not establish safe access, reset, load, or operation. | Obtain direct-owner results and run a bounded operating test. |
| “We bought the platform” | Acquisition does not establish configuration, capacity, provider work, or recipient access. | Test the exact service contribution and handoffs. |
| “Every builder needs a builder” | The map becomes an infinite programme rather than a located dependency. | Stop when a compatible qualified contribution is available or return the first unsupported result. |
| “The specialist will enable it” | The specialist's result, timing, authority, and relation to the provider task are hidden. | Request the exact domain result and preserve its conditions. |
| “One calibrated answer is enough” | Memorized reproduction can conceal inability to discriminate a changed case. | Use another representative case and observe disagreement handling. |
| “The service test proves the programme works” | Provider operation is not learner receipt, learning, transfer, or retention. | Route those questions to HCD.9 and HCD.11–HCD.13. |

### HCD.8:9 - Consequences

The practitioner gains a finite bridge from an unavailable support design to an actually operating contribution. Existing capacity can be used quickly, missing provider work becomes testable, and a blocked environment or specialist result is returned precisely enough for its direct owner.

The cost is a separate provider or environment case with its own evidence. Obtain/build comparison, preparation, domain criteria, operating tests, disagreement handling, and direct-owner returns consume time. Some development arrangements remain preliminary or blocked rather than being declared ready from their assets.

### HCD.8:10 - Rationale

A capability-development arrangement depends on providers and environments that can perform their own work. The learner's programme cannot make those contributions available merely by naming them. Conversely, an existing qualified result does not need to be rebuilt to satisfy a recursive picture.

Binding recursion to the first unsupported dependency preserves the practical build-the-builder insight without inventing a universal creator relation or fixed levels. Separating preparation from operation, and provider operation from learner effect, gives HCD.7 a truthful availability result while leaving stronger claims with their proper evidence owners.

### HCD.8:11 - SoTA-Echoing

The working question is: **How should a practitioner obtain or build a missing provider or enabling contribution and establish that it operates for the bounded capability-development use?**

The selected best-known line combines an exact provider target task, contribution-specific preparation, representative operating evidence, and a finite dependency return. Kraiger and Ford's 2021 workplace-instruction review ([`COG-10`](https://doi.org/10.1146/annurev-orgpsych-012420-060109)) keeps instructional events, learner activity, learning outcomes, transfer, and job performance distinct and makes learner, design, work environment, opportunity, and support material. Matsuo and Tsukube's 2020 cognitive-apprenticeship review ([`SOC-02`](https://doi.org/10.1016/j.ijme.2020.100417)) supplies modelling, coaching, scaffolding, articulation, reflection, and exploration as conditional provider-development moves rather than a complete provider credential. HCD.8 adapts those contributions into the target-task, preparation, and operating-test steps in §§4.3–4.6.

The serious ordinary alternative is asset-first provider development: publish a guide, train every teacher, reserve hours, configure a platform, and call the arrangement ready. It is easier to administer, but it hides which contribution was absent, whether an existing provider was preferable, whether a changed case can be handled, and whether the contribution operates at the needed time and load. HCD.8 retains materials, instruction, and assets as preparation while rejecting them as operation evidence.

Tullis, Goldstone, and Hanson's 2015 laboratory experiment ([`COG-11`](https://doi.org/10.1080/00222895.2015.1008686)) supplies a bounded warning that assisted practice and later unassisted performance can differ; it does not prescribe unsupported provider tests or a universal fading schedule. Wiese, Kilty, and Bennett's 2018 clinical realist synthesis ([`SOC-03`](https://doi.org/10.1111/medu.13655)) makes task risk, trust, learner position, supervision, and service pressure visible in one clinical setting; its governance does not become a general HCD rule. These limits produce the explicit support configuration, consequence boundary, and direct-owner exits in §§4.3–4.7.

A.1.STM supplies the backward search to the first unsupported builder dependency and the stop at a direct subject pattern. E.18.NET supplies a recursive selected-network account only when independently identified transformation-flow structures and exact cross-boundary relation occurrences are actually needed; neither a dependency sketch nor this HCD result establishes such a network. These are framework architecture contributions, not evidence that a provider or programme works.

The selected line costs more than recording training completion or asset delivery, but it preserves contribution fit, actual operation, direct ownership, bounded recursion, and evidential truth. Reopen it when the missing contribution, provider task, obtain/build options, risk, support, operating conditions, direct-owner result, or serious source changes, or when repeated use shows that a lower-effort operating test preserves the same decision-bearing values.

### HCD.8:12 - Relations

- **`HCD.7`:** supplies the exact unavailable contribution, required conditions, supported arrangement, and return point; it decides actual availability after HCD.8's bounded result.
- **`HCD.1`–`HCD.6`:** may supply a human provider's target task, present evidence, programme, Method, and representative practice values when those are genuinely missing; they are not a compulsory subprogramme for every provider.
- **`HCD.9` and `HCD.10`:** may enact and vary provider practice where provider development needs them; their learner-attempt records remain separate from the operating-service result.
- **`HCD.11`–`HCD.14`:** may assess or revise a human provider's capability from compatible evidence; one service test establishes only its bounded operating contribution.
- **`A.1.STM`:** locates the first unsupported builder or enabling dependency while keeping the final development contribution visible and local truths with their subject patterns.
- **`E.18.NET`:** can organize independently identified transformation-flow structures and exact cross-member relations when that stronger network question is live; HCD.8 does not infer them from a build sequence.
- **`A.15.1` and production, service, operations, organization, platform, privacy, safety, legal, employment, accessibility, and target-domain practices:** retain actual Work, production, capacity, authorization, risk, correctness, and operating conclusions. HCD.8 consumes their bounded results.
- **Learning-product practices:** may supply provider guides, cases, keys, interfaces, and practice carriers; their existence does not establish provider capability or service operation.

### HCD.8:End

<a id="hcd-9"></a>
## HCD.9 - Perform Deliberate Practice with Feedback

> **Type:** Human Capability Development practitioner focused-practice pattern
> **Status:** Candidate

### HCD.9:0 - Use This When

Use this pattern when a development Method, representative task, and feasible support arrangement have been selected, and a person needs to attempt one changeable part of later Work, receive feedback that bears on that action, correct it, and try again. Use it for a safely bounded practice episode, not for accumulating nominal hours or repeating a completed exercise.

The first useful result is a **focused-practice episode result**: the task and intended focus, the learner's first attempt before answer-bearing help when that distinction matters, help actually received, the observed action and result, feedback content and timing, the learner's correction, a retry or exact unresolved difficulty, and the next practice focus. It can show changed performance in those episode conditions. It does not by itself establish independent capability, transfer, retention, or a causal effect of the programme.

The practical gain is a usable next attempt. A teacher, coach, peer, tool, or AI contribution no longer disappears into “feedback was given,” and the corrected answer does not overwrite the first response. The practitioner can preserve what was already demonstrated, focus on the actual error, and change the next case enough to test the corrected action rather than memorized wording.

Do not use HCD.9 when the task does not represent the target contribution, when required feedback or safe access is unavailable, or when the question is a stronger performance, transfer, or retention inference. Return task design to HCD.6, support to HCD.7/HCD.8, and evidence interpretation to HCD.11–HCD.13. A teacher demonstration, AI answer, attendance record, or corrected final product is not the learner's first attempt.

### HCD.9:0.1 - Working Terms

| Name in this pattern | What it denotes |
| --- | --- |
| focused-practice episode | One bounded attempt–feedback–correction–retry loop directed at a named action, criterion, error, or discrimination in a representative task. |
| practice focus | The specific action or relation the learner is trying to change or stabilize, rather than a topic name or global judgement of the person. |
| first attempt | The learner's initial response under recorded task, time, support, source, tool, AI, and provider conditions. It may be supported or independent; the conditions determine the claim. |
| changeable action | An observable selection, explanation, calculation, check, request, refusal, adaptation, enactment, or correction on which feedback can bear. |
| feedback contribution | Information supplied to the learner about the task, criterion, current result, error, or next action, with a provider, time, form, and opportunity to use it. A score or praise alone may not supply an actionable contribution. |
| correction | The learner's response to feedback: revised reasoning, action, work product, check, request, or explanation. A provider's replacement answer is not the learner's correction. |
| retry | A subsequent attempt on the affected requirement, normally with enough changed condition to expose rule use when discrimination or adaptation matters. |
| focused-practice episode result | The preserved first attempt, support received, observation, feedback, correction, retry or unresolved difficulty, qualification, and next practice focus. |

In this pattern, *deliberate practice* names this focused and feedback-connected working move. It does not denote a universal dose, hours threshold, causal package, or promise of expertise across domains.

### HCD.9:1 - Problem Frame

Practice is often recorded as exposure: pages completed, hours logged, cases submitted, or sessions attended. That record does not say what the person attempted, which help was present, what error was observed, whether the feedback reached the person in time, or what changed in the next attempt.

The opposite failure is answer substitution. A teacher demonstrates the correct result, a peer rewrites the response, or AI generates a fluent correction; the final work product improves, but the learner's contribution becomes unknown. When the first attempt is overwritten, later assessment cannot distinguish independent action, supported action, and copied repair.

A useful practice loop therefore preserves the initial conditions and response, supplies information that the learner can act on, and checks the affected action again. It keeps local correction valuable without promoting it to a capability, transfer, or retention conclusion.

### HCD.9:2 - Problem

The common shortcut is “more practice.” It treats duration or repetition as the intervention while leaving the target action, provider, information content, task variation, and next observation unspecified. A learner can repeat the same error, receive only a score, or become better at the displayed answer.

A second shortcut records only the corrected product. This removes evidence of the first error and the support that produced the repair. It can make a teacher's demonstration or AI output look like independent learner performance.

A third shortcut retests everything after one defect. It wastes already supported results, hides the precise difficulty, and may not fit the promised time. Conversely, passing an unrelated total score can conceal a critical error that still needs correction.

### HCD.9:3 - Forces

- An unaided first response can reveal current action, while safety, privacy, irreversibility, or high consequence may require early intervention.
- Immediate feedback can stop error rehearsal, while a safe delay may be needed to observe the learner's initial reasoning.
- A detailed explanation can support a novice, while it can also replace the action the learner was meant to perform.
- Scores are easy to compare, while the learner needs information about a criterion, error, and next move.
- Reusing a task makes correction economical, while a changed condition may be needed to distinguish understanding from answer memory.
- Preserving passed requirements saves effort, while a new answer can sometimes contradict them and reopen their status.
- Focused repetition can improve performance in the practised conditions, while accumulated practice history does not establish a universal causal dose or cross-domain transfer.
- Provider and tool support can make practice feasible, while help actually received must remain visible in the result.

### HCD.9:4 - Solution

Bind one representative task to one practice focus. Preserve the first attempt and its actual support conditions, observe the changeable action, provide criterion-bearing feedback at a risk-appropriate time, obtain the learner's correction, and retry the affected requirement under conditions that can expose the intended change.

#### HCD.9:4.1 - Bind the Episode before the Attempt

State:

1. the human holder, later-Work target, selected HCD.5 Method, HCD.6 task, and receiving development decision;
2. the practice focus, observable action, required result, domain criteria, protected errors, and useful uncertainty;
3. the task condition that makes the action necessary and one condition that could change the warranted move;
4. permitted sources, tools, AI, peer, teacher, specialist, time, prompts, demonstrations, and other help from HCD.7/HCD.8; and
5. the feedback provider, safe intervention point, correction opportunity, retry boundary, and stopping condition.

If the task, criterion, provider, access, or correction opportunity is missing, return that exact gap. Do not spend the episode and call the resulting exposure practice.

#### HCD.9:4.2 - Preserve the First Attempt and Actual Help

When independent action is part of the purpose, obtain and preserve the learner's response before an answer-bearing cue, demonstration, correction, or generated solution. Record the condition, elapsed time, sources opened, tools and AI used, questions asked, prompts supplied, peer or teacher intervention, and the point at which each contribution arrived.

A supported first attempt is legitimate when support belongs to the intended task. Name it as supported. If safety or consequence requires the provider to interrupt before completion, preserve the last safe action and reason for intervention; do not manufacture an unsafe independent observation. Intended help, permitted help, available help, and help actually received remain different facts.

#### HCD.9:4.3 - Observe the Changeable Action

Compare the attempt with the stated criterion and evidence. Locate the smallest action that explains the current difficulty or useful success: the selected source, configuration check, calculation, distinction, causal claim, request, refusal, adaptation, execution, or verification. Preserve uncertainty and plausible rival explanations when the observation cannot distinguish them.

Do not turn one error into a stable trait or broad deficit. An incorrect result may come from the task condition, inaccessible source, support mismatch, misunderstanding, arithmetic, domain knowledge, fatigue, or another factor with different next Work. If that distinction changes the intervention, return to HCD.3, HCD.6, HCD.7, or the direct domain owner before prescribing more repetition.

#### HCD.9:4.4 - Give Feedback the Learner Can Use

A feedback contribution names:

- the exact requirement or criterion and the observed part of the attempt;
- what remains useful and should be preserved;
- the error, uncertainty, missing check, or unsupported relation that needs attention;
- the smallest cue, question, example, explanation, or correction information suitable for the learner and task;
- the action the learner performs next and the opportunity and deadline for using the information; and
- the provider, channel, timing, support boundary, and any consequence or escalation.

Choose timing from the purpose, risk, and error. Intervene immediately for an unsafe, irreversible, privacy-breaking, or repeatedly rehearsed critical action. When conditions are safe and the first response is informative, allow enough of the attempt to preserve the learner's reasoning before feedback. There is no universal immediate or delayed timing rule.

A score can locate a threshold and praise can acknowledge effort, but neither substitutes for information about the action when a correction is required. A full solution may be appropriate as a worked example in another episode; if it supplies the current answer, record that the learner's independent action was not observed.

#### HCD.9:4.5 - Obtain a Correction and Targeted Retry

Ask the learner to use the feedback: explain the changed relation, revise the action or product, perform the missing check, or state a justified stop. Preserve the correction separately from the provider's feedback. Then retry the affected requirement.

Use a changed but comparable case when the target includes recognizing applicability, selecting a Method, adapting it, or responding to a decision-bearing condition. Repeating the exact answer may be sufficient only when stable execution of that same operation is the target. Keep requirements already demonstrated unless the new response contradicts them or the changed condition makes them material again.

If several independent gaps cannot be corrected and checked in the available episode, return the unresolved requirements and the smallest additional time, task, provider, or output decision. Do not add hidden practice hours or erase the gap with an aggregate score.

#### HCD.9:4.6 - Decide the Episode Outcome without Overclaiming

Use one of these bounded positions:

- **changed action under recorded conditions:** the retry shows the intended correction or discrimination with its actual support;
- **unchanged or recurring difficulty:** the same relevant error remains after usable feedback and a valid retry;
- **new or differently located difficulty:** the retry changes the diagnosis or exposes another dependency;
- **safe stop or incomplete episode:** risk, missing access, provider failure, time, or task invalidity prevents a truthful retry.

The first position is a useful local practice result, not proof that feedback caused the change or that capability will appear independently, transfer, or persist. Route those interpretations to their direct Methods. The other positions can change the next Method, task, support, or practice focus without declaring the learner generally incapable.

#### HCD.9:4.7 - Return the Practice-Episode Result

An HCD.9 result contains:

| Result position | Required content |
| --- | --- |
| episode boundary | Holder, target, Method, task, practice focus, conditions, criteria, protected errors, and receiving use |
| first attempt | Learner action and work product, time, uncertainty, and support state before feedback |
| actual help | Sources, tools, AI, peers, teachers, specialists, cues, demonstrations, and when each was received |
| observation | Criterion-bearing success, error, uncertainty, plausible cause, and what remains supported |
| feedback | Exact content, provider, timing, channel, intended next action, risk boundary, and opportunity to use it |
| correction and retry | Learner's correction, changed or repeated task condition, affected requirements, result, and support |
| disposition | `changed action under recorded conditions`, `unchanged difficulty`, `new difficulty`, or `safe stop/incomplete` |
| continuation | Next practice focus, task or support revision, stronger evidence question, unresolved gap, and smallest reopen condition |

Preserve the trace at the resolution needed by the next decision. Do not require one record per pattern when one complete task tests several connected Methods, and do not merge distinct first, helped, corrected, transfer, or delayed attempts into one score.

#### HCD.9:4.8 - Recognition and Assurance

**Recognition.** Use HCD.9 when a learner is about to practise or has produced a result, but the practitioner cannot yet point to the first attempt, actual help, feedback-bearing action, learner correction, and next retry. “Completed,” “received feedback,” and “improved” are warning labels when those facts are missing.

**Assurance.** Stronger reliance needs a representative task, qualified criteria, preserved support conditions, a safe first-attempt boundary, feedback with usable information, a learner-authored correction, a valid retry, and a conclusion limited to the observed conditions. High-consequence errors need direct domain and authority owners; HCD.9 supplies no final employment, authorization, release, transfer, or retention decision.

#### HCD.9:4.9 - What Changes in Practice

A practitioner stops asking, “How many hours did the learner practise?” and asks, “What did this person try under which help, which action did the feedback address, what did the person change, and did a comparable retry expose the corrected move?” The next episode follows the remaining difficulty rather than a generic demand for repetition.

### HCD.9:5 - Archetypal Grounding

#### HCD.9:5.1 - Redirect a Highest-NPV Answer to the Tested Configuration

In the release case, the learner's first answer selects external module B because its stipulated NPV is highest. Ordinary references and calculation tools are permitted. The financial calculation remains useful, but the answer relies on a vendor report without checking which firmware was tested.

The teacher does not replace the answer. The feedback is one discriminating question: “Which configuration does the report support?” The learner finds that the report concerns FW7 while the required release uses FW8, marks B as unsupported for that use, preserves the compatible financial comparison as conditional, checks A and C, and proposes a supported next step.

The retry changes the condition. A compatible HW3/FW8/SCHED2 report and 24 test hours now support ten changes in three homogeneous batches, which require 23 hours, while demand of twelve leaves a continuing queue. Repeating the earlier refusal is wrong. If the available time is then 22 hours, promising ten changes becomes unsupported. The episode shows whether the learner changed configuration and resource checking under the recorded support; it does not yet establish unfamiliar transfer or retention.

#### HCD.9:5.2 - Turn a Pump Claim into a Discriminating Observation

A pump attempt uses 22.3 kW and 99.5 m³/h and concludes that increased energy per volume proves higher resistance. The calculation supports an energy change, but resistance, regulation efficiency, and measurement uncertainty remain plausible explanations.

Feedback preserves the calculation and asks which feasible pressure observation would distinguish the leading explanations. The learner revises the claim, requests a pressure measure within the remaining specialist time, and states how a value near 448 kPa would support increased resistance under the stipulated model while a value near 400 kPa would redirect attention toward regulation efficiency. The correction is the conditional explanation and observation choice, not a teacher-supplied cause.

A later case changes uncertainty or omits comparable pressure data. A generic “collect more data” answer does not pass; the learner must preserve rivals and select an observation that can change the decision.

#### HCD.9:5.3 - Fit a Targeted Retry into Three Hours

The programme's retry slot allocates 135 minutes to the learner's correction and short changed case, 15 minutes to an individual teacher check, and 30 minutes to recording the result: three hours in total. The teacher chooses a case small enough for the affected requirement to be checked in that interval.

Requirements already demonstrated remain supported unless the new response contradicts them. If several independent gaps cannot be corrected and checked in the slot, the remaining requirements are recorded as incomplete. Continuing requires a separate decision about time or a narrower output; it is not added silently to the 340-hour programme and overall group success does not erase it.

#### HCD.9:5.4 - Keep Demonstration and AI Help out of the First Attempt

A teacher may demonstrate one move on an anchor case before the learner receives a distinct variant. The answer and key remain with the teacher; on the variant, the learner first receives the condition. If a detailed cue or demonstration is supplied after difficulty, the resulting correction is supported and the trace says so.

AI may search, calculate, draft, or critique within the permitted boundary. The trace records the learner's question before AI use, the generated contribution, decisive source and calculation checks, and the learner's explanation. A fluent AI revision is not the learner's correction until the learner checks and uses it. A later changed-support probe may be selected by HCD.10, while HCD.11–HCD.13 govern what that observation can establish.

### HCD.9:6 - Bias-Annotation

Lenses: **Gov**, **Arch**, **Onto/Epist**, **Prag**, **Did**. Scope: one focused practice–feedback–correction–retry episode for a named human action.

| Recurring bias | Likely drift | Repair |
| --- | --- | --- |
| hours-dose bias | Logged time is treated as the mechanism or proof of improvement. | Preserve the task, action, support, feedback, correction, and retry. |
| corrected-product bias | The final answer overwrites the first attempt and received help. | Keep first response, provider contribution, learner correction, and retry separate. |
| answer-substitution bias | Teacher, peer, or AI output becomes the learner's performance. | Require a learner action after the contribution and retain attribution. |
| score-only bias | A mark or threshold supplies no actionable information. | Name the criterion, observed error, useful part, and next action. |
| feedback-immediacy bias | One timing rule is applied regardless of risk and purpose. | Choose timing from safety, consequence, first-attempt value, and error rehearsal. |
| exact-repeat bias | Memorizing the corrected answer is mistaken for rule use. | Change a decision-bearing condition when discrimination or adaptation matters. |
| total-retest bias | One defect causes every supported requirement to be repeated. | Retry affected requirements and reopen others only when contradicted or material. |
| practice-effect bias | A local changed attempt becomes capability, transfer, retention, or programme-effect proof. | Limit the conclusion and route stronger inference to HCD.11–HCD.13. |

### HCD.9:7 - Conformance Checklist

- [ ] The holder, later-Work target, selected Method, representative task, practice focus, criteria, and receiving use are explicit.
- [ ] Required providers, access, support, correction opportunity, retry boundary, and safe stop are available.
- [ ] The first attempt is preserved before answer-bearing help when independent action is part of the purpose.
- [ ] Intended, permitted, available, and actually received help remain distinct.
- [ ] Observation names a changeable action and does not turn one error into a stable trait.
- [ ] Feedback identifies the relevant attempt, criterion, useful part, error or uncertainty, and next learner action.
- [ ] Feedback timing follows the purpose, risk, consequence, and value of the first response rather than a universal rule.
- [ ] The learner's correction is distinct from the provider's demonstration, answer, or AI output.
- [ ] The retry tests the affected requirement and changes a decision-bearing condition when rule use must be exposed.
- [ ] Previously supported requirements are preserved unless the new response contradicts them or makes them material again.
- [ ] Unresolved independent gaps receive an exact continuation rather than hidden extra time or an aggregate pass.
- [ ] The disposition remains bounded to episode conditions and makes no unsupported causal, capability, transfer, or retention claim.

### HCD.9:8 - Common Anti-Patterns and How to Avoid Them

| Anti-pattern | Why it fails | Repair |
| --- | --- | --- |
| “Practise for ten more hours” | Duration does not identify the action, error, feedback, or observation. | Select one practice focus and close one traceable loop. |
| “Feedback was provided” | Content, recipient, timing, and opportunity to use it are unknown. | Record the exact criterion-bearing contribution and next action. |
| “Here is the correct answer” | Provider output can replace the learner action. | Use the smallest adequate cue and obtain a learner-authored correction. |
| “The corrected file proves mastery” | The first attempt and support have disappeared. | Preserve attempt, help, correction, and retry as different facts. |
| “Repeat the same question” | Answer memory can mimic the intended discrimination. | Change the condition that should change the warranted move. |
| “Retake the whole module” | Already supported requirements and the exact gap are lost. | Retry the affected requirement and preserve compatible success. |
| “They improved after feedback” | Temporal order does not establish causality or durable capability. | Report changed performance under the recorded conditions and seek stronger evidence separately. |
| “Everyone passed overall” | A critical or individual unresolved gap is hidden by aggregation. | Keep holder-level requirements, safe stops, and continuations visible. |

### HCD.9:9 - Consequences

The learner receives feedback that can change a specific next action, and the practitioner receives a replayable account of what the person did before and after help. Successful requirements can be preserved, critical errors remain visible, and the next episode follows evidence rather than generic repetition.

The cost is detailed episode evidence and provider time. Tasks need observable criteria and a valid retry; feedback must arrive while it can still be used; and attribution may reveal that an apparently improved product says little about the learner. Not every difficulty fits the planned slot.

### HCD.9:10 - Rationale

Feedback contributes to development only through what reaches a learner and can be used in another action. Its name, score, or provider intention cannot substitute for content, timing, receipt, correction, and retry. Preserving the first attempt protects both learning design and later evidence interpretation.
