A local correction is valuable without proving a general capability. The episode result therefore ends at changed or unchanged action under stated conditions and sends transfer, retention, causal, and consequential decisions to the Methods that can support them.

### HCD.9:11 - SoTA-Echoing

The working question is: **How should a practitioner conduct a focused practice attempt and feedback loop that produces a usable correction without losing attribution or overclaiming the result?**

The selected best-known line treats feedback as task- and information-specific and connects it to an opportunity for correction and retest. Wisniewski, Zierer, and Hattie's 2020 meta-analysis ([`MOT-05`](https://doi.org/10.3389/fpsyg.2019.03087)) shows substantial heterogeneity across feedback forms and outcomes; task, information content, receiver, and opportunity to use it are material, while the mean effect does not select praise, score, corrective information, timing, or channel for this case. Winget and Persky's 2022 practical review ([`COG-06`](https://doi.org/10.5688/ajpe8906)) supports formative assessment, corrective activity, retesting, and an explicit threshold as one coherent arrangement, but not one universal threshold or later professional-performance claim. HCD.9 adapts these contributions into §§4.2–4.6.

The serious ordinary alternative is dose-and-score practice: assign more hours, provide a grade or model answer, keep the final submission, and infer improvement from completion. It is inexpensive to administer, but it loses the first attempt, actual help, information-bearing feedback, learner correction, changed-condition discrimination, and unresolved requirements. HCD.9 retains time and scores as episode conditions while rejecting them as the practice mechanism or sufficient result.

Nurse and colleagues' 2024 systematic review of deliberate practice for therapeutic skills ([`BEH-03`](https://doi.org/10.1080/10503307.2024.2308159)) supports focused objectives, guidance, feedback, and repeated refinement as promising for discrete therapeutic skills while leaving the evidence base, delivery form, patient outcome, broad professional capability, and durable transfer unsettled. Macnamara, Hambrick, and Oswald's 2014 meta-analysis ([`BEH-02`](https://doi.org/10.1177/0956797614535810)) shows that retrospective deliberate-practice histories explain differing and incomplete shares of performance variance across domains; it does not establish a universal hours threshold, causality, or cross-domain transfer. These limits produce the episode-level result and explicit non-dose boundary.

The selected line costs more observation and traceability than counting hours and completions, but it preserves learner attribution, actionable information, critical errors, usable correction, and the exact next focus. Reopen it when the target action, task, support, risk, feedback source or content, retry condition, population evidence, or serious alternative changes, or when repeated use shows that a lighter trace preserves the same decision-bearing distinctions.

### HCD.9:12 - Relations

- **`HCD.5`:** supplies the selected development Method, proposed mechanism, fit conditions, evidence need, and stop.
- **`HCD.6`:** supplies the representative task, decision-bearing variation, criteria, protected errors, permitted help, and feedback opportunity.
- **`HCD.7` and `HCD.8`:** supply available providers, access, tools, AI, environment, capacity, human checking, and any built contribution; HCD.9 records what was actually received.
- **`HCD.10`:** receives prior attempts and next focus when variation, spacing, interleaving, challenge, or support should change across episodes.
- **`HCD.11`:** can assess bounded performance from compatible attempt evidence without treating the practice loop as proof by form.
- **`HCD.12` and `HCD.13`:** govern unfamiliar-transfer and delayed-retention questions; a corrected practice retry establishes neither.
- **`HCD.14`:** may revise the affected Method, task, feedback, support, or programme assumption from compatible evidence while preserving unaffected parts.
- **Learning-product practices:** may implement the task, prompts, worked example, feedback interaction, and schedule; the carrier does not establish learner action or effect.
- **Target-domain, safety, privacy, legal, employment, assessment, provider, and authority practices:** retain correctness, protected-error, intervention, and consequential decisions.

### HCD.9:End

<a id="hcd-10"></a>
## HCD.10 - Vary, Space, Interleave, and Progress Practice

> **Type:** Human Capability Development practitioner practice-progression pattern
> **Status:** Candidate

### HCD.10:0 - Use This When

Use this pattern when representative tasks and focused-practice episodes already exist, and the practitioner must decide what to change across the next episodes so that a person encounters a decision-bearing difference, returns to practice at another time, selects among similar Methods or actions, or works with a different level of challenge or help. Start from the target action, prior attempts, intended later conditions, and the observation the change should make possible.

The first useful result is a **condition-sensitive practice progression**: a bounded set of next episodes in which each selected change has an intended discrimination or development purpose, task conditions, separated timing where spacing is used, retrieval and source conditions, permitted and actual support, progression rule, observation, safe stop, and reopen condition. The result may select one move, several compatible moves, or stable supported practice before any increase in difficulty.

Spacing means distributing repeated practice episodes across separated times. It does not by itself require retrieval, source removal, interleaving, greater difficulty, or less help. Whether an episode asks the learner to retrieve, consult a source, use AI, or work with changed support is selected separately from the target contribution and development Method.

The practical gain is discriminating practice rather than decorative variety. The practitioner can say which changed condition should change the warranted action, why two tasks are mixed, why an interval matters, and when support should remain, change, or return. An observed failure can reopen the assumption instead of automatically increasing difficulty.

Do not use HCD.10 to build the first representative task, repair an unavailable provider, infer transfer or retention from a schedule, or intensify challenge when the basic operation is not yet feasible. Return to HCD.6 for task design, HCD.7/HCD.8 for support, and HCD.11–HCD.13 for stronger evidence interpretation. When apparent failure could instead be applicability, access, expression, adaptation, enactment, or support, obtain the applicable E.23.CAE differential before assigning more practice on the assumption of capability loss.

### HCD.10:0.1 - Working Terms

| Name in this pattern | What it denotes |
| --- | --- |
| decision-bearing condition | A task, context, resource, support, configuration, timing, risk, or evidence value whose change can alter the warranted human action or Method. |
| variation | A change to one or more decision-bearing conditions intended to expose preservation, change, or failure of the target action. Renaming the setting while keeping the same answer is not useful variation. |
| spacing | Repeated practice episodes distributed across separated times, with the interval and intervening activity made explicit. It is a timing relation, not an automatic retrieval or support rule. |
| retrieval condition | What the learner must recover without reopening a named source or answer, what remains supplied, and why that distinction matters to the target contribution. |
| source condition | Which references, data, patterns, tables, calculators, tools, or other resources are available and what the learner must do with them. Source use can itself be part of expert performance. |
| support condition | Permitted and actually available human, peer, tool, AI, cue, example, or specialist help for an episode. A support change is separate from an interval. |
| interleaving | Mixing episodes from distinguishable task or Method families so that selection or discrimination is required, rather than presenting all examples of one family together. |
| progression | A conditional change in challenge, complexity, independence, speed, variability, consequence, or support based on prior attempts and the intended later use. |
| condition-sensitive practice progression | The selected next episodes, each with its purpose, task conditions, timing, retrieval, sources, support, expected observation, decision rule, stop, and qualification. |

Variation, spacing, retrieval, source use, interleaving, support change, and progression are different design choices. One episode can combine them, but the practitioner must be able to say what each selected choice contributes and which observation could disconfirm it.

### HCD.10:1 - Problem Frame

A learner can succeed repeatedly while the task, timing, sources, help, and answer structure remain unchanged. The success may be useful, but it does not show how the person will act when a configuration changes, a similar Method competes, a familiar cue disappears, or the work returns after an interval.

The usual response is to bundle several fashionable difficulties: delay the next task, remove sources, mix topics, withdraw guidance, and increase complexity. When the learner fails, the practitioner cannot tell which change mattered. The difficulty may be inaccessible data or excessive load rather than a useful challenge.

A usable progression selects changes from the target action and existing evidence, preserves their separate meanings, and gives each one a response rule. It can keep practice stable when variability would currently obscure rather than develop the action.

### HCD.10:2 - Problem

The common shortcut is calendar imitation: schedule reviews at attractive intervals and call them spacing, retrieval, transfer, and retention at once. Dates alone do not identify repeated practice episodes, intervening use, sources, support, or the inference the later observation can sustain.

A second shortcut is random difficulty. Cases are shuffled or made harder without a target discrimination. A novice who cannot yet perform the basic operation receives mixed tasks, reduced help, and an inaccessible source; failure is then blamed on weak capability.

A third shortcut treats support withdrawal as the definition of progress. This can remove a tool or reference that belongs in later Work, or convert an AI-supported task into a different task without saying so. Equipped performance, independent contribution, changed-support performance, transfer, and retention remain distinct questions.

### HCD.10:3 - Forces

- Stable supported practice can establish the basic action, while unchanged conditions can produce cue-bound success.
- Variation can expose decision rules, while cosmetic or excessive variation adds noise without a useful discrimination.
- Separated episodes can support later access, while one universal interval cannot fit different tasks, histories, and horizons.
- Retrieval can exercise unaided recovery, while expert later Work may legitimately depend on references, tools, or current data.
- Interleaving can require Method selection among similar cases, while blocked practice can be better for an unfamiliar basic operation.
- Less guidance can expose a human contribution, while premature withdrawal can increase avoidable load or unsafe error.
- More challenge can expand the practice envelope, while a failed attempt may indicate access, applicability, support, or task-design failure instead of capability loss.
- A scheduled later episode supplies an observation, while transfer and retention conclusions require their own task, delay, support, and practice-history argument.

### HCD.10:4 - Solution

Use the target action, prior episode evidence, and intended later conditions to select only the practice feature changes that have a named purpose. Specify variation, timing, retrieval, sources, support, interleaving, and challenge separately; observe the resulting action; and keep, reverse, or revise the progression when its assumption fails.

#### HCD.10:4.1 - Bind the Target, History, and Later Conditions

State:

1. the human holder, target action, selected Method, representative task family, domain criteria, protected errors, and intended later Work;
2. the relevant HCD.9 attempts, first-response conditions, help received, corrections, retries, unresolved difficulties, and intervening practice;
3. the later conditions that matter: task variation, configuration, source and tool access, AI use, provider help, time pressure, consequence, and horizon;
4. the current basic operation the learner can or cannot yet perform and the uncertainty that remains; and
5. the receiving use of the next observation and the stronger HCD.11–HCD.13 inference, if any, that remains separate.

Do not select a harder schedule from completion or confidence alone. If the prior attempt does not identify the action and support state, repair that evidence or run a bounded HCD.9 episode first.

#### HCD.10:4.2 - Select the Smallest Change that Can Answer the Question

Use this discrimination table:

| Question about the next practice | Candidate change | Required statement |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Does the learner change the action when a deciding condition changes? | variation | Which condition changes, which action should be preserved or changed, and which rival response is exposed? |
| Can the learner perform another episode after a material interval? | spacing | Which repeated episode, separated times, interval rationale, intervening activity, and later observation are used? |
| Must a contribution be recovered without reopening a source? | retrieval | What is retrieved, what remains supplied, why unaided recovery matters, and what support is still allowed? |
| Must the learner distinguish which Method or case family applies? | interleaving | Which alternatives are mixed, which similarities cause confusion, and what selection explanation is required? |
| Is stable performance ready for a changed level of challenge or help? | progression | Which feature changes, what prior evidence supports the change, and what condition pauses or reverses it? |

Select several changes in one episode only when their contributions and failure interpretations remain recoverable. Otherwise change one decision-bearing feature at a time or create comparable branches.

#### HCD.10:4.3 - Design Variation around a Warranted Change of Action

Choose a condition that can make the correct action differ: configuration FW7 versus FW8, 24 versus 22 test hours, 80 records versus 60 distinct requests after duplicates, pressure near 448 versus 400 kPa, complete versus missing source information, or a different authority or support boundary.

State the expected discrimination before the attempt. A useful variant can require a justified refusal, supported continuation, changed calculation, specific request, different Method, or preservation of the earlier action. A new story with the same values and answer is surface variety, not evidence-bearing variation.

#### HCD.10:4.4 - Space Repeated Episodes without Smuggling in Other Changes

Name the earlier episode, later episode, separated times, interval, and intervening activity. The interval should follow the intended use horizon, practical opportunity, prior access, risk, and available evidence rather than a universal spacing number. Record any practice, work use, feedback, or source encounter between the episodes.

Then specify separately for each episode:

- whether the learner retrieves a contribution or receives it;
- which sources, patterns, data, tables, calculators, tools, and AI are available;
- which human, peer, teacher, specialist, cue, or worked-example support is permitted and received;
- whether the task, configuration, time, consequence, or Method choice also changes; and
- what the later attempt can and cannot establish.

Two episodes at separated times constitute spacing even when the same appropriate references and tools remain available. Removing AI or closing a source is a support or source change, not proof that spacing occurred. A later observation after continuing use is not retention without intervening practice merely because a date has passed.

#### HCD.10:4.5 - Interleave only when Selection Is Part of the Target

Mix task families when the learner must recognize which Method, relation, calculation, check, or response applies and when their similarity makes that discrimination meaningful. Require the learner to state why one route fits and why a serious neighbor does not. Preserve category similarity, order, support, and result so that a failure can be interpreted.

When the basic operation is new or still unstable, begin with blocked or stable supported episodes if that makes the action learnable and observable. Introduce mixing after the learner can perform the constituent action sufficiently for selection to become the real difficulty. Interleaving is a candidate, not a default schedule.

#### HCD.10:4.6 - Progress Challenge and Support Conditionally

Progress one material feature: increase variation, combine more contributions, shorten response time, add consequence, reduce an answer-bearing cue, change AI or tool support, require a more independent first move, or move closer to later-Work conditions. Keep the protected safety, privacy, authority, and domain constraints.

Use a rule rather than a ladder label. For example: after a successful independent variant, remove the detailed cue but retain source access; after an applicability error, restore the working entry and one discriminating question; after a calculation error, give calculation-specific feedback and retry a changed value; after recurring support dependence, compare supported and changed-support attempts before further withdrawal.

Pause, reverse, or redesign when error becomes unsafe, the learner cannot yet perform the basic operation, support removal changes the intended later task, feedback cannot be used, or the observation no longer distinguishes the progression assumption. No fixed fading path or difficulty increase is required.

#### HCD.10:4.7 - Differentiate Apparent Failure before Assigning More Practice

When different explanations would lead to different next Work, ask whether the learner failed to recognize applicability, access the needed response or source, express the result, adapt the Method, enact the action, or operate under the changed support. Apply E.23.CAE when its differential is available and material. Its result informs the HCD decision; it does not choose the practice change.

A missing source, unavailable AI, inaccessible interface, invalid task, excessive load, or changed configuration is not automatically capability loss. Repair the direct condition, return to stable supported practice, change the variant, or select another Method as the evidence warrants. Intensify only when the stronger challenge still addresses the diagnosed practice need.

#### HCD.10:4.8 - Return the Progression and Observation Rule

An HCD.10 result contains:

| Result position | Required content |
| --- | --- |
| progression boundary | Holder, target action, Method, task family, later conditions, criteria, risks, and receiving use |
| prior evidence | Relevant attempts, corrections, support received, unresolved difficulty, and intervening practice |
| selected change | Variation, spacing, retrieval, source condition, interleaving, support change, or progression feature, each with its purpose |
| episode design | Conditions, separated times where applicable, interval, order, materials, sources, tools, AI, human help, task, and workload |
| expected discrimination | Action to preserve or change, serious rival response, and observation capable of changing the design |
| response rule | Continue, stabilize, pause, restore support, redesign, return to another HCD Method, or request an E.23.CAE differential |
| qualification | What the planned or observed episode can establish and which transfer, retention, or causal claim remains open |
| continuation | Next episode, stronger evidence use, safe stop, unresolved gap, and smallest reopen condition |

A calendar is one carrier of the result, not the result itself. A learning product may implement the selected sequence; HCD.10 retains the human-development purpose, evidence boundary, and revision rule.

#### HCD.10:4.9 - Recognition and Assurance

**Recognition.** Use HCD.10 when the next instruction is “make it harder,” “space it,” “mix the topics,” “test retrieval,” or “remove the help,” but the target discrimination, timing relation, source condition, support state, or response rule is missing. The warning sign is a bundled schedule whose failure has no unique interpretation.

**Assurance.** Stronger reliance needs prior attempt evidence, one target contribution, representative tasks, explicit decision-bearing changes, separated times for spacing, separate retrieval/source/support choices, task-similarity grounds for interleaving, conditional challenge progression, direct risk owners, and an observation with a lowering rule. A scheduled visit or hard task establishes no transfer or retention by form.

#### HCD.10:4.10 - What Changes in Practice

A practitioner stops asking, “Which spacing and difficulty recipe should everyone follow?” and asks, “Which condition should change this person's action, when should the task recur, what must be retrieved, which resources and help remain legitimate, why are these cases mixed, and what result will make us stabilize, progress, or back off?”

### HCD.10:5 - Archetypal Grounding

#### HCD.10:5.1 - Four Scheduled Returns Are a Design Example

FoundationsProgramme revisits six selected patterns at weeks 8, 17, 25, and 34. Week 8 returns to F.1 and F.0.1 in a new quantitative source question after about six weeks. Week 17 revisits C.16 and B.5.2 in a manager or engineering case outside the main project. Week 25 combines F.1, C.16, and A.10.1 around sources, measurement, and revision for a configuration question. Week 34 uses F.1, C.16, and C.11 in an integrated case.

These are repeated practice episodes at separated times, so they instantiate a spacing design. They are not an optimal calendar. Each two-week topic also contains an independent variation within the time assigned to return to earlier learning, and the programme records whether the Methods were used in intervening weeks. The later observations therefore occur after delay with continuing practice history; they do not establish retention without intervening use.

Support is selected separately. Week 8 retains references and ordinary tools while reserving five minutes for independent recognition of the question before search. Week 17 permits pattern texts and AI with a short separate internal-core probe. Week 25 retains the same means and records intervening use. Week 34 retains normal equipment for the main task and separates a narrow changed-support fragment. The calendar does not decide retrieval or tool withdrawal by itself.

#### HCD.10:5.2 - Keep the Thirty-Minute Core Probe and Its Fifteen-Minute Fragment Distinct

The final core probe lasts thirty minutes. Within it, one fifteen-minute fragment is performed while AI is unavailable. The supplied tables and an ordinary calculator remain available. The bounded human action is to recover the specified time or configuration constraint, reject an unsupported release, and propose a supported next move; a large calculation and full written solution are not required.

The no-AI condition applies to that fifteen-minute fragment, not to the entire thirty-minute core probe or the programme's main task. Normal pattern and AI access remains permitted for the main task. The result records whether the person performed the narrow contribution and what other help was needed. It is a changed-support observation inside the stated probe, not spacing, a universal AI-withdrawal rule, or a retention conclusion. HCD.13 governs the later delay/support interpretation.

#### HCD.10:5.3 - Vary the Release Case instead of Rewarding Refusal

One practice episode supplies a report for FW7 when FW8 is required; the warranted action is to reject that use. A later variant supplies compatible HW3/FW8/SCHED2 evidence and 24 test hours. Ten changes in three homogeneous batches require 23 hours, so a bounded continuation is supported while demand of twelve leaves a growing queue. Repeating the earlier refusal now fails. With only 22 hours, promising ten changes becomes unsupported again.

The variants change evidence applicability and capacity separately. Their value lies in selecting refusal, continuation, or constraint revision from the current condition, not in increasing difficulty or changing surface context.

#### HCD.10:5.4 - Stabilize before Interleaving and Fading

A learner who cannot yet compare energy per volume receives a worked example, integrated data, and stable supported practice. The next attempt changes the values but keeps the same operation and source access. Only after the operation is usable are pump explanation, ticket-load comparison, and release-configuration cases mixed when selecting the Method becomes the target.

After a successful independent variant, a detailed cue can be removed while ordinary references remain. If the learner then cannot identify the task at all, the practitioner restores the working entry and one discriminating question rather than removing more help. The progression follows observed action and intended later conditions, not a universal novice-to-expert ladder.

#### HCD.10:5.5 - Do Not Diagnose Loss from an Inaccessible Source

A later episode appears to show that the learner forgot a configuration rule, but the required current report was not accessible and the old cached report concerns another firmware. More repetition of the rule would not repair the access failure. The practitioner restores or requests the correct source and, when the distinction changes the next action, uses the applicable E.23.CAE differential before deciding whether a capability-development change is needed.

### HCD.10:6 - Bias-Annotation

Lenses: **Gov**, **Arch**, **Onto/Epist**, **Prag**, **Did**. Scope: selecting variation, timing, task mixing, retrieval, source, support, and challenge changes across bounded human practice episodes.

| Recurring bias | Likely drift | Repair |
| --- | --- | --- |
| desirable-difficulty bundle | Delay, retrieval, interleaving, source removal, and harder tasks are applied together. | Select each feature by a named target and preserve separate failure interpretations. |
| calendar-effect bias | Scheduled dates are treated as spacing effect, transfer, or retention. | Name repeated episodes, intervening practice, conditions, and the separate inference Method. |
| spacing-retrieval conflation | A later episode is assumed to require unaided recall. | Specify retrieval, sources, tools, AI, and human help independently of timing. |
| interleave-everything bias | Random mixing replaces task-similarity and Method-selection grounds. | Interleave only when discrimination among alternatives is part of the target. |
| harder-is-better bias | Difficulty is increased despite unstable basic performance or invalid access. | Stabilize, diagnose the limiting condition, and progress one warranted feature. |
| support-removal bias | Tools or references belonging to later Work are withdrawn as proof of progress. | Change support only for a stated human contribution or evidence use. |
| failure-as-loss bias | An unsuccessful changed-condition attempt is called lost capability. | Differentiate applicability, access, expression, adaptation, enactment, and support. |
| scheduled-proof bias | A later task is reported as transfer or retention by date alone. | Preserve task, support, delay, intervening use, and route interpretation to HCD.12/HCD.13. |

### HCD.10:7 - Conformance Checklist

- [ ] The holder, target action, selected Method, representative task family, prior attempts, and intended later conditions are explicit.
- [ ] Each selected practice change has a named purpose, expected discrimination, serious rival response, and response rule.
- [ ] Variation changes a decision-bearing condition rather than only names or scenery.
- [ ] Spacing names repeated practice episodes across separated times, the interval, and intervening activity.
- [ ] Retrieval, source access, tools, AI, human help, task variation, and challenge are selected separately from spacing.
- [ ] Interleaving is used only when distinguishing among sufficiently similar task or Method families is part of the target.
- [ ] Stable supported practice remains available when the learner cannot yet perform the basic operation.
- [ ] Progression changes one recoverable feature or uses comparable branches, with a pause, restoration, or backoff rule.
- [ ] Support withdrawal does not remove a resource constitutive of intended later Work without explicitly changing the target question.
- [ ] Apparent failure is differentiated from access, applicability, expression, adaptation, enactment, support, and task-design failures when material.
- [ ] Weeks 8, 17, 25, and 34 remain a design example with continuing practice history, not an optimum or retention proof.
- [ ] The final core probe remains thirty minutes; only its named fifteen-minute fragment lacks AI, while supplied tables and calculator remain.
- [ ] Planned or observed episodes make no unsupported causal, universal-interval, transfer, or retention claim.

### HCD.10:8 - Common Anti-Patterns and How to Avoid Them

| Anti-pattern | Why it fails | Repair |
| --- | --- | --- |
| “Review after six weeks” | No repeated task, intervening history, support, or target observation is specified. | Bind both episodes and their conditions before calling the schedule spacing. |
| “Spacing means closed-book recall” | Timing and retrieval are different choices. | State separated times, then select source and retrieval conditions independently. |
| “Mix all topics” | Random order may add load without testing Method selection. | Mix task families only around a named similarity and discrimination. |
| “Remove help every time” | Support may be premature to remove or constitutive of later Work. | Use an evidence-based progression rule and restore help when its assumption fails. |
| “Make the next task harder” | Difficulty has no target relation or lowering rule. | Change one decision-bearing condition and state the expected action. |
| “Failure means forgetting” | Access, applicability, expression, adaptation, enactment, or support may be the real limit. | Differentiate before assigning more practice for presumed loss. |
| “Week 34 proves retention” | Ongoing practice and changed task/support conditions are ignored. | Preserve history and use HCD.13 for the qualified delayed interpretation. |
| “The final test is fifteen minutes without AI” | It collapses the thirty-minute core probe into one fragment and drops supplied tools. | Keep the thirty-minute probe, fifteen-minute no-AI fragment, tables, calculator, narrow action, and main-task support distinct. |

### HCD.10:9 - Consequences

The practitioner gains a progression whose changes have interpretable purposes. Practice can expose condition-sensitive action, revisit it at relevant times, require Method selection where appropriate, and adjust support without turning every difficulty into a bundled experiment. Failed assumptions have explicit backoff routes.

The cost is more design discipline than copying a calendar or difficulty recipe. Prior attempts, intervening activity, task similarity, source and support conditions, and response rules must be recorded. Some attractive combinations remain unselected because their effects or failure meanings cannot be separated.

### HCD.10:10 - Rationale

The useful unit is not difficulty but a relation between a target action, a changed condition, and an observation. Variation, timing, retrieval, sources, support, task mixing, and challenge can each alter that relation in different ways. Keeping them separate makes a progression revisable and lets appropriate tools remain part of human performance.

A later practice observation is still practice evidence. Its date, unfamiliarity, or reduced support does not alone establish transfer or retention. Explicit history and boundaries let HCD.11–HCD.13 make the stronger inference without reconstructing hidden schedule changes.

### HCD.10:11 - SoTA-Echoing

The working question is: **How should a practitioner select variation, spacing, interleaving, retrieval, support, and challenge changes across practice episodes for one target action and evidence need?**

The selected best-known line is task-, similarity-, support-, and horizon-sensitive rather than recipe-based. Carpenter, Pan, and Butler's 2022 review ([`COG-01`](https://doi.org/10.1038/s44159-022-00089-1)) supports spaced encounters and effortful retrieval as credible candidates for durable access under source-specific conditions while selecting no universal gap, repetition count, threshold, or curriculum. Brunmair and Richter's 2019 meta-analysis ([`COG-03`](https://doi.org/10.1037/bul0000209)) shows that interleaving depends materially on between- and within-category similarity, complexity, and material type; some source families showed no benefit or favored blocking. HCD.10 adapts these contributions into the separate timing, retrieval, and task-selection steps in §§4.2–4.5.

The serious ordinary alternative is a bundled desirable-difficulty recipe: space all topics on one calendar, require closed-book retrieval, interleave them, remove help, and raise challenge together. It is easy to standardize, but a failure cannot distinguish timing, access, task selection, support, load, or capability. HCD.10 retains each candidate move while rejecting their automatic combination and any universal difficulty ladder.

Czyż, Wójcik, and Solarská's 2024 motor-learning meta-analysis ([`COG-04`](https://doi.org/10.3389/fpsyg.2024.1377122)) reports a heterogeneous random-versus-blocked transfer result whose applied-setting estimate was not significant; laboratory adult evidence does not establish a general professional schedule. Paas and van Merriënboer's 2020 review ([`COG-05`](https://doi.org/10.1177/0963721420922183)) supports changing worked examples and guidance with prior knowledge and task complexity, not fixed fading or “less load means more learning.” These limits produce the stable-practice entry and conditional progression rules in §§4.5–4.7.

Tullis, Goldstone, and Hanson's 2015 laboratory motor experiment ([`COG-11`](https://doi.org/10.1080/00222895.2015.1008686)) makes assisted practice and later unassisted performance different observations and offers a bounded support-scheduling cue, not a universal fading rule. Bastani and colleagues' 2025 school-mathematics trial ([`AI-04`](https://doi.org/10.1073/pnas.2422633122)) supplies counterexample evidence that higher AI-assisted practice performance can coexist with no improvement or worse later unassisted performance and that guardrail design matters; it establishes no adult-workplace effect or general AI ban. These qualifications motivate the separate support state and the narrow no-AI observation in §§4.4–4.7 and 5.2.

The selected line costs more than copying a schedule, but it preserves the target discrimination, legitimate tool use, interpretable failure, safe backoff, and later evidence boundary. Reopen it when the target action, prior attempts, task similarity, intended horizon, interval evidence, source or support configuration, risk, E.23.CAE differential, or serious alternative changes, or when representative use shows that fewer distinctions preserve the same decision value.

### HCD.10:12 - Relations

- **`HCD.5`:** supplies the selected development Method, proposed mechanism, fit, limits, and evidence need.
- **`HCD.6`:** supplies representative task families, decision-bearing variations, criteria, protected errors, permitted help, and domain-provider returns.
- **`HCD.7` and `HCD.8`:** supply feasible providers, sources, tools, AI, environments, capacity, human checking, and any built contribution for the selected episodes.
- **`HCD.9`:** supplies attempt, actual-help, feedback, correction, retry, and next-focus evidence; HCD.10 selects what changes across later episodes.
- **`E.23.CAE`:** can differentiate applicability, access, expression, adaptation, enactment, support, and capability-change explanations when they would alter the next practice decision; it does not select HCD.10's practice change.
- **`HCD.11`:** may use a compatible condition-sensitive attempt for a bounded performance inference without treating schedule form as evidence.
- **`HCD.12`:** governs unfamiliar-transfer interpretation; variation or interleaving alone establishes no transfer.
- **`HCD.13`:** governs delay, intervening-practice, source, and support interpretations; a scheduled later episode establishes no retention by form.
- **`HCD.14`:** may revise only the affected Method, task, schedule, support, or evidence assumption from compatible results.
- **Learning-product practices:** may implement the concrete calendar, task sequence, prompts, resources, and interfaces; HCD.10 owns the person- and target-relative progression rationale and evidence boundary.
- **Target-domain, safety, privacy, legal, employment, provider, platform, assessment, and authority practices:** retain correctness, access, risk, capacity, and consequential decisions.

### HCD.10:End

<a id="hcd-11"></a>
## HCD.11 — Assess Performance in Representative Work

> **Type:** Human Capability Development practitioner method pattern
> **Status:** Current practitioner preview

### HCD.11:1 - Problem Frame

**Use this when** a person's document, course grade, helped answer or successful task is about to support a claim about what that person can contribute in later Work. Start by writing the contribution to be judged, the conditions in which it is needed and the decision that will use the judgement. A compatible HCD.1 demand account can supply this frame; an equivalent qualified account is sufficient.

The first useful result is a **bounded capability-evidence account**: what the person demonstrably did, with which means, against which criteria, and what that supports for the receiving use. The result may instead identify an absent observation or an invalid assessment. This lets a practitioner choose the next useful practice or observation without promoting a good product into an unsupported claim about its maker.

Assess the human contribution within equipped work. If the work requires a current source, calculator, accessibility support or specialist return, competent use of that support may be part of the target. An internally available contribution is a different target and needs its own task.

Do not start an assessment when the real question is still what contribution is needed, what counts as domain-correct work, or whether the person had a workable assignment and access. Obtain that input first. Familiar performance does not answer unfamiliar transfer or retention after delay; those are HCD.12 and HCD.13 questions. Release, credentialing and employment decisions may require stronger evidence and retain their own responsible decision makers.

### HCD.11:2 - Problem

A visible output often mixes contributions from the person, a template, an AI assistant, a colleague and feedback. A correct answer after a cue can conceal the very recognition failure the assessment was meant to expose. Conversely, removing a required source can make competent source-dependent work look like a personal deficit.

A single score compounds the problem when correct calculation offsets an unsupported compatibility claim, missing observations are treated as errors, or a representative sample is replaced by whichever exercise is easiest to mark. The assessment then directs practice toward the wrong problem and gives later users more confidence than the observations warrant.

### HCD.11:3 - Forces

| Force | Working tension |
| --- | --- |
| Authentic work and attribution | Preserve the means used in later work while making the person's decision-bearing contribution observable. |
| Coverage and cost | Expose consequential variation without making every small practice decision require a full examination programme. |
| Feedback and observation | Feedback can help learning while changing what a later answer demonstrates. |
| Comparable judgement and domain specificity | Use explicit criteria and consistent scoring without replacing domain correctness with a generic rubric. |
| Useful conclusions and uncertainty | Return the claim already supported while retaining untested conditions and unresolved evidence gaps. |

### HCD.11:4 - Solution

#### HCD.11:4.1 - Frame one assessable contribution

Name the person, representative work family, relevant configuration, allowed means, evidence window and receiving use. Describe an observable contribution such as “detect an unsupported configuration claim and propose a justified continuation,” not “understand engineering.”

Separate the contributions when their evidence can differ. Calculation, source selection, independent challenge, coordination and explanation may all matter, but success in one does not establish the others. State which contributions must be available before a hint and which may properly use sources or other people.

If the demand is only prospective, an assessment may explore the proposed contribution; its result remains evidence for that declared task, not confirmation that the demand is current.

#### HCD.11:4.2 - Connect the claim to a task and an inference

Before seeing the answer, make this short chain recoverable:

| Question | Minimum answer needed |
| --- | --- |
| What is claimed? | The person's contribution under the stated conditions. |
| What task could elicit it? | A representative task containing the decision-bearing difficulty, with the required access and information. |
| What will count as evidence? | The action, explanation, work product, error or omission that would distinguish successful contribution from a plausible rival. |
| What may be inferred? | The bounded conclusion that those observations can support, and the conclusion they cannot support. |

Obtain domain criteria from a current qualified Method, result or competent specialist. Specify material errors, acceptable variants, and any condition that cannot be compensated by success elsewhere. Do not construct a scoring rule that can award overall success while such a condition fails.

Select tasks for consequential variation: for example a matching and a mismatching source, a feasible and an infeasible resource condition, or an ordinary and an exceptional coordination demand. Explain why the selected sample is sufficient for the receiving decision. There is no universal number of tasks. If a rare but consequential condition is untested, exclude it from the claim or obtain the relevant evidence before stronger reliance.

Return a missing criterion, unsuitable task or unavailable competent assessor before judging the person. These are assessment defects, not performance failures.

#### HCD.11:4.3 - Observe the attempt without hiding help

Explain the task conditions and permitted means. Observe enough of the attempt to attribute the contribution: relevant source use, choice, adaptation, action, result and any required recognition before a cue. Keep material errors, help, corrections and unfinished work visible.

A product alone is enough only when its origin and means of production cannot change the intended inference. Otherwise, use the least intrusive discriminating observation: a short explanation of one consequential choice, a source trace, a direct observation or a fresh task. Do not add an oral examination by default.

Keep the first attempt separate from correction after feedback. A teacher's question about the missing configuration can improve the answer; it also removes independent detection from that later observation. Record what the cue supplied rather than merely marking the answer “corrected.”

#### HCD.11:4.4 - Judge contributions and repair only the affected inference

Compare each observed contribution with its criterion. Distinguish a demonstrated error from an absent observation, an inaccessible task and a disputed judgement. Do not give a missing observation the value “failed” unless the task conditions made the required non-response itself informative.

For a material scoring disagreement, return to the same observation and criterion. Clarify an ambiguous criterion or obtain another qualified assessor when needed. If the original task did not discriminate the relevant alternatives, use a new task. Averaging incompatible judgements does not settle what happened.

When feedback or a task repair changes the conditions, preserve the first result and report the later result under the changed conditions. A later success can support a new claim; it does not rewrite an earlier failure.

#### HCD.11:4.5 - Return a result proportionate to its use

A short account is sufficient when it preserves the person and contribution, task and conditions, observations including help, criterion-based judgement, bounded inference, uncertainty and next observation. No particular form is compulsory.

Use the following distinctions in the conclusion:

| Finding | Useful return |
| --- | --- |
| The required contribution was observed | State the supported performance and the narrower capability claim, with tested conditions and sample limits. |
| A material criterion failed | State which contribution failed in which attempt; preserve successful contributions. |
| Observations cannot distinguish the claim from a live rival | State the missing discriminating observation and the current limit. |
| Task, access or criterion was defective | Return the defect to its owner and identify which judgement must wait or be replaced. |

Stop when the intended reversible decision has enough evidence or when a named missing input prevents it. Escalate assurance only when the receiving use needs broader coverage, independent judgement, scoring consistency, accessibility checks or a defensible validity argument. The assessor's account supplies evidence; it does not grant release or professional authority.

Reopen only the affected claim when a new observation, changed configuration, source, tool, criterion or receiving use changes what the evidence supports.

### HCD.11:5 - Archetypal Grounding

#### HCD.11:5.1 - Correct arithmetic, failed compatibility, later independent performance

Learner-L17 is a fictional person. The attempts below are constructed observations for learning how to assess; they are not observations of Engineer-E27, Engineer-P14 or programme participants.

The task is to recommend a defensible continuation for an engineering change service. HW3/FW8 is required. The supplied financial assumptions give approximate NPVs of 48.9, 51.6 and 44.6 thousand for alternatives A, B and C. B's highest NPV does not supply compatibility: its available report concerns FW7. A needs a further shared-power check. C retains HW3/FW8 and uses SCHED2, with two hours of required checks per change and one hour of preparation per homogeneous batch of at most four.

With 24 test hours available, ten changes need 20 + 3 = 23 hours; twelve need 24 + 3 = 27. C can therefore support a proposal for ten, not twelve, with two new changes per week left in the queue. The release manager must agree to that limit and retains the release decision. These engineering criteria are stipulated case facts, not general engineering or safety guidance.

The assessed contribution is to inspect configuration evidence, preserve required checks, and explain a supported continuation and its queue consequence. L17 has the reports, task tables, calculator and ordinary AI assistance; no teacher hint is permitted during an independent attempt. The assessor observes source and assistant traces and asks for an explanation only where needed to attribute the conclusion. In the two fresh cases below, the trace places L17's recognition before any teacher or AI cue names the mismatch; ordinary tool access remains available.

| Attempt and conditions | Constructed observation | Judgement and inference |
| --- | --- | --- |
| First attempt, no teacher cue | L17 calculates the financial ordering correctly and recommends B without noticing that the report is for FW7. | Calculation is supported. The compatibility criterion fails; the first recommendation is unsupported. |
| Same task after “Which configuration was tested?” | L17 finds FW7, withdraws B and explains A's remaining check and C's bounded ten-change proposal. | The correction is supported with a configuration cue. Independent detection has not been shown in this attempt. |
| Fresh representative case, ordinary means, no cue | L17 personally finds a mismatching report and explains why the higher financial value cannot settle compatibility; L17 proposes the supported C limit and queue return. | Independent configuration checking and bounded continuation are supported in this case. |
| Second fresh representative case, ordinary means, no cue | L17 again detects the mismatch, traces the relevant evidence and explains the 23-hour limit and remaining queue. | The specified contribution is supported across the two fresh cases, with a small-sample boundary. |

The result does not average the four attempts. It preserves the original miss, the helped correction and the later independent observations. For local practice planning, the practitioner can preserve the supported calculation and configuration contribution while testing material resource changes next. These observations do not establish performance across every engineering configuration or authorize a release.

#### HCD.11:5.2 - An inaccessible assessment is not a demonstrated deficit

A task asks a person to use a current technical source, but the assessment removes the source and an accessibility aid normally required for reading it. Failure to retrieve its detail cannot support the intended source-use judgement. Restore the target conditions or explicitly select a different, justified independent-core question; do not prescribe remedial learning from the invalid comparison.

### HCD.11:6 - Bias-Annotation

Visible fluency, confidence, speed and polished AI output can draw attention away from the contribution that matters. Inspect the criterion-bearing action, including a justified refusal or a justified positive proposal.

A familiar task can favour prior exposure; an unnecessarily unfamiliar interface can measure access difficulty. Keep familiarity and access conditions visible. High-consequence uses need the relevant accessibility and fairness evidence; a convenient local task does not supply it.

### HCD.11:7 - Conformance Checklist

- The same person, contribution, conditions and receiving use are recoverable.
- The task elicits the claimed contribution and covers the consequential variation being claimed.
- Domain criteria, material errors and non-compensable conditions were explicit before scoring.
- The observation distinguishes the person's contribution from assistance where that distinction matters.
- First attempts, feedback, corrections and unfinished work remain separately interpretable.
- Missing evidence, assessment defects, scoring disagreements and performance errors receive different returns.
- The conclusion preserves uncertainty, untested conditions, an ordinary stop and the next observation needed for stronger use.

### HCD.11:8 - Common Anti-Patterns and How to Avoid Them

| Anti-pattern | Better move |
| --- | --- |
| “The submitted answer is good, therefore the person can do the work.” | Inspect the person's contribution where the producer or means could change the inference. |
| “The corrected answer counts as independent success.” | Retain the cue and test the contribution in a fresh uncued attempt. |
| “The total score passes despite the compatibility error.” | Keep the non-compensable criterion decisive and preserve the correct calculation separately. |
| “Removing every tool reveals real capability.” | Assess the required equipped contribution; specify an independent core only when the work needs it. |
| “One success proves the whole capability.” | State the tested envelope and obtain observations for the consequential untested variants. |

### HCD.11:9 - Consequences

The assessment returns less impressive but more useful claims: a practitioner can see what to preserve, what to practise, and what cannot yet be judged. Targeted observations can cost less than a larger examination that misses the decisive contribution.

The cost is explicit task selection and observation of help and material errors. Richer scoring and independent assessment are justified by the receiving use, not imposed on every small practice decision. A higher score that hides a failed constraint is a worse result for that use.

### HCD.11:10 - Rationale

Capability is inferred from performance under conditions; it is not the document, grade or observation itself. Making the claim-to-observation link explicit exposes both overclaiming and needless retesting. Separating contributions also makes repair local: a failed compatibility check need not erase a valid calculation, and an invalid task need not become a diagnosis of its participant.

### HCD.11:11 - SoTA-Echoing

**Working question:** how can an assessment support a claim about a person's contribution when tasks, assistance and later use differ?

**Selected line — adopt and adapt.** The [2014 Testing Standards, Chapter 1](https://www.testingstandards.net/uploads/7/6/6/4/76643089/9780935302356.pdf) tie evidence to an intended interpretation and use. [Expanded evidence-centred design](https://www.frontiersin.org/journals/psychology/articles/10.3389/fpsyg.2019.00853/full) connects tasks and observations to inferences and makes learning during assessment relevant. HCD.11 adapts that line into the short chain in §4.2 and the separate attempts in §5.1.

**Serious alternative:** score the final work product with one rubric. That is cheaper when the product's origin is settled and the inference concerns only that product. Where help or a non-compensable criterion changes the human claim, the same rubric can give a confident wrong answer. Keep its useful criterion-based scoring, but reject the substitution of total score for the intended contribution.

The [AI tutoring experiment by Bastani and colleagues](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/40560616/) adds a bounded counterexample: assisted practice gains in high-school mathematics did not guarantee later unassisted performance. It supports keeping support conditions visible, not an adult-workplace effect estimate or a ban on AI.

The selected line changes §4.3's observation of help and §4.4's separate judgements, at the cost of only the discriminating evidence needed for the use. These sources guide design; they do not validate this local assessment. Reopen the task or inference when local evidence shows that it rewards a proxy, misses a material error or attributes a tool's contribution to the person.

### HCD.11:12 - Relations

HCD.1 can supply the human-demand and later-Work frame; equivalent qualified input permits direct entry. HCD.3 diagnoses a limiting human target when the evidence needs a causal differential rather than another score. HCD.4 can use compatible new contribution evidence in a profile comparison.

HCD.12 tests material unfamiliar variation; HCD.13 tests delay and support dependence; HCD.14 can revise an affected development assumption from this result alone. E.23.CAE supplies a bounded differential among applicability, access, adaptation, enactment and capability change when that distinction changes the next action. Target-domain Methods and specialist results continue to supply correctness and criticality.

Use C.16 for an actual measurement claim, including its scale, method and comparability basis, and B.3 for a stronger assurance claim when required. This Method introduces neither a universal mastery score nor a new decision authority.

### HCD.11:End

<a id="hcd-12"></a>
## HCD.12 — Test Transfer to Unfamiliar Work

> **Type:** Human Capability Development practitioner method pattern
> **Status:** Current practitioner preview

### HCD.12:1 - Problem Frame

**Use this when** a person has demonstrated a contribution in familiar work and someone wants to rely on it after a meaningful change in task, data, tool, setting or coordination. Begin with the earlier performance and name what is unfamiliar to this particular person. A compatible HCD.11 result or equivalent qualified performance evidence is enough to enter.

The first useful result is a **bounded transfer account**: which contribution survived which change, what required adaptation or help, and what remains untested. It lets a practitioner preserve familiar success while addressing a specific transfer gap.

This Method concerns transfer of an already demonstrated contribution. It can also organize a separately declared test of learning from a new source, but that result must remain distinct. Do not call a renamed exercise unfamiliar, infer transfer from course completion, or use an unknown baseline as if prior capability had been demonstrated. When the earlier contribution is uncertain, assess it first through HCD.11.

A delayed test of otherwise comparable work is primarily an HCD.13 question. Actual deployment, release, safety and employment reliance retain their own evidence and authority conditions.

### HCD.12:2 - Problem

Familiar success can depend on the exercise's wording, a named Method, a teacher's cue or one repeated answer. Under new conditions, the person may fail to recognize the problem, fail to find an available response, apply it without adaptation, or reach an inadequate result. Those failures ask for different next actions.

A transfer test can also reward a new rigid habit. Someone who has learned to reject mismatching evidence may now reject complete evidence as well. A test containing only traps cannot show that the person can make a justified positive move when the conditions allow it.

### HCD.12:3 - Forces

| Force | Working tension |
| --- | --- |
| Meaningful novelty and comparability | Change what could defeat copied execution while keeping the target contribution recognizable. |
| Recognition and assistance | Observe selection before a cue when that is required, while preserving sources and tools that belong to the work. |
| Challenge and fairness | Test consequential change without importing an unrelated prerequisite or inaccessible interface. |
| Positive action and restraint | Require justified action with sufficient evidence as well as justified refusal when a necessary condition fails. |
| Local evidence and broad ambition | Return useful transfer evidence without turning one unfamiliar case into all-domain adaptability. |

### HCD.12:4 - Solution

#### HCD.12:4.1 - Recover the prior claim and the meaningful change

Recover the same person's earlier contribution, task, conditions, permitted support, criterion and observations. Identify relevant prior exposure to the new task or response. If familiarity is uncertain, keep that uncertainty in the result.

Describe the material change and why the old answer might now fail. Examples include evidence from a different configuration, reduced capacity, a different tool with the same required result, or a new specialist interface. A change of names, numbers that leave the decisive reasoning untouched, or formatting alone is weak evidence of unfamiliarity.

Preserve the target requirement or explicitly identify its change. If the new task requires another professional Method or prerequisite, obtain that contribution or report the new requirement; do not diagnose failed transfer of the old contribution merely because the task now asks for something else.

#### HCD.12:4.2 - Design a discriminating unfamiliar task

Choose a task the person has not rehearsed in its decision-bearing form. State the changed fact, the response it should change, the criterion and the allowed means. Ask how a copied answer, an indiscriminate refusal and a lucky guess would fare.

Where the work requires both action and restraint, include sufficient-evidence and insufficient-evidence cases. A useful small comparison may be one condition that supports action and another that changes its limit. Broader transfer claims need coverage of the material variation they include; a single convenient task cannot supply it.

Keep irrelevant difficulty controlled where possible. If both the tool and the work problem change, either explain why that combined condition is the intended question or separate the changes enough to interpret failure.

Before use, have the domain criteria and expected consequences available. A task whose own correct answer is disputed cannot settle the person's transfer.

#### HCD.12:4.3 - Observe the first attempt under its declared conditions

When independent recognition is part of the target, present the work problem before naming the Method, PatternID or decisive feature. The practitioner may use the ordinary sources and tools specified by the target. Independent recognition here means the required selection was not supplied; it does not mean working without every external resource.

Keep the following distinctions only as far as observations support them:

| Contribution | A discriminating observation |
| --- | --- |
| Recognition | The person identifies the relevant difficulty before a diagnostic cue supplies it. |
| Access | The person retrieves or finds a usable response with the permitted resources. |
| Adaptation | The person changes the response when a material condition changes. |
| Enactment | The person carries out the response sufficiently to judge the contribution. |
| Result | The resulting action or product meets the domain criteria, including relevant limits and consequences. |

An incorrect final answer alone may not distinguish these contributions. Use the smallest explanatory observation or qualified differential that would change the next action. Do not force every attempt into five confident labels.

After recording the first attempt, feedback can support learning. Record what it supplied, and keep the corrected attempt separate. A Method supplied by the assessor can support application evidence; it cannot demonstrate the preceding independent selection.

#### HCD.12:4.4 - Return the transferred contribution and its boundary

Compare the new observations with the earlier claim under the named change. Preserve supported contributions even when another contribution fails.

State the tested change, independent and helped observations, failed criteria, uncertainty, untested variations and the next observation needed to strengthen the claim. Use “demonstrated in this unfamiliar case” when that is the scope. Do not use “transfers” without saying what transferred where.

If performance returns after a cue, report the cue-dependent result. A fresh uncued case is needed before strengthening an independent-recognition or adaptation claim. If the new task or access was defective, return that problem instead of treating the result as human failure.

Stop when the intended practice or support decision is supported. Stronger reliance may require several unfamiliar cases, independent assessment, a defensible coverage argument and checks of domain correctness and access. A failed transfer case does not erase the demonstrated familiar envelope.

Reopen only the contribution affected by a new variation, changed source or tool, corrected task, or stronger receiving use.

#### HCD.12:4.5 - Separate learning from a new source when that is the intention

Sometimes the useful question is how well a person learns a new Method during the task. State that intention before the test. Identify the newly supplied contribution, the exact source and the opportunity to learn from it. Distinguish finding the source from learning with equal access to it.

Observe prior familiarity, initial recognition, first application after source access, adaptation to a material change, help, time, material errors, corrections and unfinished work. Test the new Method's own contribution; familiar arithmetic or an already known comparison is not evidence of acquiring a different whole Method.

A better first application after reading the new source supports that declared learning task. It is not retention of an already learned Method. A “faster learning at unchanged correctness” claim additionally needs comparable conditions, a suitable comparison and actual observations; shorter completion time alone cannot supply it.

### HCD.12:5 - Archetypal Grounding

#### HCD.12:5.1 - Transfer requires a positive proposal and a changed resource answer

Learner-L17 and the following observations are fictional. They illustrate the Method and provide no data about Engineer-E27 or actual programme participants.

Earlier qualified observations show L17 checking configuration evidence and explaining a supported continuation in two fresh cases without a teacher's configuration cue. In those cases, L17 rejected a report about FW7 when HW3/FW8 was required. Calculation and the 23-hour C continuation were also supported. Those familiar contributions are not themselves new transfer evidence. The earlier observations do not yet demonstrate adaptation to changed capacity.

The new case has a report that actually supports HW3/FW8/SCHED2 and confirms the required checks. Preparation takes one hour per homogeneous batch of up to four; required checks take two hours per change. Twelve changes arrive per week. At 24 available hours, ten changes require 23 hours, while twelve require 27. The release manager may authorize a bounded ten-change use and retains responsibility for the growing queue and release decision.

For this constructed comparison, L17's task history stipulates that the standalone evidence-use question—a complete report offered without a preceding mismatch problem—has not been rehearsed. That changed question must now lead to a justified positive proposal; repeating the earlier FW7 refusal would be wrong. The task does not ask for a pattern name. Reports, calculator and ordinary source and tool access are available. The source and assistant traces place L17's first recognition and proposal before any teacher or AI cue supplies the decisive condition.

| Observation | What it supports | What it does not support |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Before any PatternID or configuration cue, L17 checks the complete report and proposes ten changes in three homogeneous batches, requiring 23 hours, with two new changes left in the weekly queue. | Transfer of configuration-evidence use and a bounded positive proposal in this unfamiliar case. | Release authority, elimination of the queue or general transfer across all engineering work. |
| The assessor changes only available time from 24 to 22 hours. L17 initially keeps the ten-change promise. | A demonstrated adaptation failure under the changed resource condition. | A conclusion that the earlier configuration or calculation contribution has disappeared. |
| After feedback asking L17 to check the changed time constraint, L17 calculates that nine changes require 18 + 3 = 21 hours, while ten still require 23, and revises the proposal. | Correct adaptation with feedback. | Independent resource adaptation on the first changed-condition attempt. |

The next useful return is local: preserve the supported finance and configuration evidence, identify capacity adaptation as unproven independently, and arrange a fresh uncued variation before stronger reliance. HCD.14 can use this result immediately to propose a small practice revision; a retention test is not a prerequisite.

An assessor who accepts “the evidence is never sufficient, so I refuse” would miss the positive-action criterion in the first row. An assessor who scores only the corrected final answer would miss the independent adaptation failure in the second.

#### HCD.12:5.2 - The named Method supplies what the test meant to observe

In another constructed task, a person makes no relevant first move but produces a correct comparison immediately after the assessor says which Method to use. The result supports application after that cue. It leaves uncued recognition unresolved or failed according to the original task criterion; it does not establish global capability loss.

If the practical question is application with a routinely supplied Method, that result may already be useful. If the work requires the person to select the Method, the next task must preserve that selection problem.

### HCD.12:6 - Bias-Annotation

Unfamiliarity is person-relative. A task familiar to the assessor may be novel to the learner, and a nominally new task may have been rehearsed elsewhere. Retain relevant exposure instead of treating cohort membership as proof.

Do not make “caution” a universal success criterion. A person who refuses every case may avoid one error while failing the required useful action. Also check that apparent adaptation is not merely the assessor naming the changed condition.

### HCD.12:7 - Conformance Checklist

- Prior qualified performance belongs to the same person and contribution.
- The material change and person-relative unfamiliarity are recoverable.
- The task preserves the target or explicitly declares the new requirement.
- Positive action and refusal are distinguished where both matter.
- Required selection is observed before the Method or cue supplies it.
- Allowed sources, tools, help and first versus corrected attempts remain visible.
- The conclusion states exactly what transferred, failed or remains untested.
- New-source learning, delayed retention and deployment authority are not inferred from the transfer result.

### HCD.12:8 - Common Anti-Patterns and How to Avoid Them

| Anti-pattern | Better move |
| --- | --- |
| “The names changed, so this is transfer.” | Show which unfamiliar condition could make the old response wrong. |
| “Every test contains a hidden error.” | Include complete evidence that requires a justified positive action. |
| “I named the Method, and the person selected it.” | Report application after a cue; preserve selection as a separate question. |
| “The changed case failed, so the old capability is gone.” | Preserve the old envelope and locate the failed adaptation or missing prerequisite. |
| “Reading a new source proves retention.” | Report learning with that source and its supplied contribution. |

### HCD.12:9 - Consequences

The practitioner gets a precise next target rather than a general judgement that someone “cannot transfer.” A well-chosen small case can distinguish rigid repetition, cue dependence and useful adaptation without a large new examination.

The cost is explicit novelty and criterion design. Broader variation needs more evidence, and some failures remain ambiguous. That ambiguity is preferable to prescribing the wrong intervention. More refusals or a higher final score can be worse when they hide loss of justified action or increased dependence on feedback.

### HCD.12:10 - Rationale

Transfer is a relation between demonstrated contributions and changed conditions, not a property conferred by finishing a course. Preserving the prior claim makes both success and failure informative. Keeping recognition, access, adaptation and result separable shows which new observation could change the conclusion without pretending that every failed answer reveals its cause.

### HCD.12:11 - SoTA-Echoing

**Working question:** how can an unfamiliar task distinguish useful transfer from repeated execution, while allowing learning resources when the intended work uses them?

**Selected line — adopt and adapt.** The [workplace-instruction review by Kraiger and Ford](https://www.annualreviews.org/content/journals/10.1146/annurev-orgpsych-012420-060109) distinguishes instructional activity, learning and later transfer. HCD.12 turns that distinction into the prior-claim and changed-condition comparison in §4.1–§4.4, with the action-versus-refusal test in §5.1.

**Serious alternatives:** repeat the same exercise under new labels, or remove all resources to avoid assistance. Repetition is useful for practising execution but leaves consequential adaptation untested. A resource-free test is useful for an independently required core but can omit the ability to learn with a source. At comparable task time, a decision-bearing variation gives a more relevant answer than cosmetic novelty; source access remains a declared condition rather than a hidden advantage.

The historical [Schwartz and Martin preparation-for-future-learning study](https://aaalab.stanford.edu/assets/papers/2004/Inventing_to_prepare_for_future_learning.pdf) showed why assessment with a later learning resource can distinguish outcomes missed by a resource-free test in ninth-grade statistics. HCD.12 adapts that design distinction in §4.5. It does not use that study as evidence that a particular adult programme accelerates learning.

This selected line requires a task whose criteria discriminate the contribution, not an automatic claim of transfer. Reopen the comparison if prior exposure defeats the novelty claim, the variant changes an unacknowledged prerequisite, or source access and feedback cannot be separated from the claimed independent contribution.

### HCD.12:12 - Relations

HCD.11 supplies prior performance evidence; equivalent qualified evidence permits direct entry. HCD.13 concerns delay and support dependence rather than novelty alone. HCD.14 can use a transfer limitation to revise an affected development assumption without waiting for every other assessment. HCD.3 owns diagnosis of a limiting human target when the cause remains decision-changing.

E.23.CAE can supply a qualified differential among recognition, access, adaptation, enactment and capability change. A recovery after a cue or restored condition has only that differential meaning; it is not proof of transfer beyond the tested case.

Target-domain Methods govern the actual response and correctness criteria. A learning-product designer owns the concrete tasks, timing and instrument; this Method supplies the transfer and new-source-learning distinctions that the design must preserve.

### HCD.12:End

<a id="hcd-13"></a>
## HCD.13 — Test Retention and Tool Dependence

> **Type:** Human Capability Development practitioner method pattern
> **Status:** Current practitioner preview

### HCD.13:1 - Problem Frame

**Use this when** earlier success is being relied upon after an interval, or when the person now performs differently with changed tools or support. Begin with a qualified earlier observation of the same person's contribution and the conditions under which it succeeded. Then separate the delay question from the support question.

The first useful result is a **retention and support-dependence account**: what remains demonstrable after which interval and practice history, under which support, and what any apparent loss does or does not show. The practical gain is to avoid both assuming that earlier success lasts indefinitely and prescribing relearning for an access or configuration failure.

Retention concerns an already demonstrated contribution. If that earlier performance was never established, assess the current contribution through HCD.11. Use HCD.12 when the central question is unfamiliar work. If a known missing source or failed tool already explains the current inability to perform, return that arrangement problem to its owner before diagnosing loss.

Source-dependent work can remain well performed with its sources. An independently available core is a separately justified demand, not the person's only “real” capability. Stronger safety, credentialing, employment or deployment reliance requires the relevant domain evaluation and assurance.

### HCD.13:2 - Problem

A delayed failure can reflect many changes besides loss of capability: no recent practice, different tasks, inaccessible sources, unfamiliar tools, missing cues, fatigue, or a changed criterion. Testing only after a delay while also removing support confounds the two questions.

A successful delayed test can also be overread. Continued use may have maintained the contribution; a warm-up or earlier probe may have taught or primed the answer; a short core may cover only a few contributions. None of those observations establishes retention without practice, whole-course mastery or transfer to other work.

### HCD.13:3 - Forces

| Force | Working tension |
| --- | --- |
| Realistic delay and feasible observation | Use the interval that matters for work while accounting for time, assessor and access costs. |
| Retention and continued development | Describe intervening practice and instruction instead of treating every elapsed week as non-use. |
| Equipped work and independent contribution | Preserve required means while testing a separately justified reduced-support target. |
| Differential evidence and probe reactivity | A reference return can distinguish rivals, but the probe can itself teach, prime or fatigue the person. |
| Useful local claims and causal restraint | Return what the comparison supports without inventing a memory mechanism or programme effect. |

### HCD.13:4 - Solution

#### HCD.13:4.1 - Recover the earlier contribution and the intended interval

Recover the person, contribution, criterion, earlier observations, relevant task variation, allowed means and evidence window. A remembered impression that “the person could do it” is not a qualified baseline. If the earlier evidence cannot support the intended comparison, assess now and establish an honest current starting point.

Choose the interval from the work: how long might this contribution be unused, what support will remain available, and what consequence would a failure have? There is no universal retention interval. An infrequent high-consequence task may need a different observation and assurance basis from a routine task.

Recover intervening use, practice, instruction, source changes, tool use and relevant uncertainty. Report continuing practice as continuing practice. An uncertain history narrows the conclusion rather than becoming an assumed period of non-use.

#### HCD.13:4.2 - Compare delay under reference conditions

Specify a delayed task that elicits the same contribution at a comparable demand and criterion. Use the earlier qualified support conditions, or explain the deliberate change and its effect on the inference. Preserve task access and distinguish material differences from harmless surface variation.

Observe the delayed attempt before remedial teaching when the retention question needs that observation. Keep help, incomplete work, material errors and corrections separate. If the criterion or required source has changed, assess the affected new requirement rather than interpreting every difference as human decline.
