The direct comparison is earlier versus delayed performance under the stated reference conditions. It can support retention in that tested envelope and history. It does not isolate the cause of the result from every intervening influence.

#### HCD.13:4.3 - Test support dependence as a separate question

State which support matters and why. A tool contrast is useful when the person must sometimes contribute without that tool, when a fallback is required, or when the assessor needs to distinguish the person's contribution from assisted output.

For a reduced-support core, specify its own contribution, data, allowed means, time and criteria. Preserve required accessibility support. Do not remove a current technical source merely to make a source-dependent task appear independent.

A minimum interpretable comparison distinguishes:

| Comparison | What it can answer |
| --- | --- |
| Earlier and delayed attempts with relevant reference support | What remains demonstrable after the interval under that support and recorded history. |
| Matched current attempts with a decision-bearing support difference | What the observed performance depends on in that contrast. |
| Only a delayed attempt with support also withdrawn | What happened in that combined condition; delay and withdrawal are not separately identified. |

Control order, cueing, rehearsal and fatigue as far as the receiving use requires. Use fresh comparable material or separate occasions when an earlier task could supply the answer. Record unavoidable order effects; repeated testing is not inert. A positive independent core supports only its stated contribution, not all work previously performed with a tool.

#### HCD.13:4.4 - Investigate apparent loss only when the distinction changes the next action

First check that the present demand is inside the earlier claim. Return an outside-envelope demand or known configuration failure directly.

When at least two live explanations would lead to different development or support work, and probing is safe and informative, use a qualified E.23.CAE differential. Recover a previously successful or separately qualified reference condition without further teaching. Vary the smallest condition that distinguishes the rivals. A reference–changed-condition–reference comparison can be useful when order or transition history matters.

Observe whether the person recognizes the applicable response, can access it, adapts it, enacts it and achieves the required result only where the evidence distinguishes those contributions. Do not infer every earlier failure from an incorrect final product.

If the response reappears without relearning under the qualified reference condition, a simple global-loss explanation is weakened in those conditions. That recovery does not prove unfamiliar transfer or identify a memory mechanism. If the probe teaches the answer, support changes cannot be separated, or safety prevents the contrast, return the unresolved distinction.

A warranted claim of changed capability requires holder-specific evidence after the relevant envelope and configuration rivals have been addressed. A single unsuccessful return probe is not enough by itself.

#### HCD.13:4.5 - Return separate conclusions and a practical next move

Keep the following separately reportable: delayed performance under reference support; performance in any independent core; observed support dependence; a supported access, adaptation or enactment difficulty; possible loss or warranted revision of a capability claim; and unresolved or omitted tests.

State the interval, practice history, conditions, observations, inference and limits. The next useful action may be ordinary continued use, a targeted observation, access repair, a revision hypothesis through HCD.14 or a direct specialist return. The evidence account does not choose every later action on the person's behalf.

Stop at the smallest supported conclusion. For stronger reliance, obtain the relevant baseline quality, task comparability, observation coverage, independent judgement and protection against probe-induced learning. Extra follow-up time, access and assessor effort need an actual arrangement; specifying an eight-week check does not obtain them.

Reopen only the affected claim when delay, use history, source, tool, task, criterion or intended reliance changes.

### HCD.13:5 - Archetypal Grounding

#### HCD.13:5.1 - A matched eight-week comparison with an independent core

Learner-L17 and all observations in this example are constructed. The example stipulates a separately resourced follow-up with an assessor and source access. It neither schedules an actual programme visit nor supplies observations of Engineer-E27 or a learner cohort.

At reference time T0, a qualified assessment records three selected contributions associated with F.1, C.16 and C.11. The engineering tasks require HW3/FW8. A supplier report for FW7 cannot justify FW8; a complete HW3/FW8/SCHED2 report can support the stated configuration. SCHED2 requires two hours of checks per change and one hour of preparation per homogeneous batch of up to four. Ten changes require 23 hours, twelve require 27; at 24 available hours, a ten-change proposal leaves two of twelve new changes in the weekly queue. At 22 hours, nine changes require 21 and ten cannot be promised.

The existing alternatives are A, a second stand still requiring a shared-power check; B, the supplier option with the FW7 report; and C, the compatible SCHED2 arrangement with the stated capacity limit. Supplied financial assumptions give A/B/C approximate NPVs of 48.9/51.6/44.6 thousand. B's financial rank cannot settle its compatibility.

Two stipulated weekly logs each record ten accepted changes, all required checks completed and twelve arrivals; together they show four additional queued changes. Those observed counts and periods remain distinct from the modelled 23-hour requirement. The source set contains these logs, the exact configuration reports and the calculation assumptions. The release manager is the chooser for release/use; L17 prepares a bounded recommendation. These are local exercise facts.

The delayed assessment is eight weeks after T0. The constructed history records two intervening source-selection uses and one comparison/decision-support exercise, with no rehearsal of the exact follow-up cases. Thus the question is retention under intermittent practice, not eight weeks of non-use.

| Selected contribution | Qualified observation at T0 | Constructed observation eight weeks later |
| --- | --- | --- |
| F.1: select sources that can change the current answer | L17 retains the exact configuration report and relevant resource/throughput evidence, explains each contribution, and excludes a general product brochure from the compatibility answer. | With a fresh comparable case and normal source access, L17 again selects the answer-changing sources and preserves the report's configuration limit. |
| C.16: preserve units, conditions and the distinction between a modelled quantity and an observed result | L17 distinguishes modelled hours per proposed batch plan from observed changes per week, and refuses to treat the former as measured throughput. | L17 preserves units, observation periods and the same distinction; a missing observed value remains unknown. |
| C.11: support a bounded comparison of existing options | L17 identifies the release manager as chooser, compares the given options against current compatibility and time conditions, and states the remaining check or queue consequence. | L17 gives the corresponding bounded recommendation without replacing a failed constraint by financial rank. |

These are selected contributions, not a demonstration of the whole F.1, C.16 or C.11 Method envelope.

At both T0 and follow-up, a separate fifteen-minute core uses supplied tables and a calculator but no AI. It asks L17 to recover the required configuration, notice an unsupported report, and reconstruct a feasible time-limited proposal. In both constructed observations, L17 distinguishes the configuration and time constraints and explains why ten cannot be promised at 22 hours.

The core runs before the source-dependent task on different material, with no corrective teaching until both are complete. The report retains that order. It supports the narrow core under those conditions and the later source-dependent performance after that core; it is not evidence that task order never matters. A receiving use requiring an entirely unwarmed first attempt would need that observation.

The result is retention of the compared contributions under the stated eight-week history and support conditions, plus the separately tested core. It supports neither untested contributions nor retention without practice, and does not establish an actual learner result or a causal effect of the development arrangement.

#### HCD.13:5.2 - A failed source link changes the arrangement result

During an additional constructed source-dependent task, the required technical source is inaccessible. The earlier qualified contribution depended on using that source. The assessor can verify the access failure directly.

Return source access to the responsible provider and leave the dependent performance judgement incomplete. There is no reason to run a memory-loss diagnosis to explain a known missing input. If a later restored-access attempt is observed, report it under the restored condition without retroactively completing the interrupted attempt.

#### HCD.13:5.3 - Recovery after a cue narrows an apparent-loss claim

In a separate constructed comparison, the person does not select a familiar Method for the current problem. Without teaching its content, the assessor supplies a previously qualified task cue; the person then produces the familiar response. If that cue is a legitimate reference condition and the contrast addresses the live rival, the result supports availability under that condition and a possible selection difficulty.

It does not show uncued selection, unfamiliar transfer or the mechanism of remembering. If supplying the cue also explains the solution, the no-relearning comparison fails and the loss-versus-access distinction remains unresolved.

### HCD.13:6 - Bias-Annotation

An assessor may prefer a simple story of forgetting or resilience. Resist it when support, access, practice or task conditions changed. People with required assistive technology should not receive a lower capability judgement merely because the assessment removed it.

Repeated probes can favour people who learn from the test itself. Record relevant practice and order effects rather than interpreting every later improvement as stable retention.

### HCD.13:7 - Conformance Checklist

- Earlier qualified evidence concerns the same person and contribution.
- The chosen interval and intervening practice history are explicit.
- Delay and support questions have separate interpretable comparisons, or the combined-condition limit is stated.
- A reduced-support core has its own justified target and preserves required access arrangements.
- Help, feedback, unfinished work and probe reactivity remain visible.
- Any apparent-loss differential uses a qualified reference and addresses live rivals without hidden teaching.
- Retention, support dependence, access difficulty and capability-loss claims remain distinct.
- The result names tested coverage, unresolved questions, resource needs, stop and reopen conditions.

### HCD.13:8 - Common Anti-Patterns and How to Avoid Them

| Anti-pattern | Better move |
| --- | --- |
| “Eight weeks passed, so this is retention without practice.” | Recover the actual use history and qualify the conclusion. |
| “The delayed no-tool test failed, so the person forgot.” | Separate delay from support withdrawal or report the combined condition. |
| “The answer returned after coaching, so there was no loss.” | Distinguish a qualified no-relearning return from newly taught performance. |
| “The fifteen-minute core proves the whole course.” | State its selected contributions and preserve untested coverage. |
| “The follow-up is in the plan, so resources exist.” | Obtain time, assessor and access before the actual observation. |

### HCD.13:9 - Consequences

The practitioner can preserve supported performance, repair access and target a genuine uncertainty without restarting development indiscriminately. A short independent core may answer an important fallback question while leaving normal source-dependent work intact.

The cost is a recoverable baseline, practice history and a meaningful comparison. Some distinctions remain unresolved without another observation. A cleaner-looking “retention score” is worse if it hides tool withdrawal, teaching during the probe or a narrower tested contribution.

### HCD.13:10 - Rationale

Elapsed time is a condition of the comparison, not proof of its cause. Support dependence is likewise not synonymous with a deficit: it may be appropriate to the required work. Separating the questions makes the next action local, while a qualified reference return prevents apparent loss from automatically becoming a relearning prescription.

### HCD.13:11 - SoTA-Echoing

**Working question:** what can a delayed assessment show when practice and support can also change performance?

**Selected line — adopt and adapt.** [Carpenter, Pan and Butler's review](https://www.nature.com/articles/s44159-022-00089-1) supports spacing and retrieval as bounded learning approaches; the [classroom-testing meta-analysis by Yang and colleagues](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/33683913/) reinforces that testing conditions can affect learning. HCD.13 therefore records intervening practice and probe reactivity in §4.1–§4.4 instead of treating a test as a passive reading.

**Serious alternative:** compare an earlier assisted score with one delayed unassisted score. It is inexpensive and can describe that combined condition, but it cannot distinguish delay from assistance withdrawal. The selected line keeps a reference-support comparison and adds only the support contrast needed for the decision. Its extra cost is explicit; an unaffordable contrast returns a narrower result rather than a false loss claim.

The [Bastani and colleagues AI study](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/40560616/) motivates the support distinction with school-mathematics evidence, not adult occupational or long-retention evidence. The exact mutation is §4.3's separate independent-core target and §5.1's two reports. E.23.CAE supplies the reference-return discipline when an apparent-loss differential is actually needed.

No cited source supplies a universal interval, a local programme effect or the truth of this fictional case. Reopen the conclusion if task comparability, practice history, required access or teaching during the probe changes the inference.

### HCD.13:12 - Relations

HCD.11 supplies or recovers the prior and current performance evidence. HCD.12 concerns meaningful unfamiliarity, which may coexist with delay but needs its own claim. HCD.14 can use a retention or support finding to revise the affected development assumption.

E.23.CAE governs a qualified apparent-loss differential; a known configuration failure returns to A.15.8 and the direct owner instead. E.23.CDI supplies the capability-development evidence distinction under its holder and Work-family conditions; a retained response does not by itself establish an achieved development intervention.

Target-domain criteria and specialist results remain necessary for correctness. The programme or instrument owner selects and resources particular visits and tasks. Measurement, causal and stronger assurance claims require their own applicable specifications and evidence; this Method creates no common resilience score.

### HCD.13:End

<a id="hcd-14"></a>
## HCD.14 — Revise the Development Arrangement from Evidence

> **Type:** Human Capability Development practitioner method pattern
> **Status:** Current practitioner preview

### HCD.14:1 - Problem Frame

**Use this when** an observation of performance, unfamiliar transfer, delayed performance or support dependence changes an assumption behind a person's development arrangement. Begin with the affected observation and ask which part of the arrangement it calls into question.

The first useful result is a **bounded revision hypothesis**: a specific change to the arrangement, the contribution it is expected to improve, what remains protected, and the next observation that could retain, adapt or reject the change. A compatible result from HCD.11, HCD.12 or HCD.13 can be sufficient; the three are not obligatory stages.

An arrangement includes the tasks, practice, feedback, sources, tools, provider and other conditions through which development is attempted. The Method revises the smallest justified part of that arrangement. It does not treat every disappointing result as a reason to rebuild a course or select a new life or career direction.

Do not proceed from a score with no recoverable contribution or observation. Obtain the missing evidence first. If the live question is clinical treatment, professional correctness, safety, employment action or an unavailable domain Method, return it to the responsible specialist or owner. A new source edition alone does not establish deterioration in the person.

### HCD.14:2 - Problem

A development plan embodies hypotheses: this task will elicit the needed contribution, this feedback will help, this support will remain available, and this practice will prepare the person for later work. Evidence can overturn one hypothesis while leaving the others usable.

Two failures are common. One repeats the same exercise more often without identifying the discrepancy. The other redesigns everything, losing supported contributions and spending resources without evidence that the whole arrangement was at fault. Both can conceal a defective assessment, a cue that supplied the answer, or an unavailable source behind a claim that the person needs more training.

### HCD.14:3 - Forces

| Force | Working tension |
| --- | --- |
| Action and causal uncertainty | Make a small testable improvement when useful without pretending that one miss establishes its cause. |
| Local repair and shared causes | Preserve working components while enlarging the change when evidence shows that several failures share a cause. |
| Learning and assessment | Feedback can be useful during practice but must not silently supply the contribution later being assessed. |
| Professional Method and local tailoring | Adapt a qualified development Method to the case without inventing expertise from a pattern label. |
| Improvement and protection | A gain in speed or score is unacceptable if it damages a required contribution, access or resource limit. |

### HCD.14:4 - Solution

#### HCD.14:4.1 - Recover the assumption that the evidence changed

Name the person, intended later Work, human target, affected observation and receiving decision. Identify the arrangement component expected to help and the assumption now in doubt.

Use a concrete discrepancy: “the person applies the capacity rule after a cue but repeats the old promise when available time changes,” not “transfer is weak.” Preserve successful contributions and the conditions under which they were observed.

If the evidence and target concern different people, work families, configurations or periods, qualify their relation before using the result. A cohort average cannot by itself identify this person's limiting contribution.

#### HCD.14:4.2 - Compare only causes that change the revision

Ask what else could explain the discrepancy and what different action each explanation would imply.

| Live explanation | Different useful next move |
| --- | --- |
| The target or later-Work demand changed | Return the changed demand to its owner; preserve the old claim within its former conditions. |
| The task, criterion or scoring is defective | Repair the assessment and obtain a discriminating observation before revising learning from that result. |
| Required source, tool, time or access was absent | Repair the arrangement with the direct owner; do not infer a human deficit from the missing input. |
| A cue supplied recognition or adaptation | Change the cue/practice relation and test the required contribution before a cue supplies it. |
| The development Method or provider does not fit the target | Obtain a compatible Method or qualified provider result; change that component rather than merely increasing repetitions. |
| A limiting human capability, misconception or behaviour is supported | Use the supported target to select a qualified local development change; retain unresolved rivals. |

A short inspection may settle a known missing source or incorrect criterion. Use HCD.3 when the limiting human target remains unresolved. Use a qualified E.23.CAE differential when access, selection, adaptation, enactment or apparent loss can be distinguished and the distinction changes the next action. Do not add a causal investigation when the current evidence already supports an affordable reversible test and no live rival changes its safe scope.

A small testable revision may follow one well-observed miss. Label it a hypothesis, retain the rival explanation and choose a test that can disconfirm it. Evidence too weak even for that bounded change returns a specific observation to obtain.

#### HCD.14:4.3 - Specify the positive revised arrangement

State the component to change and the replacement in executable terms. For example, replace a cue-led worked example with two contrasting practice cases and feedback on the person's own first explanation, then test a fresh uncued variant. “Improve transfer” or “use HCD.5” is not a revision.

Select the professional development Method from an applicable qualified source or a compatible direct provider result. Check that its target, learner conditions, prerequisites, support and limits fit this use. If the Method or provider competence is missing, return that exact input. An unavailable HCD.5, HCD.6, HCD.9 or HCD.10 body supplies no Method by implication; an already qualified direct Method can still support a local revision.

State what must not change: supported contributions, required checks, accessibility arrangements, source access, permitted tools, time and cost limits, or specialist responsibilities. A change to the target itself needs the target owner's decision.

#### HCD.14:4.4 - Pair the change with a test and stopping rule

Name the expected observable difference and the fresh task or other observation that could show it. Include a meaningful failure condition and an observation that would point to a rival cause.

The test should elicit the affected contribution without reproducing the cue the person is supposed to supply. Preserve domain criteria and compare first performance separately from feedback-assisted correction. Check protected contributions as well as the intended gain.

State acceptable learner and practitioner cost, resources, timing and any delayed observation actually needed for the claim. Do not add a retention visit merely to complete a sequence. When delay matters, HCD.13 supplies that comparison under its own resources and evidence conditions.

Specify what to retain, adapt, abandon or return after the observation. A local before/after comparison can guide the next reversible revision; an estimate of causal effectiveness needs a suitable causal design and evidence beyond this comparison.

#### HCD.14:4.5 - Distinguish proposal, authorization and performed change

Return the revision as a proposal when the practitioner lacks the mandate or resources to carry it out. Name the actual owner of the resource, provider, target or authorization gap and what that owner must supply.

Within an obtaining mandate, the responsible practitioner can make the bounded change and then observe its result. Keep the selected proposal, authorized arrangement, performed development work and observed outcome distinct. A plan and a changed document do not establish that a person practised or learned.

Stop if the change would breach a protected condition or exceed the mandate. Use a safe restoration of the prior working component when needed; do not continue simply to obtain a cleaner comparison.

#### HCD.14:4.6 - Update the hypothesis from the new observation

Retain the change provisionally when the intended contribution improves under the test conditions without unacceptable loss. Adapt it when the result narrows the useful conditions or identifies a more local problem. Abandon or return it when the decisive prediction fails, a protected contribution worsens or the professional basis no longer fits.

Preserve unchanged claims and surviving rivals. Reopen only the assumptions affected by new evidence. Enlarge the revision when a changed demand or several independent observations show that the problem is not local; this Method does not require keeping an inadequate component merely because the first proposal was small.

### HCD.14:5 - Archetypal Grounding

#### HCD.14:5.1 - A local revision at the transfer return

Learner-L17 and the evidence are fictional. The case begins at a transfer return, before any delayed test is required. It does not report an actual programme change or learner outcome.

The earlier evidence supports calculation and configuration checking. In a new case, L17 correctly uses a complete HW3/FW8/SCHED2 report and proposes ten changes within 24 available hours. Required checks take two hours per change; preparation takes one hour per homogeneous batch of at most four. Ten changes need 23 hours, twelve need 27, so a ten-change proposal leaves two of twelve new weekly changes in the queue. The release manager retains release/use authority.

When available time changes to 22 hours, L17 initially keeps the ten-change promise and corrects it only after feedback. Nine changes need 21 hours. The calculation and configuration evidence are not erased by this adaptation failure.

The prior practice exercise used a teacher question to point out the changed resource condition. The live hypothesis is that practice did not sufficiently require L17 to detect and adapt to that change before the cue. A single miss does not settle the cause: unclear task wording remains a rival, and general resource reasoning may also need investigation.

For this constructed case, the programme owner has supplied a compatible case-practice Method and a qualified practitioner. The Method asks the learner to attempt a case, explain the consequential choice, compare it with a contrasting case, receive criterion-based feedback, and attempt fresh material. Those are stipulated professional inputs to the example.

The proposed replacement is concrete:

| Element | Revision hypothesis and protection |
| --- | --- |
| Component changed | Replace only the cue-led resource exercise. Leave the supported financial calculation and configuration practice in place. |
| First five minutes | Recover L17's explanation of the earlier promise and inspect whether the task clearly presented the changed time. If it did not, repair the task before interpreting the miss as a practice problem. |
| Twenty minutes of practice | Use contrasting cases requiring a supported ten-change proposal at 24 hours and refusal of that same promise at 22 hours, with a feasible nine-change proposal. Obtain L17's first explanation before feedback names the decisive condition. |
| Ten-minute fresh test | Give unrehearsed 20-hour and 28-hour variants with complete compatible reports and twelve new weekly changes. Do not name the resource Method or point to the changed number. |
| Expected contribution | At 20 hours, eight changes require 18 hours and nine require 21: do not promise nine. At 28 hours, twelve require 27: a proposal to meet those twelve is supported. Preserve required checks, source fit and any existing-queue limitation. |
| Protected conditions | Same source and calculator access, ordinary permitted AI assistance, accessibility arrangements, domain criteria and separate release authority. Observe L17's first recognition and explanation in the source and assistant traces; a teacher or AI cue that supplies recognition of the changed condition counts as help. |
| Cost and status | Forty learner minutes including a final five-minute result discussion, plus fifteen practitioner preparation minutes, within the example's proposed replacement allocation. The return is a proposal until its responsible owner authorizes that use of time. |

The fresh task tests both justified restraint and a justified positive action. It does not award success for repeating the earlier refusal.

#### HCD.14:5.2 - The next observation decides the smallest update

No result of the proposed revision is assumed. The test has the following conditional returns:

| New observation | Update to the hypothesis |
| --- | --- |
| Both fresh uncued first attempts adapt the promise correctly and preserve configuration checking and calculation | Retain the local change provisionally for these tested variants; do not claim a general transfer effect. |
| Correct answers appear only after a teacher or AI cue names the changed resource | Independent adaptation remains unproven; inspect cue dependence and the supported human target before adding more repetitions. |
| The time value is ambiguous or a required source is unavailable | Repair that assessment or access component; the affected attempt cannot judge the intended learning contribution. |
| Adaptation improves but configuration checking or required checks deteriorate | Revise or abandon the change: the intended gain does not compensate for the protected loss. |
| Further qualified observations show several tasks depend on the same missing prerequisite | Enlarge the target or arrangement question through the appropriate diagnostic and Method owner. |

A later HCD.13 result may update the hypothesis if delay or support dependence becomes relevant. It is neither a prerequisite for this first revision nor proof that the revision caused the later result.

### HCD.14:6 - Bias-Annotation

A familiar teaching technique can become the default explanation for every failure. Compare the assessment, support and target rivals before selecting it. Conversely, uncertainty about the final cause should not prevent a clearly bounded, affordable and reversible test.

Protect the learner from a sequence of ever-changing exercises justified only by a rising score. Ask what got worse: independent selection, source access, correctness, workload, confidence in a justified action or a previously supported contribution.

### HCD.14:7 - Conformance Checklist

- The affected observation changes an identifiable development assumption for the same person and work.
- Successful contributions and decision-changing rival causes remain visible.
- The result names a concrete component and an executable replacement, not only another pattern.
- A compatible qualified development Method or exact missing professional input is identified.
- The expected observation, fresh test, cost, protected conditions and stopping rule are explicit.
- Proposal, authorization, performed work and outcome remain distinct.
- Retain, adapt, abandon and return conditions affect the smallest justified claim or component.
- No whole-programme causal effect or new development direction is inferred from a local comparison.

### HCD.14:8 - Common Anti-Patterns and How to Avoid Them

| Anti-pattern | Better move |
| --- | --- |
| “The score fell, so prescribe more practice.” | Recover the contribution and observation, then compare the causes that change the revision. |
| “One miss proves the training Method failed.” | State a bounded hypothesis and a discriminating fresh test. |
| “Replace the whole course.” | Preserve supported components and enlarge the change only when evidence warrants it. |
| “The new plan means the person has developed.” | Separate the proposal, performed work and observed outcome. |
| “The final score rose, so the revision worked.” | Check independent contribution and protected conditions; qualify any causal claim. |

### HCD.14:9 - Consequences

The practitioner returns a usable improvement proposal without pretending that the evidence settled every cause. Local revisions preserve successful learning and make the cost and next observation inspectable.

This approach can require saying that the missing input belongs to the assessor, source owner or provider rather than to the learner. It also accepts provisional results: a useful change may be retained locally while its broader transfer or retention remains untested.

### HCD.14:10 - Rationale

An arrangement is revisable because it embodies claims about how a person will develop for later work. The right unit of repair is the smallest assumption changed by evidence, not automatically the whole programme or the person. An explicit test makes a revision useful even while a causal rival survives, provided the claim, cost and protected conditions remain appropriately bounded.

### HCD.14:11 - SoTA-Echoing

**Working question:** how should performance evidence change development when the cause may lie in the task, feedback, support or learning?

**Selected line — adopt and adapt.** The [Kraiger and Ford workplace-instruction review](https://www.annualreviews.org/content/journals/10.1146/annurev-orgpsych-012420-060109) separates instructional design, learner activity, learning outcomes and work transfer. HCD.14 uses those distinctions to locate the changed assumption in §4.1–§4.3, instead of treating every outcome as a verdict on the entire programme.

**Serious alternative:** repeat or redesign the whole course after a poor result. That is appropriate when the demand or evidence shows a broad mismatch, but wastes supported contributions when only one cue-led exercise is implicated. At comparable decision effort, the selected line returns an executable component change, a test and protected results. Its deliberate limit is that a small trial supports a local update, not general effectiveness.

The [Bastani and colleagues study](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/40560616/) makes assistance design a credible rival in its school-mathematics setting; it supplies no adult-provider prescription. The [Yang and colleagues testing synthesis](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/33683913/) makes testing and feedback conditions relevant to learning. These limits change §4.4 and §5.2: first and corrected attempts stay separate, and a later improvement is not automatically a causal effect.

Reopen the revision if a protected contribution worsens, a rival explanation changes the useful repair, the supplied professional Method does not fit, or broader observations overturn the local scope. The positive proposal in §5.1 remains a hypothesis until its authorized test supplies evidence.

### HCD.14:12 - Relations

HCD.11, HCD.12 and HCD.13 can each supply compatible evidence; none is a compulsory predecessor to the others. HCD.3 supplies a qualified limiting target or non-training return when diagnosis remains unresolved. HCD.4 can use changed contribution evidence in its existing profile comparison.

HCD.5, HCD.6, HCD.9 and HCD.10 concern development Methods, practice, providers or support under their own scope. When their bodies are unavailable, use an already qualified direct professional contribution or return the exact missing Method or provider input. Do not reconstruct those Methods from this revision pattern.

E.23.CAE supplies a useful differential when apparent loss, access or expression is at issue. C.11 governs an actual choice among existing alternatives when its chooser and comparison conditions obtain; the target, resource and programme owners retain their decisions. A causal-effect claim requires an applicable causal evaluation, including C.28 when its question is live.

The Learning Product owner changes the concrete programme or instrument. The person's development direction, commitment, clinical care and employment remain separate questions; a new HCD finding can inform them but does not decide them.

### HCD.14:End

<a id="hcd-15"></a>
## HCD.15 — Sustain Continuing Human Capability Development

> **Type:** Human Capability Development practitioner method pattern
> **Status:** Current practitioner preview

### HCD.15:1 - Problem Frame

**Use this when** one named person's programme has ended, or one development assumption has changed, while the later Work, tools, evidence, qualification conditions, or professional expectations for which the person develops continue to change.

Begin with the person, the later-Work family, and the smallest development question that current evidence can reopen. The first useful result is a **continuing development arrangement**: decision-bearing triggers, real practice and support contributions, evidence and reassessment, protected conditions and burden, owners and authority, and explicit re-entry and stop conditions.

What this buys in practice is responsiveness without ritual repetition. The practitioner can preserve what remains supported, reopen only what changed, and obtain evidence strong enough for the next decision instead of equating annual credits, attendance, community membership, or a booked calendar with continuing capability.

Do not use HCD.15 for a one-off local revision already settled by HCD.14, a whole-programme comparison owned by HCD.2, operation of current Work, organization-holder change, career or employment allocation, clinical treatment, or generic professional-community design. Return those questions to their direct owners.

### HCD.15:2 - Problem

Human capability is conditional. Later Work can change through a new Method, configuration, tool or AI system, a new critical-error finding, a changed support arrangement, an expired qualification window, or evidence that transfer or retention no longer reaches the intended use. Programme completion freezes none of those conditions.

Two failures follow. One treats recurring activity as sufficient: the person renews credits, attends a community, repeats familiar exercises, or keeps tool access, while the contribution required in later Work is no longer tested. The other repeats the entire original programme after every change, discarding supported contributions and adding avoidable cost and burden.

A continuing arrangement must therefore say what can reopen development, which contributions can answer that change, what will be observed, who can act, and when to stop or return. It is a designed arrangement, not proof that development Work occurred or that capability changed.

### HCD.15:3 - Forces

| Force | Working tension |
| --- | --- |
| Continuity and change | Preserve supported capability claims and arrangement components while responding to material changes in later Work. |
| Cadence and evidence | Keep useful opportunities available without letting a calendar become the reason for development. |
| Participation and capability | Use practice, feedback, supervision and communities as contributions while requiring direct evidence for relied-on capability claims. |
| Access and independence | Permit tools, AI and specialist support needed in real Work while separately observing contributions that must survive changed or withdrawn support. |
| Provider service and holder development | Establish provider capability, access and operating conditions without treating provider activity as the holder's development. |
| Improvement and burden | Reassess often enough to protect the later-Work use without imposing unnecessary tests, surveillance, workload, or exclusion. |
| Action and authority | Return a usable arrangement while distinguishing proposal, authorization, performed development Work, later Work, evidence, and decision. |

### HCD.15:4 - Solution

#### HCD.15:4.1 - Fix the holder, later Work, and current claim window

Name the human holder, later-Work family, material configurations and conditions, current capability claim, evidence window, continuing-development decision, and consequences that must be protected. State which correct, safe, or useful Work criteria come from target-domain or specialist owners.

Record the current truth posture. A programme design, prior assessment, credential, community record, MethodDescription, or future Work architecture can frame the question. None establishes current capability or an obtaining later-Work occurrence by itself.

If the holder, Work, evidence, configuration, or receiving decision cannot be recovered, return that exact missing value and the observation or owner that can supply it. Do not create a generic continuing-development plan for an unidentified role population and then apply it to a person by label.

#### HCD.15:4.2 - Recover the smallest affected development question

Use a compatible HCD.14 revision hypothesis or equivalent qualified evidence to identify what can change. Preserve unaffected claims, tasks, feedback, support, provider contributions, accessibility arrangements, and specialist boundaries.

Ask which assumption the new condition defeats. A changed AI configuration may reopen independent challenge while leaving source tracing intact. A failed delayed observation may reopen retention without reopening a demonstrated fresh-task contribution. An unavailable specialist may block one later-Work condition without proving a human deficit.

HCD.14 is one admissible supplier, not a compulsory predecessor. Direct holder- and Work-compatible evidence may open HCD.15. When the available evidence supports only one local revision and no continuing trigger or arrangement question remains, stop at HCD.14.

#### HCD.15:4.3 - Make triggers decision-bearing

Name the changes or observations that can reopen the affected question. Useful triggers include:

- a material change in later Work, its Method, configuration, tool, AI behavior, or specialist interface;
- a critical error, failed unfamiliar transfer, changed delayed performance, or changed support dependence;
- loss or change of provider, source, supervision, access, fallback, protected time, or permitted support;
- expiry of a qualification or evidence window that matters to the receiving decision;
- a source contradiction or target-domain result that changes the relied-on claim; or
- a newly relevant specialist, safety, clinical, legal, employment, or operating result.

Use an interval only when elapsed time changes evidential reach or the opportunity to contribute in later Work. “Every year” is not a trigger unless the year is tied to a real qualification, source, Work, risk, retention, or access condition. Name both the observation and the decision it can change.

#### HCD.15:4.4 - Configure the contributions that can answer the question

Select only contributions needed for the affected question. These may include representative practice opportunities, first-attempt capture, information-bearing feedback, coaching, supervision, graduated access, community participation, current sources, provider and specialist contributions, tools and AI conditions, human checking, accessibility support, fallback, and protected time.

For each contribution, state what it supplies, to whom, under which conditions, at what time and load, and what happens if it is absent. A community needs a relevant shared practice, access, facilitation or coordination, and a stated development use; membership and message volume are not development. A provider label or booked hour is not provider capability or operating service.

Keep development opportunities representative enough to expose the affected contribution. Preserve action and justified refusal, critical errors, source and configuration use, and specialist returns where they matter. Do not make the practice task safer by removing the condition the person must learn to notice.

#### HCD.15:4.5 - Match evidence and reassessment to the receiving use

Specify what will be observed and which inference it may support. Separate the person's first attempt from feedback-assisted correction; familiar performance from unfamiliar transfer; immediate from delayed performance; normal support from changed or withdrawn support; participation from contribution; and individual evidence from cohort or community reports.

Choose the lightest evidence that can change the current decision. A local practice adjustment may need one fresh discriminating attempt. A safety- or qualification-bearing reliance may need representative cases, critical-error coverage, delayed or perturbed-support observations, qualified criteria, and independent checking.

State the reassessment rule before collecting more data: which observation retains, adapts, narrows, reopens, or stops the arrangement; which rival explanation survives; and what remains unknown. A favourable observation under one configuration does not establish general transfer, long retention, or causal effect.

#### HCD.15:4.6 - Make ownership, authority, burden, and stops executable

For every contribution and decision, name its owner and actual authorization or missing authority. Distinguish the holder's participation and consent where applicable from provider allocation, specialist judgement, employment action, release authority, clinical governance, platform access, and programme or learning-product design.

Set cost, workload, protected-time, privacy, accessibility, and service-pressure limits. Include a safe fallback when support or access disappears. Stop or return when the task is unsafe, the target-domain criterion is missing, the provider cannot operate, burden exceeds the agreed limit, the evidence cannot judge the intended contribution, or the practitioner lacks authority for the proposed change.

Avoid continuing assessment merely to maintain a record. If the current evidence is sufficient for the receiving decision and no trigger has fired, preserve the arrangement and return to later Work.

#### HCD.15:4.7 - Return the arrangement or the exact missing result

The continuing development arrangement records:

| Position | Required content |
| --- | --- |
| Use frame | Holder, later-Work family, material conditions, qualification and evidence window, receiving decision and protected consequences. |
| Affected question | Changed assumption or evidence, preserved claims and components, and serious unresolved alternatives. |
| Re-entry | Work- or evidence-bearing triggers and any materially justified cadence. |
| Contributions | Practice, feedback, community, source, provider, specialist, support, tool, AI, access, fallback and protected-time contributions actually needed. |
| Evidence | First and helped attempts, representative performance, unfamiliar transfer, delay, changed support, critical errors, burden, criteria and inference limits as applicable. |
| Governance | Owners, permissions, authority, participation or consent conditions, cost and workload limits. |
| Response | Reassess, retain, adapt, narrow, return or stop rules; exact missing result and retry condition when blocked. |

Keep the selected arrangement, its authorization, performed development Work, performed later Work, observations, capability inference, and causal-effect claim distinct. Return `continuing-arrangement-blocked` with the absent value, affected decision, safe boundary, owner, and retry condition when a required input cannot be obtained.

### HCD.15:5 - Archetypal Grounding

#### HCD.15:5.1 - A constructed continuation arrangement for Engineer-E27

Engineer-E27, `CapabilityDevelopmentTeam-E27`, `ReleaseWork-R9`, and every observation below are **constructed** for this example. The case does not establish that ReleaseWork-R9 occurred, that a provider operated, or that E27 developed. Current engineering, safety, release, employment, and specialist owners must supply their own results for operational use.

The intended later-Work family is independent review of AI-assisted mixed physical/software release claims. The work may require configuration-impact reasoning, challenge of plausible wrong AI output, auditable evidence return, justified action or refusal, and bounded safety and maintenance specialist use. The constructed 2026-Q4 window preserves the current configuration, permitted AI conditions, current sources, critical-error criteria, and specialist-return boundary.

The example stipulates one compatible HCD.14 input: familiar review practice remains supported, while weak independent counter-hypothesis generation in unfamiliar AI-error cases calls for replacing one cue-led correction exercise with action/refusal contrasts and one fresh uncued counter-hypothesis case. That is a revision hypothesis, not evidence that the change was authorized, performed, or effective.

The continuing arrangement is:

| Position | Constructed arrangement |
| --- | --- |
| Preserved | Familiar trace-to-source practice, configuration checking, evidence return, permitted tool access, and required specialist returns remain available under their existing conditions. |
| Triggers | Reopen independent challenge after a material release-Method or configuration change, a changed AI model or assistance rule, a newly observed critical error, failed unfamiliar transfer or delayed performance, changed specialist access, a source contradiction, or expiry of the bounded qualification window. A quarter boundary alone does not reopen it. |
| Practice contribution | On a trigger, use one fresh representative case containing a plausible AI claim and a material configuration variation. Obtain E27's first trace, counter-hypothesis, action or refusal, and specialist return before coaching or AI prompts name the decisive alternative. |
| Feedback and support | Provide criterion-bearing feedback, one contrasting case, current source access, the permitted AI configuration, coaching, and bounded safety or maintenance specialist access. Record help that supplies recognition separately from E27's contribution. |
| Community contribution | A provider review forum may compare anonymized reasoning traces only if facilitation, access, confidentiality, the exact practice question, and a responsible feedback owner are present. Attendance does not count as evidence of E27's capability. |
| Evidence and reassessment | Keep first and corrected attempts, critical errors, source/configuration use, unfamiliar variation, support, burden, and unresolved rivals separate. One successful fresh case can retain the local arrangement provisionally; it cannot establish broad transfer, retention, or causal effect. |
| Burden and authority | One case and feedback round must fit the stipulated protected allocation; extra follow-up needs a separately authorized time, assessor, task, and access arrangement. Release, safety, employment, and provider-operation decisions remain elsewhere. |
| Stops and returns | Stop if the engineering criterion, current configuration, specialist route, safe case, provider contribution, or authority is missing; return that exact result. Stop the added practice if burden or a protected contribution worsens. |

No result is stipulated. Conditional returns keep the arrangement honest:

| Later observation | Arrangement response |
| --- | --- |
| E27 independently generates and tests a relevant counter-hypothesis on the fresh case while preserving trace and specialist return | Retain this local response provisionally for the tested configuration; do not claim general capability or causal effect. |
| Correct challenge appears only after a coach or AI prompt names the alternative | Independent contribution remains unproved; adapt the cue relation or return to the affected target rather than count completion. |
| The AI configuration or source changes before the attempt | Requalify the task and evidence window; do not interpret the old arrangement as current. |
| Specialist access is absent for a case that requires it | Return the access or service result to its owner; do not infer a human deficit. |
| Traceability or another protected contribution deteriorates | Stop or revise the local change; the intended gain does not compensate for the protected loss. |

#### HCD.15:5.2 - A professional credit rule is not yet an arrangement

A professional body may require annual credits. That rule can supply a qualification or re-entry condition, but it does not identify the person's changed later Work, the affected development claim, representative contribution, provider capability, transfer or retention evidence, burden, or response to failure. Use the rule as one external condition and build the HCD arrangement only when those human-development values are recoverable.

### HCD.15:6 - Bias-Annotation

Recency pressure can make every new tool, source, or incident look like a reason to retrain. Ask which exact holder claim and receiving decision the change can affect, then preserve the rest. Conversely, familiarity with a credential or annual schedule can make repeated activity look safer than a claim-sized reassessment.

Community and provider visibility create another bias: participation is easy to count, while independent transfer and critical errors are harder to observe. Keep activity useful as an arrangement contribution without upgrading it to capability evidence.

### HCD.15:7 - Conformance Checklist

- One exact human holder, later-Work family, material conditions, evidence window, and receiving decision are explicit.
- The affected development question and preserved claims come from compatible evidence; no PatternID order is used as evidence.
- Re-entry uses Work- or evidence-bearing triggers; any cadence has a material justification.
- Practice, feedback, community, source, provider, specialist, support, tool, access, fallback, and protected-time contributions are named only when needed and have owners.
- First, helped, unfamiliar, delayed, changed-support, critical-error, burden, and participation observations stay distinct at the strength the receiving use needs.
- Proposal, authorization, performed development Work, later Work, observation, capability, and causal effect remain separate.
- Burden, privacy, accessibility, safety, cost, workload, authority, re-entry, stop, return, and retry conditions are executable.
- The arrangement or blocker changes a real decision without claiming permanent capability.

### HCD.15:8 - Common Anti-Patterns and How to Avoid Them

| Anti-pattern | Better move |
| --- | --- |
| “Renew twelve credits every year.” | Tie re-entry to a material qualification, Work, evidence, source, support, or risk condition and name the decision it can change. |
| “The person attended, so capability is current.” | Treat participation as one contribution and obtain evidence for the relied-on human contribution. |
| “Repeat the whole programme after every change.” | Recover the smallest affected question and preserve supported claims and arrangement components. |
| “The community will keep everyone current.” | Name facilitation, access, practice, feedback, provider, and evidence contributions; membership proves none of them by itself. |
| “The plan means continuing development is happening.” | Separate the designed and authorized arrangement from performed development Work and observed result. |
| “More frequent assessment is always safer.” | Use the lightest decision-bearing evidence and stop when added burden or surveillance no longer changes the decision. |

### HCD.15:9 - Consequences

The practitioner gains a continuing arrangement that can reopen from changed Work and evidence while preserving what still works. Resource owners can see the access, provider, specialist, tool, time, and fallback contributions they must actually supply. The holder can see which observations matter and when recurring activity can stop.

The cost is explicit maintenance of conditions and evidence. Some arrangements will return a missing provider, source, authority, safe task, or observation instead of another activity. A local positive result may remain narrow, and continued reliance may require later transfer, retention, or changed-support evidence.

### HCD.15:10 - Rationale

Continuing Human Capability Development is necessary because the capability claim and its later-Work conditions can change after an initial programme. Its proper object is therefore the holder-specific arrangement that relates decision-bearing changes to development contributions and evidence, not a calendar or an assertion of permanent capability.

The smallest affected-question rule preserves supported value and keeps burden proportionate. The explicit result distinctions prevent a designed opportunity, provider activity, participation record, or successful helped attempt from standing in for performed development, later-Work contribution, capability, or effect.

### HCD.15:11 - SoTA-Echoing

**Working question:** how should continuing workplace development remain responsive to changed Work without equating recurring instruction or participation with capability?

**Selected line — adopt and adapt.** Kraiger and Ford's [science of workplace instruction review](https://doi.org/10.1146/annurev-orgpsych-012420-060109) is the best-known-line candidate for the evidence spine used here: instructional events, learner activity, learning outcomes, transfer, and job performance remain different, while learner, design, work environment, opportunity, and support condition the result. HCD.15 adapts that line in §§4.1–4.7 by connecting one changed later-Work or evidence condition to a holder-specific continuing arrangement, explicit re-entry, owners, burden, and reassessment. The review does not supply one cadence, intervention, or E27 trajectory.

The serious default is annual-credit, repeat-course, or membership-based continuation. It is cheaper to administer, but it cannot show which development claim changed, whether the relevant contribution was elicited, or whether it survived unfamiliar, delayed, or changed-support conditions. HCD.15 rejects activity count as the capability inference while retaining a credit rule, course, or community as a bounded contribution or external condition when it genuinely changes access or qualification.

Matsuo and Tsukube's [cognitive-apprenticeship review](https://doi.org/10.1016/j.ijme.2020.100417), Wiese, Kilty, and Bennett's [realist synthesis of supervised workplace learning](https://doi.org/10.1111/medu.13655), Abedini, Abedin, and Zowghi's [review of adult online communities of practice](https://doi.org/10.1111/bjet.13120), and Brooks and colleagues' [community-support study](https://doi.org/10.1002/lrh2.10412) support bounded modeling, coaching, supervision, access, facilitation, platform, and coordination questions. They are contribution and boundary sources, not evidence for a universal provider form, transplanted clinical governance, community effect, or this holder's capability. This comparison changes §§4.4–4.6 and the participation checks in §§5 and 7.

The selected line accepts more case-specific work than counting attendance, but it is no worse on actionability and better on evidential truth, local repair, protected conditions, and safe returns. Reopen the comparison if stronger current workplace-development evidence supplies an equally truthful lower-burden arrangement, or if a changed population, Work family, provider form, support condition, measure, horizon, or serious alternative defeats one of the relied-on contribution claims.

### HCD.15:12 - Relations

HCD.14 may supply a bounded revision hypothesis and affected evidence when holder, later Work, conditions, and evidence window are compatible. HCD.15 uses that result only as a decision input; it still establishes triggers, contributions, access, provider conditions, burden, authority, reassessment, and stops. Equivalent qualified evidence permits direct entry.

HCD.1–HCD.13 may supply the holder, later-Work demand, target, programme, Method, task, support, practice, performance, transfer, retention, and evidence values they each govern. HCD.15 preserves their results and does not reconstruct an unavailable one. E.23.CDI supplies the cross-holder development spine under its own entry conditions; HCD.15 supplies the human-specific continuing arrangement.

Observed use of an HCD.15 arrangement may later supply HCD.16 with qualified human-development evidence. The designed arrangement, attendance, community membership, or provider report is not by itself evidence of Method fit, transfer, retention, or worth. HCD.16 remains independently enterable from another exact evidence change.

HCD.2 owns whole-programme comparison. OPS owns operation of current Work. OCE owns organization-holder change and organization culture. Strategy, advice, Administration, Learning Product, clinical, employment, safety, legal, provider, platform, and target-domain owners retain their direct decisions, Methods, services, access, correctness, and authority. HCD.15 consumes or returns their exact results without transferring ownership.

### HCD.15:End

<a id="hcd-16"></a>
## HCD.16 — Develop and Refresh Human Capability-Development Methods

> **Type:** Human Capability Development practitioner method pattern
> **Status:** Current practitioner preview

### HCD.16:1 - Problem Frame

**Use this when** one exact Human Capability Development Method or status-preserved candidate and one human-development claim face evidence that can change their disposition for a named repertoire use. The trigger may be a changed learner population, later Work, provider, tool or AI condition, source claim, transfer or retention result, safety boundary, or serious alternative.

Begin by naming the exact Method or candidate, its current status, the claim that can change, and the bounded use for which the repertoire decision matters. The first useful result is a current HCD repertoire slice with one of five dispositions: `adopt`, `revise`, `branch`, `retire`, or `further probe`.

What this buys in practice is local, evidence-bearing change. A practitioner can stop preserving a Method by school loyalty or replacing it by fashion, while keeping Method identity, candidate status, provider conditions, evidence reach, and target-domain authority honest.

Do not use HCD.16 for generic Method recovery, construction, admission, description, variant identity, provenance, trial, fit, worth, introduction, or Method-Engineering culture. Use Method Engineering for those results. Do not open HCD.16 merely to update a bibliography, rebuild a publication, choose one local option, or declare an already-grounded selected set.

### HCD.16:2 - Problem

Human-development Methods are often named at the wrong grain. “Coaching,” “deliberate practice,” “apprenticeship,” “retrieval,” or “AI tutoring” can hide different operations, learner conditions, supports, providers, tasks, and evidence claims. A new source, tool version, or local success is then used either to replace the whole repertoire or to preserve it unchanged.

Both reactions lose decision value. A source may contradict only one mechanism claim, one population branch, or one assisted-performance inference. A local failure may concern provider capability, access, assessment, or target-domain correctness rather than reusable Method semantics. Conversely, a reassuring average can conceal a critical error, failed transfer, weak retention, or dependence on support.

HCD.16 keeps the evidence question human-specific while returning Method identity and generic repertoire maintenance to Method Engineering. It changes only the exact HCD claim and bounded repertoire use that the evidence can defeat.

### HCD.16:3 - Forces

| Force | Working tension |
| --- | --- |
| Currentness and stability | Respond to material evidence without treating every new publication or tool version as a new Method. |
| Method identity and HCD use | Preserve generic Method semantics and status while deciding whether a human-development use remains warranted. |
| Local evidence and transfer | Use direct observations where they fit without generalizing across learners, Work families, providers, supports, or horizons. |
| Improvement and critical error | Reward useful performance while preventing aggregate gains from hiding unacceptable errors or protected losses. |
| Assistance and human contribution | Use tools, AI, coaching and scaffolding where appropriate while separating equipped performance from later unassisted or changed-support performance. |
| Decision and uncertainty | Return a bounded disposition when evidence suffices and a proportionate probe when it does not. |
| Repertoire and selection | Keep a current field-specific slice without pretending that it performs a local choice, universal retirement, or Method admission. |

### HCD.16:4 - Solution

#### HCD.16:4.1 - Fix the repertoire use and exact claim

Name the practitioner or steward, decision owner and authority, bounded repertoire use, target human capability, later-Work family, learner or practitioner population, material conditions, evidence or qualification window, and protected consequences.

Identify one exact HCD Method or candidate and one claim whose disposition can change. A useful claim names the intended action or contribution, population, task or Work, practice and feedback conditions, provider and support, measure, horizon, and inference. “The Method works” is too coarse.

If the repertoire use, subject, status, or claim cannot be recovered, return that missing value. Do not manufacture an HCD disposition from a school name, source citation, carrier, prompt, tool, or local activity label.

#### HCD.16:4.2 - Recover Method and candidate status from Method Engineering

Obtain Method admission, reusable semantics, MethodDescription, variant lineage, provenance, generic trial, fit, worth, introduction, and currentness results from their direct Method-Engineering owners. Preserve every candidate as a candidate until independent Method admission obtains.

A changed file, source, prompt, support configuration, tool, provider, or local Work deviation does not create a Method variant. When the evidence suggests changed reusable semantics, return the exact changed operation, dependency, entry or stop rule to ME.15. When it concerns description, support, introduction, or performed Work, return it to the corresponding direct result instead.

HCD.16 consumes these results to know what it is evaluating. It neither admits a Method nor retires one universally.

#### HCD.16:4.3 - Bind human-development evidence to the correct conditions

For the exact claim, recover evidence about the target action, learner starting state, representative later Work, practice and feedback, distribution and delay, unfamiliar transfer, retention, assessment, provider capability, support and AI conditions, critical errors, burden, and protected consequences at the strength the decision needs.

Keep these evidence states separate:

- assisted practice performance;
- unassisted performance after practice;
- performance under changed or withdrawn support;
- same-job performance after prior assisted exposure;
- unfamiliar same-family transfer;
- transfer to other Work;
- delayed retention under stated intervening practice; and
- provider, cohort, service, organization, or product outcomes.

Evidence for one state may motivate a probe in another; it does not fill it. A provider's capability, a deployed tool's productivity effect, or a learner's corrected answer is not the human capability claim by substitution.

#### HCD.16:4.4 - Compare the current claim with serious alternatives

State the smallest contradiction, missing evidence, or changed condition. Compare at least the current claim, the serious alternative that would change the disposition, and the consequences of retaining, narrowing, branching, or withdrawing the claim for this use.

Ask whether the apparent problem lies in:

| Candidate locus | Decision-changing question |
| --- | --- |
| Source-to-claim row | Does the source actually support this population, task, measure, horizon, transfer, support, provider, or causal inference? |
| Method-use condition | Does the already admitted Method still fit this learner, later Work, provider, support, risk, and evidence need? |
| Population or condition branch | Does evidence defeat only one subgroup, configuration, support posture, or qualification window? |
| Assessment | Did the task, scoring, cue, tool, or missing criterion fail to expose the intended contribution? |
| Provider or access | Was the Method absent, unavailable, or enacted by an unqualified provider rather than defeated? |
| Reusable Method semantics | Did an operation, dependency, entry, stop, or other reusable claim change, requiring an ME.15 return? |

Reopen only the locus that consumes the changed basis. A newer publication date, renamed school, improved interface, or changed carrier does not reopen an unaffected HCD claim.

#### HCD.16:4.5 - Design one proportionate probe when evidence cannot decide

Use `further probe` when current evidence can change the decision but cannot distinguish the live alternatives. State the smallest probe whose result would change the disposition.

Specify the person's first attempt, allowed help, representative change, source and tool conditions, provider contribution, delay or support contrast when material, critical-error condition, protected contributions, burden, stopping rule, and decision-changing observations. Keep the target-domain criteria and safety boundary with their owners.

A generic Method trial, Method admission, variant decision, or organization rollout remains with ME or another direct owner. HCD.16 designs only the human-development evidence needed for this bounded repertoire use.

#### HCD.16:4.6 - Assign one bounded disposition

Return one of the following for the exact HCD repertoire use:

| Disposition | Meaning here | Limit |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `adopt` | Include an already admitted Method for the named HCD use under the supported conditions. | Does not admit the Method, select it for one person, authorize Work, or establish effect. |
| `revise` | Change the exact HCD claim, evidence condition, population scope, support condition, or use statement while preserving Method status. | A semantic Method change returns to ME.15; a text edit alone is not the result. |
| `branch` | Keep distinct, evidence-bearing use conditions or populations whose dispositions differ. | Does not create a Method variant or inherit admission for a candidate. |
| `retire` | Stop offering the Method or claim for this bounded HCD use. | Does not erase the Method, source history, candidate account, or other supported uses. |
| `further probe` | Preserve status and uncertainty while obtaining one decision-changing observation. | Does not defer indefinitely; the probe has burden, stop, and response rules. |

Record why each serious alternative was not selected, what remains unknown, and which observation reopens the disposition. When different claims need different outcomes, create separate entries rather than averaging them into one Method score.

#### HCD.16:4.7 - Return the current repertoire slice and route stronger results

The primary result records:

| Position | Required content |
| --- | --- |
| Use and authority | Practitioner or receiver, bounded HCD repertoire use, decision owner, authority, population, later Work, qualification window, and stop. |
| Subjects and status | Exact admitted Methods and separately status-preserved candidates; current reusable semantics, descriptions and lineage supplied by ME. |
| Human-development claims | Target contribution, learner starting state, practice and feedback, representative Work, transfer, retention, provider, support, safety, tool and AI conditions. |
| Evidence and currentness | Direct observations and sources with population, task, comparator, measure, horizon, support, provider, causal, and currentness limits. |
| Alternatives and disposition | Serious alternatives; `adopt`, `revise`, `branch`, `retire`, or `further probe`; scope and protected consequences. |
| Gaps and reopening | Missing values, probe, burden, stopping rule, retirement meaning, return owner, and decision-changing triggers. |

When several exact already grounded members must be declared for one selector-facing use, pass the members and basis to G.5. When a shipped maintained set, source pack, publication, or other named currentness object needs targeted refresh, use G.11. HCD.16 does not recreate either result for an ordinary claim-sized decision.

### HCD.16:5 - Archetypal Grounding

#### HCD.16:5.1 - A constructed E27 repertoire decision

This replay is constructed. It stipulates the Method and candidate statuses needed to demonstrate the HCD decision; it supplies no real ReleaseWork-R9 occurrence, provider Work, learner observation, Method admission, or effect.

For the bounded 2026-Q4 development use, stipulate one admitted `Trace-and-Specialist-Return Method-E27` whose reusable semantics require source/configuration tracing and an explicit return to a qualified specialist when the review crosses the supplied boundary. Preserve it as admitted only inside the example. Also stipulate two status-preserved candidate accounts: `Cue-Led-Correction Candidate-E27` and `Counter-Hypothesis-First Candidate-E27`. Their names and derivation do not admit Methods or establish variants.

The current evidence question comes from unfamiliar AI-error cases. Assisted correction was accurate after a cue, but no current observation establishes that E27 independently generates and tests a counter-hypothesis before accepting plausible wrong AI output. Trace and specialist-return contributions remain supported in the stipulated earlier cases. The evidence therefore changes only the independent-challenge claim.

The repertoire slice is:

| Exact subject or claim | Constructed disposition | Boundary and next action |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `Trace-and-Specialist-Return Method-E27` for trace and bounded return | `adopt` for the named development use under the stipulated conditions. | Preserves only the already admitted Method and supported claims; supplies no universal selection or ReleaseWork-R9 result. |
| “Accurate assisted practice demonstrates independent learning” | `retire` for this use. | Assisted correction remains useful practice evidence, but it cannot support the independent-challenge claim. |
| `Cue-Led-Correction Candidate-E27` | `branch` as a candidate account for helped correction episodes only. | Keep candidate status; do not use its helped result as evidence for independent unfamiliar performance. |
| `Counter-Hypothesis-First Candidate-E27` | `further probe`. | Compare matched unfamiliar cases before any Method admission, fit, worth, or superiority claim. |

The probe uses two matched unfamiliar release-review cases with current stipulated criteria. In each, capture the first trace, configuration hypothesis, challenge, action or refusal, critical error, and specialist return before help. One case then permits the ordinary cue-led correction; the other permits the candidate counter-hypothesis prompt only after the first attempt. A later fresh case under normal permitted support and, only if the decision needs it, a separately resourced delayed or perturbed-support observation can distinguish local independent contribution from helped correction. Stop on missing criteria, unsafe use, absent specialist access, unacceptable burden, or damage to traceability.

Possible results remain bounded:

| Observation | Repertoire response |
| --- | --- |
| Independent challenge appears on fresh unfamiliar cases while trace and specialist return remain protected | Reconsider `adopt` or `branch` for an independently admitted Method at the supported use; return admission, variant, fit and worth questions to ME. |
| Correct challenge appears only after the counter-hypothesis prompt | Preserve helped-practice value but retain `further probe` or narrow the claim; independent contribution remains unproved. |
| Traceability or specialist return worsens | Reject or revise the candidate use regardless of aggregate accuracy. |
| The task, source, configuration, or critical-error criterion is defective | Repair or return that evidence input; do not retire the human-development Method from an invalid observation. |

#### HCD.16:5.2 - Unlike evidence states do not select an engineering Method

AI-supported customer service can show deployed equipped productivity and a bounded same-job outage signal. Consulting tasks can show that assistance helps inside one task frontier and hurts outside it. School mathematics can show a difference between assisted practice and later unassisted performance. These are decision-relevant evidence shapes, but their populations, Work, tools, measures, horizons, and providers do not establish an adult release-engineering Method. HCD.16 uses them to demand the right comparison, then requires direct E27-compatible evidence.

#### HCD.16:5.3 - A source contradiction can reopen one claim without opening treatment

An emerging human counterconditioning review can reopen one source-to-claim row about an extinction-learning mechanism. A publication-bias critique can reopen a pooled claim about choice-architecture effects. HCD.16 may return `revise`, `retire`, or `further probe` for an exact HCD use, but it prescribes neither clinical treatment nor public-policy nudging. The specialist domain retains the intervention and outcome decision.

### HCD.16:6 - Bias-Annotation

Novelty and prestige can make a new source, tool, model, or school seem repertoire-changing before the affected claim is named. Familiarity creates the opposite bias: a long-used Method is protected from contrary transfer, retention, provider, or critical-error evidence.

Status laundering is especially dangerous. A well-documented candidate, successful local deviation, or newly versioned description can look like an admitted Method. Recover status and reusable semantics from ME before comparing HCD evidence, and keep a favourable HCD disposition bounded to its use.

### HCD.16:7 - Conformance Checklist

- The bounded repertoire use, decision owner and authority, population, later Work, qualification window, and protected consequences are explicit.
- One exact admitted Method or status-preserved candidate and one decision-changing HCD claim are named.
- Admission, reusable semantics, description, variant lineage, provenance, generic trial, fit, worth, introduction, and currentness come from their direct ME results.
- Assisted, unassisted, changed-support, same-job, unfamiliar-transfer, other-Work, delayed, provider, and organization evidence states remain distinct where material.
- Source population, task, comparator, measure, horizon, support, provider, causal, and currentness limits travel with the claim.
- Serious alternatives and the smallest contradiction or missing evidence are compared without a global Method score.
- The disposition is exactly `adopt`, `revise`, `branch`, `retire`, or `further probe`, with scope, meaning, burden, stop, and reopen condition.
- Candidate status is preserved; adoption and retirement do not change universal Method status.
- G.5, G.11, ME, clinical, target-domain, safety, provider, and authority questions are returned only when their own entry conditions obtain.

### HCD.16:8 - Common Anti-Patterns and How to Avoid Them

| Anti-pattern | Better move |
| --- | --- |
| “A newer paper replaces the Method.” | Name the exact source-to-claim row and reopen only the use that consumes the changed claim. |
